treatment, and same-color fans/buttons/plates share it (intentional emergent behavior, not
special-cased). The key renders tinted via `tintedKeyArt` (three `recoloredArt` swaps of KEY_ART's
yellows for shades of the channel color); the picker shows, without a "none" option and without the
door two-per-color cap, whenever a Key is the selected Object, and the key's own palette swatch wears
the chosen color. HUD: a small colored key chip per unlocked color, so a gate opening off-screen under a
Follow/Runner camera still gives feedback. Keys re-lock on Play restart and Build Reset. Not built
(per the note): the carried-key-spent-on-one-lock variant.

**Star = ghost mode, `STAR_DURATION_MS` (6s).** Pickup sets/refills `starTimer` (never stacks);
`tick()` counts it down in Build too and plays a small expiry blip. While up: `takePlayerHit` returns
immediately, `triggerHazard`/the chaser branch bail out, `triggerLethalHazard` returns WITHOUT setting
`hazardCooldown` (so a lethal tile the player is standing in kills the frame after the star ends —
verified), chasers/fleers fall into `steeringAccelFor`'s wander branch (`loseTrack`), walls stay solid
(it is not a noclip). Cue: the player renders translucent and flickering the whole time and flickers
visibly faster for the last `STAR_WARNING_MS` (1.5s) — no hidden state. Art is the existing yellow
`STAR_ART`; the sound is original chip synthesis.

**Sounds** (three new `CHIP_DESIGNS` entries, literal arrow functions in `sounds`): `heartUp` (rising
E-G#-B arpeggio), `keyGet` (bright two-note blip), `starPower` (quick shimmering arpeggio), plus
`starEnd` = `playChip("starPower", 0.5, 0.6)` for the expiry blip. Rendered and checked for length/peak
(0.27s/0.20s/0.40s, peak 0.7), but **hand-written per-frame data, never listened to** — if they don't
sound right, retune them in the sound designer. Spending a heart reuses the existing yelp/thunk.

**Captions**: `CAPTION_TEXT` gained `colHeart`/`colKey`/`colStar` ("Takes a hit for you.", "Opens gates
of its color.", "Nothing can hurt you. For a bit."), and `renderCollectibleSwatches` now calls
`wireCaption` — every other collectible id has no entry and stays uncaptioned, as before.

**Build mode details**: because collected pickups are *removed* from `actors`, a Build-time pickup is
recorded in `collectedPowerups` and `restoreCollectedPowerups()` (Build "Reset to start", and also
entering Play and exporting, so a heart grabbed while tinkering isn't baked out of Play/the shipped
game) puts them back, re-locks keys and ends the star. Ordinary collectibles still do NOT come back on
Build Reset — a pre-existing behavior, left alone. Power-ups award no score.

**Spawner**: a spawner loaded with a Heart/Key/Star just spawns a pickup like any collectible (verified
for Key). `spawnerContents` holds only a defId, so a spawned Key has no `channel` and defaults to red.

**Export / saves**: `gameHasGoalTile`, `starActive`, `takePlayerHit`, `collectPowerup`, `shadeHex`,
`tintedKeyArt` added to `runtimeFunctions`; one `constantsSource` line ships `POWERUP_DEF_IDS`,
`MAX_SPARE_HEARTS`, `HIT_INVULN_MS`, `STAR_DURATION_MS`, `STAR_WARNING_MS`, `KEY_TINT_FROM`; the export
template declares `spareHearts`/`starTimer`/`keyChannelsUnlocked`/`collectedPowerups`/`lastHeartCount`
by hand, resets them in `restartStandaloneGame`, and has the new `#heartsRow`/`#keysRow` HUD markup and
CSS. `channel` survives snapshot -> JSON -> restore (actors are cloned wholesale; confirmed). No new seed
ids, so no "append missing built-in seed" backfill is needed — confirmed the three ids are already in
`COLLECTIBLE_SEEDS` and `restoreProjectSnapshot`'s existing append line covers them. (There is no
`object-interaction-matrix.md` in the repo — the earlier matrix lives in its CLAUDE.md section — so the
matrix rows below are recorded here.)

**Interaction matrix additions.** heart × hazard: spare absorbed, knockback+flicker, no life lost;
heart × chaser: spare absorbed, same punt but no reset, flicker prevents re-catch; heart × crush:
same as hazard (crush calls `triggerHazard`); heart × lethal: ignored — still instant game over.
key × gate: opens same-color `barrier`/channel-gated `solid`, permanent for the session; key × fan:
same-color fan turns on (shared `isChannelOn`); key × button/plate: share the channel (button toggle
and plate momentary still work alongside it). star × chaser/fleer: lose track and wander; star ×
hazard/crush/chaser contact: nothing; star × lethal: harmless until the star ends, then deadly at once;
star × walls/gates: still solid. Not covered either way: star/hearts × gravity-well capture, runner
camera fail, NPC-lethal (unchanged).

Verified with Playwright (36 behavior checks, 17 export checks, plus the 8-sample sweep): goal +
collectibles does not win by collection but the goal wins; no-goal collection wins; a lone Heart/Key/
Star in a collectible-free room never wins; heart absorbs a hazard, a chaser and a falling crate with no
life lost and no reset, flicker prevents re-catch, no spare loses a life and resets as before; 4 hearts
cap at 3 spare with the 4th left on the floor; lava with full hearts still ends the game; red key opens
red not blue, stays open, re-locks with the key restored on Play restart and Build Reset; a channel-less
(old-save) key defaults to red; star starts at full, chaser stops steering at the player, hazard/lava/
chaser harmless, expiry inside lava kills; two stars don't stack; a real export with all three plays
standalone (2 hearts shown, a red key chip, star running, zero errors). The debug hook was stripped.

## Locked Door — a Fixture that only a Key opens

Direct request: a new Fixture, "Locked Door, only openable with the key," with user-supplied art
(`LOCKED_DOOR_ART`, used verbatim). Geometry id `lockeddoor` (`LOCKED_DOOR_GEOMETRY`), palette label
"locked door", caption "Only a key of its color opens it." Single orientation, color-taggable.

**Reads only Keys, never channels.** It blocks (`isBlockingGeometry`) until opened per cell. `isCellBlocking`
and `resolveAxis`'s own blocking check both consult `lockedDoorOpenAt(cx, cy)` — deliberately NOT
`isChannelOn`, so a same-color Button/Plate/Gate-style channel can never open it (verified: red switch on,
door still blocks). Opening is by touch, not by pickup: `checkLockedDoorUnlock(player)`, called from
`stepActor` next to `checkPlayerTileContacts`, opens any closed Locked Door within 3px of the player whose
color has a held Key (`keyChannelsUnlocked`) — so a Key picked up across the level doesn't open it remotely
the way it does a Gate. The door stays open for the session and the Key is not consumed (one red Key opens
every red Locked Door). An untagged door counts as red, the same default a Key has.

**State**: transient `lockedDoorsOpen` (`roomCellKey` -> true), reset exactly where `keyChannelsUnlocked`
is (`clearTransientRuntimeState`, `restoreCollectedPowerups`, export `restartStandaloneGame`), so Play
restart and Build "Reset to start" re-lock it. Never saved — the board stays unmodified (nothing is erased).
**Look**: closed shows the art; open ghosts to 25% with a channel-colored outline (the Gate/`solid` open
treatment, via `gateOpen`); the corner color badge shows which key fits. **Palette**: reuses the Key's
picker mode (`renderColorSwatches` `keyMode`) so it shows the 5 colors with no "none" option.

**Deviation to know about**: it sits in the Fixtures row as asked, but Fixtures were documented as
walk-through, never-solid pads (`FIXTURE_GEOMETRIES`). Nothing branches on that list beyond palette
rendering, so this works, but "Fixture" no longer strictly means non-blocking. **Art**: the first paste was a nearly blank placeholder; the real steel-plate-with-keyhole art replaced it.

**Export**: `lockedDoorOpenAt`, `lockedDoorColorAt`, `checkLockedDoorUnlock` added to `runtimeFunctions`;
`LOCKED_DOOR_GEOMETRY` constant line; `lockedDoorsOpen` declared/reset in the export template.
No saved-data change: new geometry id, nothing to migrate or backfill.

Verified with Playwright (26 checks): blocks with no key; a lit red channel doesn't open it; red key opens it
on touch (not on pickup) and the player walks through; key not consumed; blue key doesn't open a red door;
untagged door opens with red; Play restart and Build Reset re-lock; real palette UI (swatch, caption, 5-color
picker, painting places `lockeddoor` with a color); exported game blocked without a key and wins with one,
zero errors; 8-sample sweep + export clean; no debug hook in the repo file.

## Riding is gravity-only: pushing a crate south in a top-down room stuttered at 1.5x speed

Direct report: "glitchy behavior when pushing crates north to south on the screen," plus similar
strangeness walking north/south on slippery tiles. Traced per-frame rather than guessed. Free-roam
walking (plain and ice, N/S/diagonal) is clean — a constant 3.7px/frame at the 220px/s cap — so the
physics itself was fine; the crate push was not: pushing a crate SOUTH alternated 7.3px/3.7px per
frame for both player and crate (~330px/s, 1.5x the cap), while pushing NORTH was a clean 3.7.

**Root cause: the moving-platform riding step (see "Moving platforms (riding...)") has no notion of
gravity.** It treats any actor whose bottom edge is within `RIDE_CONTACT_EPSILON` of a standable
object's top edge (with x overlap) as a rider and re-applies the platform's frame delta to it. In a
top-down (gravity-off) room, a player pushing a crate south sits flush against the crate's NORTH face —
geometrically identical to "standing on top" — so each frame the crate's own shove delta was added to the
player a second time, which also made the next frame's overlap (and shove) larger: the 2x/1x sawtooth.
North pushes were immune only because the player is then below the crate. **Fix**: the riding step only
runs when `gravityOn()` — "on top of" is meaningless without gravity. Verified riding still works under
gravity (rider on a crate drifting on a conveyor is carried the full 240px with it).

**Not reproduced: the slippery N/S "sticky" feel.** Walking N/S/diagonally across an all-ice room matches
plain ground frame for frame (identical vy and dy), including release, so ice physics isn't it. If it
happened with a crate or Moving Platform nearby it was probably this same bug (ice makes crates keep
sliding ahead of a following player, which is exactly the flush-behind setup); if it still happens
without either, it needs a repro (gravity on/off, which tiles, any objects nearby).

Verified with Playwright: free-roam crate push S/N never steps more than 3.7px/frame; gravity riding
intact; Locked Door tests, 8-sample sweep and export unchanged. Test hook kept out of the repo file.

## NPC–collectible contact is silent

`triggerImpact` also skips the landing thud for collectibles (walls). `resolveActorContact`'s no-player branch still reflects two touching non-player actors apart, but passes `silent` to `reflectActorsApart` when either is a collectible, so a wanderer/chaser brushing a collectible no longer plays `boing`. Player pickup sounds are untouched.

## Dropped objects land in front of the player

`dropCarriedObject` places the object one body-width ahead of `player.facingDir` (same y as before), not on top of the player. If that spot is blocked by tiles (`actorBlockedByTiles`) it falls back to the old on-player spot so nothing is buried in a wall. Throws are unchanged.

## Button is now "Switch": a sliding lever, and a clickable starting state

The palette name for `pushbutton` is now "switch" (`GEOMETRY_LABELS`; internal id unchanged). Rendering no longer mirrors the whole tile: `SWITCH_OFF_ART`/`SWITCH_ON_ART` draw the same housing with the lever (red knob + two dark stem squares) one square left (off) or right (on); `drawGameplayTile` just picks one (`SWITCH_ON_ART` has its own `constantsSource` line). In Build mode, clicking an already-placed Switch of the same color with the Switch swatch selected flips its starting state (`paintGeometryModifierAt`, once per stroke via `cannonRotateStrokeCells`): colored flips `switchChannels[color]`, uncolored flips the room's gravity. Play starts from that state because the Play snapshot already captures `switchChannels` and per-room gravity. Default is off.

## The Monster's Den is now a user-authored project snapshot

The built-in sample was replaced by the user's own hand-built version (`The_Monsters_Den.json`). `MONSTERS_DEN_PROJECT` embeds that project (rooms, actorDefs, ground/background defs, switchChannels, etc.) and `buildMonstersDenGame()` just calls `restoreProjectSnapshot(structuredClone(MONSTERS_DEN_PROJECT))`, so it gets every backward-compat migration for free. Actors were stripped to placement fields (position reset to spawn, zero velocity) and stale `spawnerContents` keys for cells that no longer hold a spawner were dropped. The old maze string (`MONSTERS_DEN_MAZE`) and placement code are gone, so earlier sections describing that layout (single-row den, red Barrier/Button, orange Barrier/Plate trap) are history, not the current level. To swap it again, re-export from the editor and regenerate the constant the same way.

## Skinned tiles show their Ground Effect; spawners pause off-screen

Two fixes from a user's Spin Cycle playthrough (project + screen recording). (1) The walk speed on "slippery" tiles was fine — measured a full 220px/s — but the same suds skin was painted over both slippery ice and the sample's sticky mud, and skinned tiles hide their Ground Effect entirely (the Build-only ring was always suppressed under a skin, the exact gap the v2.24 note flagged), so a kid walked onto an identical-looking sticky patch and slowed to ~50-70px/s. `drawGameplayTile` now washes a skinned tile in its effect's `MODIFIER_SWATCH_COLORS` color whenever the tile's modifier differs from its skin's own default (`skinDef.modifier`) — both the walkable (`none`) path and the solid path, in Build, Play and export (it's gameplay information). Lethal skins are unaffected (their default already is lethal), and a skin on a plain `none` modifier stays untinted. (2) Measured spawn rate was the designed one spawn per 2.5s per spawner; what overwhelmed the board was accumulation, including from spawners far off-screen in a Follow/Runner room (up to 3 screens wide) that kept producing characters nobody saw. `tickSpawners` now skips (clock paused, no wind-up) any spawner more than `SPAWNER_VIEW_MARGIN_PX` (64) outside the current view (`cameraX` in Play, `editorPanCol*TILE` in Build); fixed rooms are always fully in view. There is still no cap or TTL on a spawner's live population (v2.38) — a spawner left running on-screen still accumulates, which is the next lever if it's still too much.

## Spawners have a live cap again (SPAWNER_MAX_LIVE = 8)

Follow-up to the off-screen pause: a user's saved project (two spawners, 24 spawned wanderers/chasers alive, board overwhelmed, reported as "firing 4-5 times a second" in Build and Play without panning) did NOT reproduce a fast rate — loaded and run in Build and Play, each spawner fires once per `SPAWN_INTERVAL_MS`, and the save's actor ids alternate strictly between the two spawners as a 2.5s period would produce; the recording itself couldn't be decoded here. Rather than guess further, `tickSpawners` gained a safety valve: a spawner skips firing while `SPAWNER_MAX_LIVE` (8) of its own spawns (`spawnerKey` match, not `ttlPopped`) are alive and resumes the instant one is gone (the clock keeps counting, so it fires right away). Nothing already on the board is removed, and a saved project that already holds more than the cap simply stops growing. This partly reverses v2.38's "no cap" — retune or remove the constant if "lots of the item" is wanted back. If a genuinely faster-than-interval rate shows up again, the cause is not in `tickSpawners`' timing (spawnActorAt has one caller).

## The "spawner firing 4-5 times a second" was characters being sent back to the spawner

Root cause found by watching the user's recording (claude-real-video + a static ffmpeg got frames out of it): the spawner was firing at its normal 2.5s period, but its chasers kept running straight (no pathfinding) into lethal tiles and `triggerNpcLethalReset` teleported each one back to its `spawnX/spawnY` — for a spawner-made actor that is the spawner's launch cell — so a stream of chasers kept popping out of the spawner while the log repeated "Caught in lethal — sent back to its spawn point!". `triggerNpcLethalReset` and `triggerGravityWellCapture` now destroy a spawner-made character (`actor.spawnerKey`: `destroyed` flag, splat + `popVanish`, delayed `removeActorById`) instead of resetting it; hand-placed characters still go home. `checkNpcLethalContacts`/`triggerNpcLethalReset` also bail on `actor.destroyed` so the fade window can't re-trigger. The SPAWNER_MAX_LIVE cap from the previous patch stays as a separate safety valve. Tooling note: the recording only decoded with a real ffmpeg (pip's `imageio-ffmpeg` static build works when apt is broken); Playwright's bundled ffmpeg is VP8-only.

## Enemy-vs-enemy bumps are silent

`resolveActorContact`'s no-player branch still reflects two touching non-player actors apart, but now passes `silent` to `reflectActorsApart` when either one is a collectible, stationary, hazard ball, or steering character (wanderer/chaser/fleer), so crowds of enemies no longer produce a constant boing. Physics is unchanged; only the sound is dropped. Object-vs-object bumps (crates, bombs, magnets...) still sound. Verified only via the 15-check regression sweep, not a dedicated audio-hook test.

## Enemies can't set off bombs

The collision-triggered bomb detonation in `resolveAxis` (fast impact >= `BOMB_IMPACT_DETONATE_SPEED`) now applies to physics objects only; steering characters (wanderer/chaser/fleer) bumping a bomb tile just stop at its edge. Bomb blasts still knock enemies back, and a player touch still arms the fuse. This narrows the earlier interaction-matrix patch, which had included NPCs.

## The Ice Cave replaces Spin Cycle

The hand-coded "Spin Cycle" sample (and its sample-scoped art, `buildSpinCycleGame`, `drawSpinCycleIcon`) was removed and replaced by the user's own project (`my-project_6.json`), shipped the same way as The Monster's Den: `ICE_CAVE_PROJECT` embeds the snapshot and `buildIceCaveGame()` calls `restoreProjectSnapshot(structuredClone(...))`. Rooms are named "The Ice Cave" and "The Frightened Yeti". Actors were reset to spawn with zero velocity, spawner-made actors were dropped, and stale `spawnerContents` keys (cells no longer holding a spawner) were removed. Earlier sections describing Spin Cycle's layout and traces are history, not the current level. Verified via the 15-check sweep (loads, plays 3s, export clean); the level itself was not re-traced or playtested beyond that.

## Characters from imported projects never moved: `displacedCooldown` was undefined

In The Monster's Den the monster was a correctly-defined chaser that never moved. Actors in a project JSON authored before `displacedCooldown` existed don't carry that field, so `stepActor`'s steering gate `actor.displacedCooldown <= 0` evaluated `undefined <= 0` -> false and steering never ran, forever (the Ice Cave sample's characters were exposed to the same thing). The gate is now `!(actor.displacedCooldown > 0)`, which treats a missing value as "not displaced". Verified by tracing the Den's monster: it now charges toward the player and stops against the closed red gate. Lesson: any new per-actor numeric field must be read with a missing-value-safe comparison, since embedded/imported projects skip `createActor`'s defaults.

## The World's Worst Elevator is now a user-authored project; bounce sound is quieter and shorter

`WORST_ELEVATOR_PROJECT` embeds the user's own hand-built version (rooms "The Lobby" and "The Penthouse (Probably)"), loaded via `restoreProjectSnapshot` like The Monster's Den and The Ice Cave; actors reset to spawn, spawner state stripped. The old coded builder and `fillWorstElevatorRect` are gone, so the earlier Worst Elevator layout/trace sections are history. Separately, the `boing` chip design (plain bouncy tiles) was cut from 28 frames to 10 with peak volume 14 -> 8; `trampolineBoing` is unchanged. Hand-edited data, not listened to; retune in the sound designer if needed. Verified by the 15-check sweep/export only; the level itself wasn't re-traced.

## Sushi Train Panic — a conveyor-belt sample (two fixed rooms, no engine changes)

`buildSushiTrainGame()`/`drawSushiTrainIcon`, registered after Gravity Switcheroo. Room 1 "The Kitchen Floor": one short east belt under the cat's feet, a door. Room 2 "The Sushi Train": bottom belt L (row 13, west, toward a fire pit), counter K, top belt U (row 11, east, 1-tile tunnel above L), stop wall (19,8-10) with a plate at (18,10), a south-facing spawner at (19,11) loaded with a crate, and a red gate (col 5) in a corridor above (floor row 5) with the goal at (2,4). The cat carries a crate west through the tunnel, hops up onto U beyond U's west end, drops it; U carries it onto the plate; wall top -> corridor floor -> gate. Measured facts it relies on: a belt (`solid:wind-*`) carries a standing player at 220px/s (the walking cap — walking against it is still 220), a crate or NPC at 160, and a hazard ball not at all (it never grounds). A freshly spawned crate gets a ~430px/s burst for 350ms (bouncyCooldown skips the cap while the belt keeps accelerating it), so a grab at the spawn point has a ~140ms window; riding L behind it gives ~1.7s in range. Jump rise is ~127px, so anything the cat shouldn't climb is >=160px up. Spawners can't sit directly above a belt tile (launch blocked), and a spawner facing against a belt jams on its own crate (cellOccupiedByActor). **SPAWNER_MAX_LIVE (8) exists** — an earlier brief assumed "no cap".

**A plate on the failing belt was tried and removed**: a second same-color plate in the tunnel flickered the gate open every time a spawned crate passed, and a cat holding LEFT at the gate walked through (traced: win). The goal is now gated by P2 alone. Traced: static 4-connected flood fill with the gate closed can't reach the goal; cat at the gate under hold/release/early-release/jump-mash never passes; standing on the plate opens it only while standing there; 24 random-input fuzz sessions, 0 wins. Engine rule left alone: a crate touching the goal also wins (`checkPhysicsObjectGoalContacts`), but here that needs the gate open. Known alternate route: stacking 2 crates on K lets the cat jump to the wall top without the L ride (the gate still needs a crate on the plate). Lethal is an instant game over in Play, so "the incinerator" is a splat + retry; room 1 was kept short (door at col 11) to make retries cheap. The gallery has no per-sample description field, so none was added. Verified: the same key-driven bot wins start to finish in live Play and in an exported file; 16-check sweep passes.

## Characters no longer thud, boing or bounce-sound while walking around

Complaint: enemies in a crowded project (`my-project_6.json`) made a sound constantly while walking over tiles and bumping each other. Instrumenting `sounds.*`/`playChip` showed the remaining sources were landing thuds (`triggerImpact`), bounce/trampoline boings gated on `isSteeringNPC`, and the walk-over `triggerBouncyLaunch` boing. Now silent for non-player steering characters, stationary NPCs and hazard balls: the `triggerImpact` thud, the four `bounceSound()` sites in `resolveAxis`, the `bounceOffTrampolineObjects` trampolineBoing, and `triggerBouncyLaunch`'s boing (player only). Physics is unchanged, and the player and physics objects (crates, bombs...) keep their sounds. Contact reactions with the player (oof/gotcha/pop) are untouched. Verified at the sound layer in the user's project (enemy-driven sounds gone from Play; build mode 6 -> 2 thuds) plus the 16-check sweep/export. Not listened to.

## Dying with lives left resets the whole level

In Play (and exported games), a life-costing hit that leaves lives (hazard, chaser catch, falling-crate crush) now restarts the level to its starting conditions after `LEVEL_RESET_DELAY_MS` (650ms, so the hit animation plays): `consumeLifeIfPlaying` schedules `restartLevelKeepingLives()`, which runs the normal Play restart (`restartPlaySessionFromSnapshot`, or `restartStandaloneGame` in an export) and restores the already-reduced life count. Everything else returns to start: actors/spawner population, collected items, doors, switches, keys, hearts, star, camera, and the READY! pause plays again. The old chaser-catch teleport-to-start is now Build-mode only. Absorbed-by-heart hits and lethal/game-over are unchanged. Verified in Cheese Heist: lives 3 -> 2, moved actors/player back at spawn, READY shown; last life still ends the game; 16-check sweep/export clean. The export's reset path was not separately played through.

## The Gravity Switcheroo is now a user-authored project

`GRAVITY_SWITCHEROO_PROJECT` embeds the user's own hand-built version (rooms "Through the Door" and "The Gravity Switcheroo"), loaded via `restoreProjectSnapshot` like The Monster's Den, The Ice Cave and The World's Worst Elevator. Actors were reset to spawn with zero velocity, the 8 spawner-made actors in the upload were dropped, and the spawner at (22,12) in room 1 keeps its loaded content. The old coded builder and its `GRAV_*` art/`fillGravRect` helpers are gone, so the earlier Gravity Switcheroo layout/trace section is history; `drawGravitySwitcherooIcon` and the `GRAVITY_OFF_POP_SPEED` mechanic are unchanged. Verified by the 16-check sweep/export only; the level itself wasn't re-traced.

## Conveyors keep their Ground texture (stone + wind drew as plain grey)

Reported on Sushi Train Panic: hand-drawing a stone-textured conveyor produced a generic grey solid tile. Root cause: `paintGeometryModifierAt`'s wind branch (the one that paints a fresh `wind-*` tile at `WIND_ORDER[0]`) hard-nulled `groundSkinBoard` ("wind is a flat Surface swatch, never a skin"), contradicting the v2.24 rule that Ground and Ground Effects are independent axes. The sample looked right only because `buildSushiTrainGame` sets skins directly. Fix: the branch stamps `selectedGroundSkinId` (and skips the channel-color tag when a skin is selected, like the general path); painting over an existing conveyor of the same geometry still leaves its direction alone but now re-skins it. `setWindDirectionAt` never touched the skin, so the arrow picker already preserved it (verified). Verified with a hooked copy through the real palette (stone + wind -> `solid:wind-n` + `gndStone`; picker to south keeps the skin) plus the 16-check sweep/export; no saved-data change.

## Lethal tiles cost a life instead of ending the game

Reported: with 3 lives, dying to a lethal tile was an instant game over. That was the documented v2.18 design ("instant KO"), but it contradicts the lives HUD, so `triggerLethalHazard` now goes through `consumeLifeIfPlaying(true)`: one life lost, level restart after the splat if lives remain, game over (with the stinger after the splat animation) only on the last life. Hearts still do NOT absorb lethal, and star ghost mode still defers it. `triggerLethalHazard` is also what Runner's `checkRunnerFailState` calls, so being caught by the Runner camera now costs a life too. **Not changed**: black-hole capture (`triggerPlayerGravityWellCapture`) is still an instant game over; it is the same shape and probably wants the same treatment if the user agrees. Earlier sections saying lethal is "instant game over" are history. Verified with a hooked copy in Cheese Heist (lives 3->2->1->0, game over only at 0, level restarted in between) plus the 16-check sweep; the export reuses the same shared functions, not separately played.

## Sushi Train Panic is now a user-authored project

`SUSHI_TRAIN_PROJECT` embeds the user's own hand-built version (rooms "The Kitchen Floor" and "The Sushi Train"), loaded via `restoreProjectSnapshot` like The Monster's Den, The Ice Cave, The World's Worst Elevator and The Gravity Switcheroo. Actors were reset to spawn with zero velocity, the 3 spawner-made crates in the upload were dropped, and the spawner at (19,11) keeps its loaded crate. The old coded builder is gone, so the earlier "Sushi Train Panic" layout/trace section above is history; `drawSushiTrainIcon` is unchanged. The level itself was not re-traced; the earlier gating guarantees (gate can't be passed without the plate) apply to the old layout only. Verified by the 16-check sweep/export.

## Bombs fall like crates; a fall of more than 6 tiles detonates one

Request: make bombs gravity-affected actors like crates, and let a long fall set them off so a level can choose between "bombs rest" and "bombs go off". A bomb is a painted tile, which can't fall, so in any room with gravity on `convertBombTilesToActors()` (called from `tick()`) turns each bomb tile into a real `CARRY_BOMB2_KIND` actor via the same conversion a grab uses (`convertGrabbedTileToActor(..., silent)`), unarmed; rooms without gravity keep the plain tile. The bomb kind joined `STANDABLE_OBJECT_KINDS` (pushed right after `CARRY_BOMB2_KIND` is declared, because of declaration order), so it is shoved, stood on and stacked like a crate; the player touching an unlit bomb (`shovePushables`) lights its fuse, matching what bumping the tile always did. **Fall rule**: `stepActor` tracks `fallStartY` (highest point of the current fall; rising or resting resets it) and on landing (`vyBefore > 0` and now grounded or bounced) calls `detonateCarriedBombAt` if the drop exceeded `BOMB_FALL_DETONATE_TILES` (6) tiles. Armed or not, thrown or dropped; short drops just rest. Behavior changes to know about: a gravity-room bomb is no longer a wall (the player pushes it, and it arms), and tile-only paths (bump-arm by the player, fast-impact detonation) don't apply to it; the actor equivalents are the shove-arm above and chain reactions via `detonateCarriedBombAt`. Existing samples/saves with bombs in gravity rooms convert on the first tick. Export: `convertBombTilesToActors` in `runtimeFunctions`, a `BOMB_FALL_DETONATE_TILES` constant line. Verified with a hooked copy: tile converts and falls; 5.5-tile drop rests, 6.5-tile drop detonates; pushing arms the fuse; no-gravity room keeps the tile; 16-check sweep. Not separately played in an export.

## Hazard balls stop boinging on plain ground; bombs can be loaded into spawners

Reported on `my-project_7.json` (a Catapult Factory variant with a default-content spawner): endless bouncing sounds. Traced by counting `claimVoice` calls with stacks: a spawner's default hazard ball bounces off every surface by design, and `resolveAxis` played `boing` for it because hazard ball is in `PHYSICS_OBJECT_KINDS` (so `isPhysicsObject`). `boingHeard` now gates all four bounce-sound sites: player, non-hazard physics objects, or a hazard ball hitting a tile that is itself bouncy/trampoline. Physics unchanged. Characters loaded into spawners were already silent on ground (landing thud, bump, boing); the remaining sounds are the spawn chime and contact with the player. Not fixed: a Large Stone in that save sits at a saved y of about -600000 and keeps drifting (corrupt save data).

Bombs in spawners: a bomb is a painted tile, so with the bomb swatch selected, clicking a spawner tile calls `toggleSpawnerContent(gx, gy, CARRY_BOMB2_DEF_ID)` (same replace/clear-on-repeat rules; mouse and touch). `spawnActorAt` gives a spawned bomb actor the fuse fields a grab would. It then behaves as any bomb actor (falls in gravity rooms, detonates after a drop over 6 tiles, lit when the player pushes it). Spawned bombs count against `SPAWNER_MAX_LIVE`. Verified in a hooked copy; 16-check sweep/export pass.

## Ground Effect: indestructible

A new modifier, `indestructible` (palette swatch after trap, caption "Bombs can't touch it.", slate ring/tint via `MODIFIER_SWATCH_COLORS`). `detonateBombAt` skips a neighbor cell carrying it, so a blast leaves it standing (a bomb's own cell still clears, and chain reactions into neighboring bombs are unchanged). Like bouncy, picking it with plain `none` ground selected defaults the geometry to solid. Nothing else destroys tiles except the player-triggered `trap`, so no other path needed a check. Old saves need no migration (new modifier id). Verified with a hooked blast test and the 16-check sweep/export; the bomb actor path uses the same `detonateBombAt`.

## Four more channel colors (pink, yellow, cyan, brown)

`CHANNEL_COLORS` grew from 5 to 9 names and `CHANNEL_COLOR_HEX` gained matching hexes (pink `#ec407a`, yellow `#fdd835`, cyan `#00bcd4`, brown `#795548`). Nothing else changed: channels are keyed by name everywhere (color layer, switch/plate/key state, door pairing, key tint, HUD chips, export constants), so the picker, gates, buttons, plates, keys, locked doors and doors all pick them up. Old saves are unaffected. White/grey were skipped (too close to the ghost/steel looks). Not built: user-defined colors (a "+" swatch with a saved `customChannelColors` list) — estimated 1-2 hours if 9 turns out to be too few. Doors still pair two per color, so this also raises the door-pair limit to 9. Verified: the picker wraps to two rows of five, and the 16-check sweep/export passes.

## Gates no longer flicker when a room loads

Reported: on room load, gates flicker open and shut, a problem when a gate is holding something. Cause: gate drawing reads `isChannelOn` plus `channelStateSince` (the slide-animation clock), both only refreshed inside `tick()`, which is frozen during the READY! pause and the door-transition window. So a freshly loaded room drew from empty state (every gate shut, even with its channel on via a switch or a crate resting on a plate) or from the room just left, and then the first tick flipped it and started a slide. Confirmed on the old build: right after Play, `plateChannelsActive` was empty for the whole READY window, then went true and started the slide. Fix: `syncChannelsToHotRoom()` recomputes plate state for the hot room and marks every channel's state as long-settled (`since: -1e9`, so no slide). It runs at Play start (after `clearTransientRuntimeState`, which wipes the clock), after a door arrival in `beginRoomTransition`, in the exported game's boot and `restartStandaloneGame`, and is in `runtimeFunctions`. Ordinary mid-room channel flips still slide as before. Verified in a hooked copy (crate on a red plate: on at the first frame, no slide) plus the 16-check sweep/export.

## Objects no longer bounce forever on bouncy floors and trampolines; fall origin resets on teleport

Reported (with the exported `index_49.html`, which is identical to the editor build): "something is spawning and constantly interacting with the ground tiles causing an endless bounce sound." Reproduced in a synthetic room, not in `my-project_7.json` as saved: a crate or hazard ball resting on a `solid:bouncy` floor attempted 668 boings in 15s. Cause: `resolveAxis` reflected EVERY floor landing, so an object at rest got a sliver of `vy` from gravity each frame, was flipped back up by the 0.82 reflection, and landed again, every frame, forever (the boing limiter only hid most of them). Fix: `BOUNCE_REST_SPEED` (130px/s): a floor impact slower than that just lands (`vy = 0`, grounded, no bounce sound) — in the tile branch and in `bounceOffTrampolineObjects`. The player on a Trampoline is exempt (it keeps its guaranteed launch). Second cause: the Trampoline's enforced `TRAMPOLINE_LAUNCH_SPEED` minimum (900) exceeded most impact speeds, so a crate/bomb/ball on a trampoline GAINED energy every bounce and never settled; now only the player gets the minimum, objects reflect at 0.97 and decay to rest. This reverses the v2.28 note that a dropped pushable launches at exactly -900. Verified: crate/ball on bouncy: 7 boings then rest (was endless); on a trampoline they bounce down to rest within ~15s.

Also hardened, not reproduced: a user reported that the bombs held by gates blow up first thing. `my-project_7.json` as saved does NOT do this in Build or Play (bombs stay on the gates, zero detonations over 9s), so the exact trigger is unknown. One real hole found and closed: the >6-tile fall rule's origin (`fallStartY`) was never cleared by `resetActorsToSpawn` or by teleports, so a bomb moved (Reset, Play restart, drag, door) after resting at a different height could land and count a fall from its OLD height. `resetActorsToSpawn` now clears it, and `stepActor` forgets it whenever an actor jumps more than two tiles between ticks. If bombs still blow at load, the saved project is needed to trace it.

## Trampoline bounces decay for objects (it still took a minute to stop)

Follow-up: with the rest threshold in, a crate/ball on a Trampoline still bounced for about a minute. Cause: Trampoline restitution is 0.97, so each bounce only loses 3% — from a long drop that is ~57 bounces to fall under the 130px/s rest speed. Now only the player bounces at `TRAMPOLINE_RESTITUTION`; any other actor (tile branch in `resolveAxis`, and `bounceOffTrampolineObjects`) uses `BOUNCE_RESTITUTION` (0.82). Measured from a 16-tile drop onto a trampoline or bouncy floor: 7 boings, then rest (crate and hazard ball). A thrown object still rebounds off a trampoline, just no longer forever.

## Cannons load, then fire at a fixed trajectory; Large Stone rides conveyors

**Cannon redesign.** Hitting a cannon used to overwrite the actor's velocity where it stood, so the arc depended on where and how it hit. Now `triggerCannon(actor, geometry, cx, cy, anchorX, anchorY)` LOADS the actor: it is snapped to the cannon's cell centre, frozen and hidden (`actor.cannonLoad`, skipped by `stepActor` via `tickCannonLoad`, by `renderActors`, and by every actor-contact/shove check) for `CANNON_LOAD_MS` (300), with a `boopPulse` on the cannon tile. Then `fireLoadedCannon` launches it from that same centre at `CANNON_LAUNCH_SPEED` along the cannon's 45-degree direction with the `muzzleFlash`. Same rule for the player, crates, bombs, balls and characters (anything `resolveAxis`/the drag handler already allowed to trigger it), and for a dropped carried-cannon actor (anchored at its own centre). The fired actor passes through its own cannon tile via `actor.cannonExit` (resolveAxis ignores that one cell until the AABB has left it). The player's direction input is ignored while `cannonCooldown` (800ms) runs, so holding a key can't bend the arc. Verified: a crate or the player hitting the cannon from the left, right or above, with or without a held key, ends in the same landing spot, and a bomb fired this way does the same. `resetActorsToSpawn` clears `cannonLoad`/`cannonExit`; export gets `tickCannonLoad`/`fireLoadedCannon` and a `CANNON_LOAD_MS` line. This supersedes the earlier Catapult Factory cannon notes about approach direction (fire direction no longer depends on the side hit; the earlier sample traces describe the old behavior and were not re-run).

**Large Stone and conveyors.** Large Stone was fully wind-immune (v2.x "too heavy"), which included conveyor belts, so it never moved. `windAccelFor(actor, conveyorOnly)`: for Large Stone only the belt under it counts; open-air wind tiles and fans still don't push it. In `my-project_9.json` the stone also sat against a solid block one tile to its right on the belt; with that block removed it rides the belt (cols 31 to 54 in about 6s) and carries things standing on it. Verified in that project and in a synthetic belt.

## Surprise Me — a dice button that builds small, funny, live arrangements (Stage 1, revised; v2.45)

(The patch note said "v2.44"; that label is taken by the power-ups section, so this is v2.45.)

A 🎲 button next to Samples (Build mode only, hidden in Play). It places existing pieces from a closed
table, `SURPRISE_TEMPLATES` — no new rules, no scripting, no new piece or engine behavior. Code is one
block right before the Boot section of `index.html`; editor-only, **not** in the export (verified: an
exported file contains none of its identifiers). Rooms it fills export/save/undo/autosave like any other.

**Templates (19).** Fourteen "real" ones — Pinball Parlor, The Fan Club, Moat Problem, Sticky Situation,
Slippery Kitchen, Zero-G Piñata, Conveyor Commute, Button Orchestra, Chaser Parade, Gravity Well Soup,
Magnet Mischief, Bomb Staircase, Gravity Roulette (an uncolored Button flips the room's gravity) — plus six
`nonsense: true` ones: Oops All Black Holes (a wall of holes), Oops All Sprites (a different one of the
project's own pets/collectibles in every cell — `pet*`/`reward*`), Oops All Buttons (a carpet of gravity
buttons), Oops All Trampolines, Crate Fountain War (two spawners firing crates at each other), Chaser Conga.
Each entry: `names` (3–4), `rows`/`legend` (a fixed ~10–13×7 layout), `gravity` ("on"/"off" — what it was
built for), `wrapH` (chance), `requires` (probes in `SURPRISE_REQUIRES`; a failing one silently skips the
template — none are skipped today), `expect` (a no-input observable fact, or `needsInput`), and
`discover`: the legend char that is the thing to find (a hidden reward, a pocket behind a Gate, a gravity
Button — no prose in-game). The harness asserts every template has one. Soft slots (`surpriseKit`) come from
the project itself: wall texture from any plain solid Ground texture (custom ones too), pets from the
default/custom wanderers and fleers (so nothing is a still life), rewards from the collectible kit (never
Heart/Key/Star), chasers from any chaser def. The player is the project's own `playerDefId`.

**Chaos, on purpose.** Pressing the dice is "all bets off": every surprise sets the room's gravity and wrap
to match what it built — gravity opposite to that with `SURPRISE_GRAVITY_CHAOS` (15%, measured 16%),
h-wrap per template (8% closed boxes, 30% open ones), v-wrap 8% — even in a room full of the kid's own
stuff, and the log says "gravity is now on/off". A kid undoes it with one undo. A Runner-camera room never
gets h-wrap (the existing mutual exclusion), and camera mode is only reset to Fixed in an empty/new room.
Templates are also mirrored left-to-right half the time (directional pieces swap: fan/belt/spawner e↔w,
cannon ne↔nw...).

**Placement — never overwrites by itself.** `surpriseMe(seed?, opts)` finds a free rectangle (nothing
painted, no actor home, no earlier surprise's area) inside the part of the room the kid is looking at
(`editorPanCol`…+`COLS`), snuggest spots first so several surprises pack into corners instead of
fragmenting the room; the seed picks among the snuggest. A 24×18 room holds about 3–4. Each surprise is
recorded on the hot room as `room.surprises = [{seed, templateId, rect, sig}]`; `sig` hashes the rectangle's
content so "untouched" can be told from "the kid edited it". When nothing fits, a three-button dialog
(`surpriseAskFull`, same seed on every button) offers **Create a new room** / **Overwrite part of this room**
/ **Cancel**. Overwrite ranks every position by how much of the *kid's own* stuff it would replace
(untouched earlier surprises cost 0, edited ones count as the kid's) and picks at random among everything
about as cheap as the best (exactly as cheap when the best costs nothing; +30% otherwise), steering away from
the surprise placed most recently so repeated presses rotate through the room instead of re-replacing one
spot (a strict minimum used to land in the same place every time — fixed), never picks a rectangle holding the
player, and its sub-label says "Only replaces an untouched surprise" or "Replaces some of your own stuff
(undo brings it back)". `opts.noOverwrite` hides it (Stage 2's Try-this must never overwrite). Each press is
one `pushUndoSnapshot()`. The first surprise in a room places the project's one player; later ones, and
surprises in a new room when the player lives elsewhere, don't (the floors still validate a virtual player
cell). Channel colors for Plates/Gates/Buttons prefer colors not already used in the room.

**Floors** (`surpriseValidate`, re-roll same seed + attempt counter, max 20, then "couldn't build"): player on
a free tile and able to move, ≥15 free tiles reachable, no lethal/hazard/bomb or chaser/hazard ball within 3
tiles of the player (also checked against an existing player in the room), no black hole within 7.5 tiles of
the player (its pull radius is 7), ≤2 doors per color, every spawner has an open target cell, piece-count
bound. Existing tiles outside the footprint count as walls for these checks.

**Also**: `#surprise=4821` in the URL or `surpriseMe(4821)` in the console rebuilds the same arrangement
(the hash is cleared afterwards so a reload doesn't stack another copy). Pressing plays chip design `rattle`
(`CHIP_DESIGNS.rattle`, hand-written, **never listened to**), the arrangement appearing plays `pop`; both go
through `playChip` directly, nothing was added to `sounds`. Caption bar shows `Surprise #4821: Tuesday Bomb
Situation` and keeps it after the mouse leaves the dice.

**Tests** — `tests/surprise.test.js [fuzzCount]` (not deployed; injects a throwaway hook into a temp copy of
`index.html`, the repo file stays clean): every template has a discoverable; fuzz (generate, press, Play, 10
simulated seconds with no input; exception, NaN, dead on frame one, frozen board, unsatisfiable floors),
gravity-chaos rate sanity; per-template `expect` on 8 canonical seeds (`chaos:false`: native gravity, no
wrap); determinism; 3-surprise-in-one-room placement (no overlaps, kid's own cells/crate byte for byte, one
undo restores exactly, one player); a 4-surprise chaotic crowded-room fuzz; the full-room dialog and its
three outcomes (overwrite prefers untouched surprises, new room, cancel changes nothing); a packed room
reports a non-zero overwrite cost and does nothing until asked; two rooms keep a single player.
1000 seeds: 0 failures in every template. Not done: Stage 2 ("Try this" in the Objects tab, never overwrites).

### Surprise Me, Stage 2: "Try this" (Objects tab)

A 🎲 **Try this** button at the top of the Objects tab (`#tryThisBtn`; hover caption "Pick an item, then try it
in a sample."). It reads the selected palette piece (`surpriseSelectedPiece()`: a geometry like fan/cannon/
spawner/Gate/trampoline/magnet/bomb/black hole/button/plate, a Ground Effect like bouncy/sticky/slippery/lethal/
conveyor, or crate/large stone/chaser/wanderer/collectible), finds templates that feature it
(`surpriseTemplatesFor` — a lookup over each template's own `legend` via `SURPRISE_PIECE_MATCH`, no per-piece
logic) and builds one via `surpriseMe(seed, { templateIds, canonical: true, chaos: false, noOverwrite: true })`.
**Canonical** = no chaos (native gravity, no wrap), no random wall skin, hazard-ball spawner content, and the
project's default pet/chaser/reward instead of random ones. **Never overwrites**: a full room offers only
"Create a new room" / "Cancel". Nothing relevant selected (player, a custom character, erase...) falls back
to a plain Surprise Me (chaos on, still no overwrite). Same placement, undo step, caption and log as the dice.
Editor-only, not exported. Tests (`tests/surprise.test.js` section 7): every template builds canonically and
plays 5s clean, every piece key matches a template, 17 palette selections each build a template containing
their piece, fallback, never-overwrite in a packed room, undo + caption, button visible in Objects.

## Bomb piles, Build-mode blasts, and a stack that twitched forever (from `my-project_9.json`)

A saved Catapult Factory variant held 71 bomb actors: 56 of them off the board (y about -28150) and many sharing
one identical spawn point (20 on a single cell). Painting a bomb onto a cell a bomb already fills used to stack
another actor each time. Findings and fixes:

- **Runaway stacks.** `shovePushables` re-seated deeply overlapping objects every frame, so each hopped up onto its
  neighbour forever. Reset-to-start on the real save put the 56 stray bombs back on their spawns and 20 frames later
  all of them were off the board again (reproduced before the fix, gone after). Now a contact deeper than 75% of the
  body is not resolved (only genuine, shallow contacts are), `convertBombTilesToActors` waits while another
  standable object fills the cell (`cellOccupiedByStandable`), `migrateStackedObjects` keeps one hand-placed
  standable object per identical spawn point on load, and `recoverStrayActor` (called after each `stepActor`) sends an
  object that ends up far outside the room back to its spawn (spawner-made ones are removed).
- **The endless boing.** Two non-player objects merely resting against each other (the spawner's pile, bombs on
  gates) were "reflected apart" every 500ms, boing included. `resolveActorContact`'s non-player branch now ignores
  contacts with a closing speed under 40px/s.
- **Reset after a Build-mode blast.** Blast damage in Build mode is logged (`buildBlastLog`: cleared solid tiles with
  their color/skin, and hand-placed bomb actors that went off) and "Reset to start" restores it (`restoreBuildBlasts`;
  a tile only if its cell is still empty). Play mode already restored everything from its snapshot. Loading a project
  clears the log.
- **Not found: the explosion at load.** The file as saved loads and runs quietly (no detonation in 40s in Build or Play);
  it is the already-damaged result of earlier sessions. Unconfirmed theory: bombs held up by gates fall >6 tiles when
  their gate's channel opens (a key, a plate, a stone dropping on a plate), which detonates them by design. If a fresh
  load still blows up, send a screen recording or the autosave state at that moment.
- **The red gate "opening on its own" is the level's own chain**: the green key opens the green gate (54,2) that holds a
  Large Stone, the stone falls onto the red plate (54,4), and the red channel turns on (verified by trace).

Tests: `tests/bombs.test.js` (pile stays on the board and is silent, no overlapping stacks, blast undone by Reset,
stack migration, stray recovery). `recoverStrayActor`/`cellOccupiedByStandable` are in the export's `runtimeFunctions`.

## Keys no longer act as a channel (they only open Locked Doors)

The v2.44 power-up patch made a picked-up Key a third source in `isChannelOn`, so a green key opened every
green Gate, fan, button and plate. The user's intent was always that a Key only unlocks Locked Doors and is
never a trigger like a Switch. `isChannelOn` is back to `switchChannels || plateChannelsActive`;
`keyChannelsUnlocked` now only feeds `checkLockedDoorUnlock` (touch a same-color Locked Door while holding that
color's key) and the HUD key chips. The earlier "Key = a permanent button" and key-x-gate/fan/button matrix notes
above are history. Verified on `my-project_9.json`: picking up the green key changes no channel and no Gate.

## Cannon: mirrored (not upside-down) west art, and a real parabolic arc

West-facing cannons are now the east art flipped left-right (`mirrorArtH`: SW = mirror of SE, NW = mirror of NE) instead of a 90-degree rotation chain that put the cannon upside down. The shot itself was stopping 1-2 tiles out because `applyAxisFriction` runs on x every frame, airborne or not, and ate the 495px/s sideways speed in ~0.27s. A fired actor now carries `ballistic` (set in `fireLoadedCannon`; cleared on landing, or immediately in a gravity-off room) and skips horizontal friction and the speed-cap clamp while it flies, and steering/input stay off for the flight. Launch vector is `CANNON_DIR_X/Y` = (0.49, 0.8717) at `CANNON_LAUNCH_SPEED` 700, i.e. vx 343, vy 609: about 4 tiles above the cannon and 9 tiles away on level ground (measured 9.2-9.4 for crate and player, both directions). This supersedes the earlier 45-degree numbers. Not re-traced: the Catapult Factory / Worst Elevator cannon legs (their ~3-tile arcs are now much longer), and gravity-off rooms keep the old friction behavior.

## Constant bump sound on load, and a spawner bomb that crushed you (from `my-project_11.json`)

- **The sync'd bump + twitch.** Every object resting on the floor lands with a ~48px/s "impact" whenever one frame is long (gravity for two frames before the collision zeroes it), so every bomb/stone thudded and wobbled in sync, again and again (17-34 per burst in the user's project). `IMPACT_NOISE_FLOOR` went 0.15 -> 0.4 (~100px/s; a one-tile fall lands at ~300), so resting objects stay silent and still. Measured 30s after load in Build: 0 thuds (was bursts).
- **The "random game reset".** Not a reset bug: a life was being lost. The south-facing spawner at (14,8) launches a bomb at ~420px/s downward; one landing beside/on the player counted as a "falling crate crush" (`resolveActorContact`, vy>200 from above) -> `triggerHazard` -> lose a life -> whole level restarts. Caught by spying `triggerHazard` during a random-input fuzz (stack + the offending actor: a spawner bomb, vy 533-565). Now a bomb never crushes (its danger is its blast) and any fresh spawn (`bouncyCooldown` still running) never crushes. Crates falling from height still do.
- Tooling: the fuzz (`p11f.js`-style: random keys + teleport next to bombs + hazard spy) is the way to find "random" resets. 6 runs x 90s after the fix: no hazard hits. Not found: any hidden off-screen colliding object (there is none; the 54 bombs in room 2 are inert).

## The Bomb Factory replaces The Catapult Factory

The coded Catapult Factory sample (`buildCatapultFactoryGame`, its factory-panel/girder/hazard-stripe/blueprint art constants and `fillFactoryRect` helpers) was removed and replaced by the user's own project (`Bomb_Factory.json`), shipped like the other user-authored samples: `BOMB_FACTORY_PROJECT` embeds the snapshot and `buildBombFactoryGame()` calls `restoreProjectSnapshot(structuredClone(...))`. Rooms are "The Bomb Factory" and "The Bomb Factory Annex"; the textures it uses (`cfPanel`/`cfGirder`/`cfHazardStripe`, blueprint backgrounds) live in the project's own `groundTileDefs`/`backgroundTileDefs`. Actors were reset to spawn with zero velocity and spawner-made actors dropped (6 + 8); the three spawners keep their loaded bombs/crate. Earlier Catapult Factory sections (three routes, rule-one gating, cannon traces) are history, not the current level; it was not re-traced or playtested beyond loading, playing and exporting cleanly (16-check sweep).

## Movers (traffic and floaters): lanes of things that carry you or hurt you (v2.46)

Shipped from the Crossing Guard Phase 0/1 work (`prototypes/crossing-guard-test.html` for the
measurements, `docs/toy-game-maker-v2.46-patch-notes.md` for the full design and risks). **Option B for
wrapping** was chosen: only an actor currently riding a mover wraps by exactly one room length; everyone
else keeps the original `applyWorldWrap` reset-to-edge rule, so no existing wrap room changes. (Label is
informal; v2.45 is Surprise Me.) The Crossing Guard sample itself (Phase 2) is NOT built yet.

**The piece.** Actor temperament `MOVER_KIND`; six fixed presets in a new Objects row "Traffic and
floaters" (`MOVER_PRESETS`, def ids `mvScooter`/`mvCart` = *hurts*, `mvDuck`/`mvTurtle`/`mvLog`/`mvCroc` =
*floats*), all 2 tiles wide, length 1/1/1/2/4/6, speed tier slow 60 / medium 110 / fast 180 px/s
(`MOVER_SPEEDS`). `moverKind`/`lenTiles`/`wTiles`/`speedTier`/`moverArts`/`moverHeadArts` live on the
**def** (so a preset is one built-in `actorDefs` entry, backfilled in `restoreProjectSnapshot`'s list);
the placed actor only adds `moverDir` ("s" default, then n/e/w) and the transient `moverT`. Art is 8x8
per tile cell drawn in the mover's own lane frame (heading +y), rotated per direction (`drawMover`, called
as a prepass in `renderActors`, with ghost copies at +-one lane length so it slides on/off the edge);
`moverHeadArts` (crocodile only) is the front cell. Click a placed mover to rotate it s->n->e->w
(`rotateMover`, resets its phase and swaps w/h). It is not draggable; erase and replace to move it.

**Motion is derived, not integrated.** Position = spawn + dir*speed*`moverT`, modulo the lane extent
(`ROWS*TILE` vertical, `roomCols()*TILE` horizontal; `moverPlaceAt`). `stepActor` has an early-return
branch like the Moving Platform's, so wind/fan/magnet/black hole/bounce/cannon/bomb knockback never
apply. Movers are excluded from `checkActorContacts` and `cellOccupiedByActor` and from
`recoverStrayActor`. `resetActorsToSpawn` resets `moverT` (Play start/restart, Build Reset), so every
attempt's lane timing is identical.

**`tickMovers(dt)` runs before the per-actor loop in `tick()`, and the order is load-bearing**: (1) decide
riders from this frame's starting positions — any non-carried actor whose CENTER is inside a floats mover
(modular, `moverCovers`); (2) move every mover; (3) carry each rider by the same displacement, clipped by
`actorBlockedByTiles` (a solid post pins a rider; the log slides on); (4) hurts-contact check. Because the
carry happens before any `stepActor`, the water check always sees post-carry positions (Phase 0: checking
before the carry hurt a rider within a frame of the log's trailing edge). `actor.ridingMover` marks a rider
for `applyWorldWrap`'s Option-B rule. Riding works with no gravity; the older gravity-only riding step for
Moving Platform/standables is untouched.

**Water rule.** In `checkPlayerTileContacts`, `hazard`/`lethal` ground is skipped while the player's center
is over a floats mover (`moverFloatsUnder`); `checkNpcLethalContacts` has the same skip. In a room that
contains a floater (`roomHasFloatingMovers`), plain Hazard only reacts when the player's CENTER cell is the
hazard cell (stepping off a log onto the bank overlaps a water cell for a moment — traced, that was a hit
without this rule). `triggerHazard(actor, away?, sfx?)` now returns true/false and takes an optional unit
vector and a sound name: a water drop (only in rooms with floaters) is knocked toward `lastDryX/Y`
(updated each frame the player is on non-hazard ground and not riding) with the new `splash` sound; a hurts
mover hit uses the perpendicular-to-lane direction toward the side the player came from, steps the player
clear of the lane, and plays `honk`. With no `away` the old behavior (back against velocity, straight up
when still) is exactly unchanged — verified for a plain hazard in a room with no mover. Hearts and star
apply for free (everything goes through `triggerHazard` -> `takePlayerHit`); lethal tiles are unaffected
except for the float exemption. **Deviation from the patch notes**: the hurts check lives in `tickMovers`,
not in a `resolveActorContact` branch, since movers are excluded from actor contacts.

**Wrap.** A vertical lane needs the room's Wrap Up/Down on to carry a rider across the seam (horizontal: Wrap
Left/Right); otherwise the rider is carried into the room edge and pinned like a solid. Build mode logs a
hint when a mover is placed/rotated into such a room; it never flips the rule itself.

**Sound.** `CHIP_DESIGNS.honk` (two square beeps) and `CHIP_DESIGNS.splash` (noise burst over a falling
pulse), literal arrows in `sounds`, `SOUND_LIMITS` 250ms/1 each. Hand-written, never listened to.

**Export.** `MOVER_KIND`/`MOVER_SPEEDS`/`MOVER_DIRS`/`MOVER_GHOST_COPIES` constants line; the thirteen mover
functions (`moverDefOf` ... `tickMovers`, `drawMover`) in `runtimeFunctions`. Mover defs ship with
`actorDefs`. No new template globals (`ridingMover`/`lastDryX/Y`/`moverT` are actor fields).

**Interaction matrix rows.** floats x player/NPC/crate/bomb whose center overlaps: carried by the mover's
displacement plus own steering. floats x Hazard/Lethal ground under a rider: skipped. hurts x player: plain
Hazard with lane-perpendicular knockback, hearts/star apply. hurts x NPC/crate/bomb: nothing. Anything x
mover (push, wind, fan, magnet, black hole, cannon, bounce, bomb blast): no effect. gravity x mover: none
(unsupported in a gravity room beyond looping/carrying). solid tile x rider: clips the carry. mover x mover:
no interaction (lanes shouldn't overlap).

**Known limits / flags.** Click-placed movers snap to the clicked cell; lanes must not overlap (first mover
in array order wins). The swatch for a preset shows its first 8x8 cell only (the duck's left half). The
mover art is hand-drawn and plain; sprites per preset are open for the sample. Fairness (speeds/gaps) is the
sample's job: Phase 0 limits are cars <= ~120px/s with 4+ tile gaps, floats <= ~220px/s and >= 4 tiles long;
the shipped tiers sit inside that, except the fast scooter (180) which wants wide gaps.

Tests: `tests/movers.test.js` (37 checks, hook-injected temp copy like `tests/bombs.test.js`): rider stays put
over ~2 laps with zero relative drift and zero hits; steering adds; +-4px edge sweep both edges with jittered
dt; lane spacing stable over 50s; walk-off to the bank; solid-post pin; car knockback direction/honk; water
drop knockback toward the last dry spot/splash; plain hazard unchanged without a floater; Play hearts/no
heart/star; Reset phases; snapshot round trip and old-snapshot backfill; rotate cycle; immunity; NPC on a log
over lethal; horizontal lane; palette row + canvas placement + click-rotate + caption; and an exported file
whose rider is carried on a log with no hit and zero errors. Also re-run: 16-check sweep/export,
`tests/bombs.test.js`, `tests/surprise.test.js`.

## "Crossing Guard" — a Frogger-style crossing, the movers sample

`buildCrossingGuardGame()` / `drawCrossingGuardIcon`, registered last in `SAMPLE_GAMES` (coded builder, not a
saved project; its frog, water skin and Paint tiles are sample-scoped defs added inside the builder, so
nothing leaks into new projects and it never depends on autosave). One 72-wide Follow-camera room, no
gravity, **v-wrap on** (the vertical lanes loop), win by touching the goal column at col 63.

**Layout** (columns): start bank 0-4; a six-lane road at cols 5-30 (2-wide lanes at 7,11,15,19,23,27 with
2-wide dry islands and dashed lines between; carts = slow hurts, scooters = fast hurts with wide gaps,
alternating s/n); median bank 31-35 with a heart; a **seven-lane river at 36-49 whose 2-wide lanes are
ADJACENT** (logs, turtles, a crocodile, ducks, logs, turtles, a crocodile; alternating directions; water is
`none:hazard` with an animated `cgWater` skin); goal meadow 50-71 with a finish line and coins. Coins sit on the
road islands; hearts/coins are optional (a goal tile exists, so collection doesn't win).

**Design lesson, found by tracing, not guessed**: the first layout put a 1-column water gap between river
lanes. That is impossible: the water rule is centre-based, so the frog's centre is over open water for ~0.15s
while crossing the gap, and drowns. Lanes must touch (a hop is "be over a float of the next lane as your centre
crosses the boundary"), and the bank must touch the first/last lane for the same reason. Also: a rider sitting
exactly on a float's edge is fine (centre-based), so a bot/test must not require margin around the *current*
float.

**Fairness traces** (`tests/crossingguard.test.js`, hook-injected temp copy, `requestAnimationFrame` disabled so
every run is deterministic manual ticking; 30 checks): a patient bot (crosses a road lane only when no car will
touch its row during the crossing; hops a river lane only when a float of the next lane will be under its centre
at the boundary; waits on islands/floats, which are always safe) wins with **zero hits** from six different start
delays (~16-22s); holding right from the start never wins (dies on the road); holding right through the river
alone wins 0/12; idling for two minutes on the start bank costs nothing; per-hop "how long must a patient player
wait" measured over 400s: road lanes 1.1-2s, river hops 0-2.3s, bank->first log 3.1s (all <= 14s asserted);
the lives loop works in the real engine (a car hit costs a life, the level restarts after ~650ms with every mover
phase back at 0 and READY! up); the camera follows the frog to the goal; and the **exported file** is crossed by
the same bot with zero hits and zero errors. Tuning that came out of it: the first river had 44-67% float
coverage per lane and was never gating (windows almost always open) — thinned to one or two floats per lane
(33-44%) so the river now needs real timing; the bot's stopping distance (the frog coasts ~14px after releasing
a key) had to be respected when approaching a road lane (a test, not a level, issue).

**Not done / flags**: the road and river were traced with a scripted bot, not a human; the sample's tuning
numbers (lane speeds, float counts) are a first pass. Horizontal lanes are not used. The goal is a whole column,
so any row wins. Screenshots were taken in Build/Play at several camera positions and the gallery tile checked.

## "Rock Bottom" — a Boulder-Dash-shaped digging cave (gravity, dirt you dig, boulders you drop)

`buildRockBottomGame()` / `drawRockBottomIcon` / `RB_MAP`, registered last in `SAMPLE_GAMES`. Gallery line (the gallery has no
description field, so it lives here): *"A princess drops her tiara gems down the old well and digs down after them: dig the dirt,
let the boulders fall where you want them."* One 72x18 Follow-camera room, gravity on, no wrap, **no goal tile**: collecting all
12 gems wins (v2.44 rule: collection wins only when no goal tile exists). No Heart/Key/Star. **No engine code changed** — every piece
is an existing mechanic; the sample-scoped skins/backgrounds/actor defs live inside the builder (so nothing leaks into new projects
and nothing depends on an autosave). The player is the existing Princess preset art ("Princess Pip", `rbHero`), the beast is the
existing monster preset art as a `chaser` (`rbBeast`).

**Layout** (`RB_MAP`, generated from ASCII; `#` bedrock, `D` dirt, `.` sky, `G` grass-on-rock, `T` grass dirt). Rows 0-2 sky, row 3
grass, row 17 bedrock. Part 1 is the hub (cols 24-44) and the other four parts are bedrock-sealed chambers entered through one-dirt
plugs, so any order of 2/3/4 works. Everything below the surface has a cave wall behind it (five palettes, 2-3 decorations each:
roots, worms, mushrooms, bricks, pipes, bones, glow, claw marks, eyes, crystals, glowshrooms) and every solid cell has a skin: dirt =
warm brown speckle (3 variants + grass dirt), bedrock = cool, dark, chunky-outlined cracked blocks (4 variants per part palette,
one per part with a fossil/gold fleck/ember seam/claw marks/crystal decoration). Lava reuses the built-in `lthLava`.

1. **Topsoil** — start on the surface, walk off into the old well (cell 26), land in a pre-dug corridor (row 10) with two gems. A
   dirt shelf at (34,10) holds two stacked crates above it; the pillar east of it is five tiles of bedrock with the third gem on top
   in a sealed pocket. Dig the shelf from the side: both crates fall one cell and stack (44px). Stand on the stack, jump to the
   pillar top. The pillar is 5 tall *because jump apex is 122/127/130px at 30/60/144fps* — a 4-tall pillar (128px) is reachable or
   not depending on frame rate; with one crate (apex+22 = 144-152) the 160px top is still out of reach, with two (166-174) it is in
   (checked at all three frame rates). The chimney the stack falls in is two columns wide so the player can jump up out of the corridor
   cell beside it (a 1-cell-wide chimney under a bedrock lid gave only 10px of headroom and made the stack unreachable).
2. **Plate Vault** (west, row 10) — plug, hall, a dirt shelf at (19,10) with two crates stacked above it and a 2-deep pit under it with
   the red plate at the bottom; the red gate sits at (14,10) with two gems behind it. Dig the shelf from the east and both crates fall
   through into the pit onto the plate (0.30s). The chamber above the hall is open on both sides on purpose (see the squeeze note).
3. **Lava Moat** (east, row 15, a one-tile-tall tunnel) — plug at (44,15), then a dirt "cork" at (52,15) sitting on a 2-deep lava pit
   (rows 16-17) with three Large Stones stacked in a bedrock shaft above it. Dig the cork from the west: the stones fall and fill the
   pit to 2px above the walkway; hop the 2px lip and walk on. Two gems beyond.
4. **Beast's Tunnels** (southwest, rows 14-15) — plug at (24,15), a crate on the floor, a 1-wide **plate pit** (19,16) with the green
   plate, a 2-wide **trap pit** (16-17,16), a green gate at (13,14-15) and the sealed lair behind it with the beast and three gems.
   Push the crate west: it falls into the plate pit (the only hole it can reach first), the gate opens, the beast charges east, runs off
   the edge into the trap pit and is stuck for good (a chaser can't climb even a 10px step). Hop the trap pit (24px takeoff window;
   the tunnel is 3 tall there) and take the gems. Without the trap pit the beast catches the player (traced).
5. **Deep Vault** (east, past the moat) — far-side crate, a blue **plate pit** at (62,16) directly in front of the blue gate (63,13-15),
   vault with two gems. Push the crate west to east: it falls into the pit onto the plate (0.97s), the gate opens, hop out of the pit
   and walk in. Reachable only through Part 3's tunnel, independent of Part 2 and Part 4.

**Dependency answer**: 1 -> {2, 3, 4} in any order (the hub is Part 1: the well, the corridor and the shaft at cols 28-30 down to the
lower corridor). 5 needs 3 and nothing else. Every part has a boulder-only step: 1 the stack for the pillar gem, 2/4/5 a boulder on a
plate (the player standing on the plate cannot also be at the gate), 3 the stones (the cork cannot be broken without releasing them).

**Trace table** (`tests/rockbottom.test.js`, real constants, manual 1/60 ticks):

| part | steps | boulder fall | escape / what a hold-through does | margin |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Topsoil | well 0.73s, 2 gems, shelf 0.95s, jump to stack, jump to pillar | crates land 0.12s after the crumble | nothing can land on the digger (the crumbled cell is beside him; holding Right pushes the stack 5px into the pillar) | hold/release/early: 0 hits; pillar reachable only with the 2-stack at 30/60/144fps |
| 2 Plate Vault | plug 0.95s, shelf 0.97s | 0.30s to rest on the plate | holding Left walks into the column and shoves both crates west along the hall (no hit); recoverable by walking back east (crate falls in) or carrying | release at the crumble: gate opens, 0 hits |
| 3 Lava Moat | plug, cork 0.95s | stones at rest 0.37s | holding Right walks into the open pit: lava, one life, level restarts clean (verified after the real 650ms) | release at the crumble: 0 hits, crosses |
| 4 Beast's Tunnels | plug 0.95s, push the crate ~0.8s | gate to beast-in-pit 0.93s | the player ends up standing in the 10px-deep plate pit (stuck until he jumps), beast already in its pit: no hit; falling into the trap pit = a life | hop window 24px |
| 5 Deep Vault | push ~0.97s | n/a | holding Right cannot lose the crate (pit stops it) | gate stays open |

Order independence: all six orders of {2, 3+5, 4} win after Part 1 (same solvers from each part's door); a full real-input
playthrough (well, dig, jump, hop, push, hop...) wins with all 3 lives, and the exported file plays the Part 1/3/5 inputs with
zero errors. Wrong-order / mistake recovery: nothing can be left unrecoverable in a way a restart (lives) does not clear — Part 2's
shoved crates are recoverable in place; Part 3's only mistake is dying (restart restores cork, stones, player and READY!).

**Engine facts found building it (reported, not patched)**:
- A crate or stone squeezed between the player and a *solid* wall (a tile directly beside the column it falls in) is ejected upward
  through the wall by `resolveAxis`'s landing branch (seen at y=74 above the surface, twice). Part 2's chamber is open on both sides
  and Part 3's stones live in a shaft above the tunnel for this reason. Part 3's hold-through still ejects one stone but the level
  restarts anyway.
- Lava kills on *touching* the top face (walking over a lava cell at floor level kills), so a stone fill must end above the walkway;
  two stones leave a 12px lip (needs headroom 12 on top of the player), three stones in a 2-deep pit leave 2px.
- A running player crosses a 1-wide (32px) hole without falling (momentum), but not a 2-wide one; a hop over an open 1-wide lava pit
  in a 1-tall tunnel works with an early jump (so the pit is never left open: the cork releases the stones the instant it breaks).
- Digging reads the tile the AABB overlaps at the player's *own* row; with Down held the floor under his feet is dug too, so
  every dig spot has a bedrock floor (one dirt patch, 3 wide, in the corridor floor is the "dig down here" cue).
- Gems are removed 220ms after pickup by a real `setTimeout`, so tests that tick by hand must wait real time before counting gems or
  checking for the win.

**Cut / not done**: gems do not sparkle (collectible art is a single static frame — an animated gem would need a new frame mechanism);
no spare stones/crates in Parts 3-5 (a spare in the tunnel can be pushed in front of the dig spot; the failure cases all restart
cleanly); the lives/restart path is not exercised in the exported file; sound is untouched. Camera screenshots at the five Follow
positions at the start and after digging a tunnel through every part are in `prototypes/rock-bottom-shots/`.

## Death resets fire once; side-dig lock; handled objects can't crush you

Three play-feel fixes reported together.

1. **Lava cost two lives.** `consumeLifeIfPlaying` scheduled a level restart 650ms later, but `HAZARD_COOLDOWN_MS` is
   450ms, so a player still standing in lava was hit again before the reset (lives 3 -> 1, two restarts; reproduced).
   `levelResetPending` (+ `levelResetTimer`) now makes `consumeLifeIfPlaying` a no-op while a death reset is counting down,
   and `triggerLethalHazard`/`triggerHazard`/the chaser catch return early too (no repeated splat or knockback). The timer
   is cleared by `clearTransientRuntimeState` (every Play restart/exit) and the export's `restartStandaloneGame`. This also
   removes a real "random reset" source: a stale timer from an earlier death used to fire after the player had already
   restarted (or after a second overlapping hit), resetting the new session mid-play.
2. **Other random resets (grab/move).** Not reproduced as a separate bug (throwing into a wall and grabbing the bottom crate
   of a stack both cost nothing in tests). Hardened the crush rule instead: a falling crate/stone only crushes at
   `CRUSH_MIN_VY` (400, ~2 tiles of fall; was 200, ~0.5 tile, so disturbing a stack could cost a life and restart the
   level), and an object the player just dropped or threw (`safeUntil`, `SAFE_AFTER_HANDLING_MS` 2500) never crushes them.
   If a reset still happens when grabbing/moving, a screen recording or the saved project is needed.
3. **Side dig carried you into the hole.** Holding Right+Down through a diggable wall kept accelerating the player the
   instant it opened (Rock Bottom's lava cork). On a horizontal dig-through `resolveAxis` now zeroes `vx` and sets
   `actor.digLock`; `stepActor` ignores horizontal input until both left/right keys are released once. Down digs are
   unaffected. Tests that dig then re-press a key must tick one frame between (`B.dig` in `rockbottom.test.js` does).
   Rock Bottom Part 3's old "hold-through dies in lava" tests now assert the opposite (stops at the opening, no life lost).

Export: `CRUSH_MIN_VY`/`SAFE_AFTER_HANDLING_MS` constants line; `levelResetPending`/`levelResetTimer` declared and reset
in the export template. Tests: `tests/deathdig.test.js` (9 checks), `tests/rockbottom.test.js` updated (48 pass).

## Door checkpoints, a "never disappear" guard for the player, and three sample touch-ups

1. **Doors are checkpoints.** Dying with lives left no longer restarts the whole game: `respawnAtCheckpoint()` puts the
   CURRENT room back to how it started (a fresh clone from the pristine room list — `buildSnapshot.rooms` in the editor,
   `initialGameData.rooms` in an export, via each environment's own `pristineRoomList()`) and the player back where they
   entered it: the arrival spot of the last door (`setCheckpoint()` runs at Play start and at the end of
   `beginRoomTransition`) or the game start. Other rooms keep their progress. This room's own changes are undone
   (broken walls, moved crates, collected items, spawned content) as are `switchChannels`, keys, locked doors, hearts and
   score, which are restored from the copy stored with the checkpoint. READY! plays again; the player gets a door cooldown
   so the door they stand on can't whisk them out. If no checkpoint applies it falls back to the old whole-level restart.
   Game over (last life) is unchanged. Warps are not checkpoints. Earlier "restarts the whole level" notes are history.
2. **Never disappear.** Fuzzing (random keys, teleports next to enemies, every sample) reproduced the report: a player
   overlapping solid ground (or the room edge) deeper than a collision can resolve ping-pongs one tile per frame with
   zero velocity and leaves the board for good. `keepPlayerOnBoard(p)` (called from `tick()` after the player's step)
   puts the player back at the last clean spot in the same room (else the room start) when it is outside the room on a
   non-wrapped axis (beyond `PLAYER_BOARD_MARGIN` 6px), non-finite, or embedded in solid for `PLAYER_EMBED_FRAMES` (12)
   frames in a row, and zeroes velocity. The enemy contact reaction itself (`reflectActorsApart`) is mild; the root
   physics weakness (deep-overlap resolution picks a side by velocity sign) is not rewritten, the guard contains it.
   Fuzz after the guard: 0 off-board frames across all ten samples.
3. **Sample fixes** (all embedded projects): Sushi Train room 1 — two belt tiles that pointed the wrong way, (8,12) now
   west and (9,17) now east (interpreting "two not facing the correction direction" as the odd tile in each belt; retune
   if another pair was meant). Worst Elevator room 2 — the upper-left wind field no longer points up: those tiles aim
   down, and the two tiles at the diagonal edge of each row aim west. Bomb Factory room 2 — the middle wall's lower
   part (col 12, rows 13-16) that had been saved blown away is solid again; compared against the older upload
   `my-project_11.json`, whose other differences (lava moved, one tile at the far left) were left as the user's edits.

Export: `setCheckpoint`/`respawnAtCheckpoint`/`resetActorsToSpawn`/`purgeSpawnedActors`/`keepPlayerOnBoard` in
`runtimeFunctions`, `checkpoint` declared and set in the export template, `PLAYER_*` constants line. Tests:
`tests/deathdig.test.js` (19 checks incl. checkpoint and guard); verified in an exported Sushi Train that a death in room 2
returns to room 2's door.

## Caveat: the Movers engine piece is "sample-only" for now

The v2.46 Movers ("Traffic and floaters") engine mechanics — lane motion derived from `moverT`, rider
carrying, the water/float rule, lane-perpendicular hurt knockback, rider-only wrap (Option B) and the
`honk`/`splash` sounds — exist to power the **Crossing Guard** sample. The decision for now is to keep them
as a *sample*: they may be folded into the base game in a later upgrade, but that has not been decided, and
nothing should assume they are part of the core toolbox. In practice:

- Don't treat Movers as a general palette feature when designing new pieces, templates or other samples
  (e.g. Surprise Me / Try this must not start featuring them without an explicit go-ahead).
- Keep the code self-contained (the mover functions, `MOVER_KIND` and the Objects-tab "Traffic and floaters"
  row) so it can be promoted, gated or removed cleanly. If it is ever promoted, revisit the open flags in
  the Movers section (placement snapping, lane overlap, swatch art, fairness tuning).
