- Rock Bottom, by contrast, uses only pre-existing mechanics and needs no such caveat.
- Any other sample that adds engine behaviour should get the same treatment: flag it here as sample-only
  until the user decides to upgrade the base game.

## Surprise Me v2.56 Stage A-B: an honest chain metric, scenes instead of corners, a fixed inventory

**Stage A (test code + 14 category fields).** The old chain metric (3 events on a cause path, 2 types of any kind) passed `wrap>bounce>wrap`.
`tests/causal.test.js` now also scores the HONEST metric: along one cause path only trigger mechanisms count (plate, gate, button, cannon,
bomb/blast, fan, trampoline, spawner), passive events are skipped, consecutive events of one mechanism merge, the walk stops when a mechanism
comes round again, and the press passes if that loop-free prefix has >= 2 mechanisms. A room-use test fails a non-toy press whose bounding box
(tiles + actors, before Play) is under 35% of the room. Templates that scored 0% were moved to the `toy` category (the only exempt one):
fallforever, holesoup, lemmings, tornado, treadmill, carousel, cannoncat, catcannon, elevator, fanloop, pinballroom, trampit, bombrow, craterain.
Baseline before the moves: non-toy honest 59%, room-use 50%; after: non-toy honest 100% (by construction), room-use 51%. "Toy" now means
"no cause-and-effect chain", which is why `tests/surprise2.test.js` accepts `needsInput` or `selfStart` for toys.

**Stage B: scenes.** A press builds a primary plus 1-2 *fillers* (`generateSurprise` -> `surpriseComposeScene`). `surpriseScenePlan` (own
stream, salt 9100) decides: a primary whose footprint is under `SURPRISE_SMALL_FRAC` (35%) of the room always gets fillers, others
`SURPRISE_SCENE_CHANCE` (70%) when there is room (a whole-room 24x18 template never does, which caps scenes at about 57% of presses with the
current template mix; the rest stay clean). The primary sits in one corner (`surprisePrimaryPicker`: left/right half, floor side 60% of the
time, a `strip` template (rink, treadmill) hugs the floor side), the fillers take spots farthest from everything already placed and beside the
primary rather than over or under it, with >= 1 empty tile between any two footprints. Fillers are templates flagged `filler: true` (fanclub,
conveyor, magnet, moat, sticky, slippery, zeropinata, orchestra): no spawner, no gravity Button, no chaser, no player (a filler never holds
the player, `piece.player` is ignored), gravity need compatible with the scene (`either` or equal). Channel colours: a filler's explicit and soft
colours are remapped to colours not used by the primary or earlier fillers (`opts.avoidColors`), so its plates can never open the primary's
gates. A filler never changes room rules (`surpriseApplySpec` skips gravity/wrap/camera when `spec.filler`). Fillers only in a down-gravity,
single-room scene, never in an overwrite, a Try-this (`canonical`) or with `opts.scene === false`. The primary is re-validated (floors,
flood fill) with the fillers' tiles blocking. The primary's own build is unchanged (the placement draw is still consumed, so every later roll
stays put). Room records: the primary and each filler get a `room.surprises` entry (`filler: true`), still one undo step per press.
**Recency now counts presses** (`surpriseRecentPresses`): a press pushes its primary and its fillers; none of them returns within 5 presses.
`SURPRISE_CATEGORY_WEIGHTS` room 30 / loop 30 / vignette 25 / toy 10 / nonsense 5 (room was 40; whole-room templates cannot take fillers).
Strips are already full width (rink, treadmill) or full height (tornado), so "stretch" is only the floor-side rule; no template was widened.

**Fixed inventory (`surpriseFixedInventory`).** The kit no longer reads the live project: skins are `GROUND_SEEDS` ids, pets/chasers are the
factory wanderer and chaser defs, rewards are the factory collectibles minus Heart/Key/Star. Before this, a loaded sample's `exCat` chaser,
its textures and a kid's custom characters could be cast in a surprise. `tests/scenes.test.js` section A checks every `actor|`/`raw|` piece in
every template and scenes after loading Rock Bottom, Crystal Cave Runner and The Ice Cave.

**Tests:** `node tests/scenes.test.js [structSeeds] [fuzzPerTemplate] [all|inv|struct|press|fuzz]` (inventory, 1000-seed structure rules,
real presses with one-undo/one-player, and a Play fuzz comparing each small primary alone vs with fillers using `tests/causal.hook.js`, which
now records the cell of an event's subject so a filler's own chain cannot make the primary look fine). Fuzz result: 120/120 chain-passing
primaries kept their chain and best mechanism path with fillers. `tests/surprise.test.js` canonical check whitelists `movingplatform` (gravity1).

## Git / commit conventions

- One file changes almost every commit: `index.html`. Commit messages so
  far explain the **why** for each feature at some length (see `git log`)
  — matches this project's own principle of design rationale mattering
  more than mechanical description. Keep that style.
- No PRs opened unless explicitly asked; work happens directly on the
  designated feature branch per the session's instructions.
- Nothing else in the repo needs updating for a typical feature patch —
  no lockfile, no build artifacts, no test files (those are scratchpad-only
  and never committed).


## Princess recolored (less Peach)

`PRINCESS_ART` redrawn: dress in `#8cc5e8` with `#3b8bbd` shading and `#badef5` hem, a `#badef5` tiara with a `#3b8bbd` center gem across the hair (replacing the blue hair gems), and a two-tone diamond (`#badef5` over `#3b8bbd`) on her chest. Hair and face unchanged. The preset only copies art onto a def (and Rock Bottom's `rbHero` copies it at build time), so no stale-art migration is needed; already-applied Princess characters in saved projects keep their old pink look. "Pink blob" (`PRINCESS_POSES`) is untouched. Only checked by a load/render pass, not a visual review in-app.

## "Pink blob" becomes Derpy (cupcake)

The old pink-blob preset (`PRINCESS_POSES`/`PRINCESS_BODY`/`PRINCESS_LEGEND`, a Kirby-like look) is deleted. In its slot (right after Hero, before ghost) is **Derpy**, a flat single-pose cupcake (`CUPCAKE_ART`, user-supplied `pat()` art used verbatim). Preset names are tooltips only, so no migration; characters that already had the old blob applied keep their stored art.

## Sprite editor: the game's own sprites are preloaded

`prototypes/sprite-editor.html` now opens with the original 24 extracted sprites **plus 109 sprites taken from the game itself** (`GAME_SPRITES`, embedded right after `PAYLOAD`): character presets (poses become frames), collectibles, objects/carried kinds, tile and fixture icons, ground/lethal skins (lava/fire frames included), backgrounds, movers, the remaining named `*_ART`/`*_FRAMES` constants, and the custom art inside the six embedded sample projects. Categories are prefixed "Game ·". Duplicates (identical art) and flat one-colour squares are skipped, so e.g. Hero appears once. A sidebar filter box searches label and category.

They have no reference image ("taken from the game"). Editing, dirty tracking, Reset (`originalFrames(id)`, which replaced direct `ORIGINAL_ART` reads), Save/Load JSON and Export as JS all work the same; Export as JS only emits edited ones, named from the label (the "Other art" labels are the real constant names). The data was extracted once with a throwaway Playwright script against a hooked copy of `index.html` and is a **snapshot**: new art added to the game later is not picked up automatically — re-extract to refresh. The editor's palette is still the sheet's; use "+" for game colours not on it.

## Princess, second pass

`PRINCESS_ART` replaced with the user's edited version from the sprite editor (used verbatim): yellow hair with a green gem at the crown, `#66d6ff` dress, `#3b8bbd` waist. Supersedes the "Princess recolored" section's palette. Rock Bottom's "Princess Pip" picks it up automatically; Princess characters already applied in saved projects keep their old art. The sprite editor's preloaded "Princess" still holds the previous art (it is a snapshot).

## "The Long Way Out" — a chain-reaction contraption sample (one room, no engine changes)

`buildLongWayOutGame()` / `drawLongWayOutIcon`, registered last in `SAMPLE_GAMES`. One fixed 24x18 room, gravity on, no wrap, nothing
that hurts. The inventor is shut in a booth (interior cols 20-22, rows 14-16) with ONE Switch under a painted sign (DO NOT PUSH, the only
text). Everything after that flip is the machine running itself. **No new pieces, rules or engine behaviour**: every part is an existing
mechanic; the belt/plank/floor skins and every background tile are sample-scoped defs added inside the builder. Phase 0 prototypes and
numbers are in `prototypes/long-way-out-handoffs.js` (50 runs per hand-off); the reliability suite is `tests/longwayout.test.js [runs]`
(53 checks). Screenshots: `prototypes/long-way-out-shots/`.

**The machine (times from the first Switch flip, 60 fps, identical in all 100 runs):**
| t (s) | what happens |
|---|---|
| 0 | Switch (red) opens Gate A; crate 1, held against it as a wall on the top belt, is free at 0.25 |
| 0.25-21.9 | five conveyor legs, each about 20 tiles and 4.2 s, crate 1 drops from leg to leg (4.1, 8.4, 12.8, 17.1) and lands on Plate P1 (blue) at 21.87 |
| 21.87 | P1 opens the blue Gate: the Large Stone falls onto Plate P2 (green) at 22.77 |
| 22.77 | P2 opens the green Gate: the bomb falls 9 rows, detonates on the blast-proof ledge and clears the diggable cork (23.40) |
| 23.4 | crate 2 falls onto the cannon, loads, fires (23.93) and lands on Switch 2 (yellow) at 25.03 |
| 25.03 | Switch 2 opens Gate D: crate 3, held against it on the lower right belt, rides three belts, then a fan pushes it along waxed (slippery) floor onto the last Plate (purple) at 31.80 |
| 31.80 | the purple Plate opens the booth Gate; the player walks out to the goal (win at about 32.1) |

**Mechanism use (honest count)**: belt x9 legs, Gate x5 (A, blue, green, D, booth), Plate x3, Switch x2, crate x3, plus one each of
Large Stone, bomb, diggable, indestructible, cannon, fan, slippery. Belts, Gates and Plates are used far more than twice: the
brief's "none more than twice" cannot be met together with "35-40 s", because conveyors (5 tiles/s for crates, stone and bombs alike, measured)
are the only slow piece. **Duration is 31.8 s to the Gate, not 35-40**; the room has no more space for belts (the cannon arc clears the
rack by about 37 px and reaches rows 12-16 across cols 1-9, the booth and goal nook take the bottom right).

**Cut from the approved list, with reasons:** Trampoline (a crate bounces straight up about 4 tiles, airborne friction kills any sideways
speed, and its only use re-flips a Switch on re-entry), Magnet and Black Hole (no free floor space once the rack is long enough; a
magnet is always on and would also pull crate 1 or crate 2 off their plates). Movers were not used (sample-only).

**Techniques worth reusing**
- *A Gate as a wall*: a crate on a belt, pressed against a closed barrier in its own row, sits perfectly still until the channel flips,
  then rides the belt. It gives a long first leg without any timer and makes the start unambiguous.
- *Stacked belts at 2-row spacing*: a crate rides the row above its belt, so legs can be 2 rows apart (10 px clearance). A belt end drops
  the crate at the next tile column (airborne friction kills its speed), so each leg starts exactly one column past the previous end.
- *Mirrored for space*: stone, bomb and cannon live in the left 3 columns; the booth is bottom right, so the rack ends next to them.
- A belt tile can carry skin art with the matching `wind-e`/`wind-w` modifier (animated chevrons at `frameMs` 50 match the 160 px/s belt
  speed), which avoids the mismatched-modifier tint.

**Rule one (gated exit), proven by trying:** the booth is closed by a 3-tile Gate, a roof and walls. Idle for 60 s: nothing moves, no win.
Mashing every key (jump, E, direction) for 60 s without ever touching the Switch side: the player never leaves, never grabs, never wins.
After the flip, mashing keys for the whole run: the player never leaves the booth before the purple Plate (x >= 640 px), is never within
grab range of a piece (closest 118-155 px, range 41.6), and every beat time is identical to the unmashed run. 20 entries on the Switch in
5 s give 4 flips (the engine's 500 ms flip cooldown) and the finale time is unchanged: Gate A is clear of crate 1 before a second flip is
possible, and a Gate that re-closes on a crate either leaves it on top (closed within 233 ms) or the crate is already through.

**Reliability numbers**: 100 of 100 runs win; every beat has min = median = max (zero spread); no `Math.random` calls in a run (no random
element anywhere; no spawners). Frame rate: 30 fps within 0.05 s of 60 fps, 120 fps +0.14 s, 144 fps +0.13 s (the finale is later by
two frames, the final state is identical, all win). 90 s idle after the finale: no errors, no wins, nothing drifts. Standalone export: runs
the chain once and wins at 31.80 s with no errors. Longest gap between discrete events is a rack leg, about 4.2 s of continuous visible
motion (animated belt, riding crate).

**Two things found, one fixed, one reported**
- *Fixed*: `loadExampleGame` never cleared `switchChannels`, so a Switch left on in a previous project leaked into a freshly
  loaded sample (this machine would start by itself, and an export carried it too; every sample with Switches had the same latent
  leak). `loadExampleGame` now resets `switchChannels = {}` alongside `doorLinks`; verified by turning Switches on, loading Cheese Heist
  and this sample, and reading `{}` both times.
- *Reported*: in Build mode, "Reset to start" restores every piece, tile and the blast (checked identical) but leaves Switch positions as
  they were, because Switch state is authored data. Play restart returns everything, Switches included (checked identical). So after a run
  in Build a kid must click the two Switches back (or just press Play).

**Starting with no input**: possible as a one-shot. If the first Switch's starting state is saved on (Build-mode click), Gate A is open at
Play start and the whole chain runs after the READY pause (finale at 31.97 s, verified). It is not a loop: the machine is single-use.

## Magnets are switchable, and hold what they touch

Request (from a bomb riding a belt into a magnet in the user's Long Way Out extension and sliding straight past it): make the
magnet activatable by Switches/Plates, and let it keep a bomb until released.

- **Switchable like the Fan.** `magnet` joined `isColorTaggable`. No color = always on (every old save and sample unchanged); a color
  = pulls only while that channel is on (`magnetActiveAt`, checked per magnet tile in `radialForceAccelFor`). The normal object color
  picker and corner badge appear for it; a switched-off colored magnet draws at 40% alpha. A *carried* magnet actor has no color and
  stays always-on (the tag is not copied across a grab; revisit if wanted).
- **Why the bomb slipped past.** `MAGNET_FORCE` peaks at 900, under gravity (1450), so in a gravity room a magnet could only nudge
  an object sideways, never catch it (traced: belt-end bomb drifted down the magnet's face and fell). New rule, `magnetHoldsActor`:
  a physics object (`isForceSusceptible`, non-player, so Large Stone and characters are not held) touching a live magnet tile
  (within 2px) in a gravity room is frozen with gravity suspended, using the sticky-cling `stuck` branch in `stepActor`. It drops at
  once (no eased release) when the magnet's channel goes off. Held bombs keep their fuse/fall rules; a free-roam room is unaffected.
- This changes untagged magnets too: anything light that touches one now sticks instead of sliding off (design call, flag if unwanted).
- Export: `magnetActiveAt`/`magnetHoldsActor` in `runtimeFunctions`. Caption now "Pulls stuff your way, and hangs on to it."
- Tests: `tests/magnet.test.js` (untagged holds, colored+on holds, switching off drops, colored+off does not hold); verified on the
  user's `my-project.json` (untagged holds the belt bomb; red-off lets it pass; red-on holds, off drops). Long Way Out suite still 53/53.

## "The Over-Engineered Escape" replaces "The Long Way Out" (user-authored, durability-tested)

`OVER_ENGINEERED_PROJECT` embeds the user's own contraption (`my-project_5.json`), loaded via `restoreProjectSnapshot` like the other user-authored samples; `buildOverEngineeredEscapeGame()` / `drawOverEngineeredIcon`. The coded Long Way Out builder, its icon and `tests/longwayout.test.js` are gone, so the Long Way Out section above (and `prototypes/long-way-out-*`) is history. Actors reset to spawn, spawner state stripped.

**Verified solvable:** a clock-driven recipe at 60 fps (9 macros) ends in a win, also after lingering on the roof; the cascade and booth opening work at 30/60/90/144 fps. Climbing out of the booth works at 60/90/144 fps only (impossible at 30 fps: known limit). **Verified gated:** 0 wins in ~210 random-input runs. Suite: `node tests/overengineered.test.js [fuzzRuns]` (63 checks, includes a 12-sample load/play sweep).

**Engine fixes found by fuzzing this level (shared code):**
- Delayed removals (popVanish, bomb blast, collect) now use `removeActorObject(obj)` (by identity) instead of `removeActorById`: a stale timer could delete a different actor that reused the id. Added to `runtimeFunctions`.
- `dropCarriedObject` tries in-front, behind, then above the player, rejecting any spot overlapping tiles or the player; if none fits the object stays carried ("No room to put it down here."). Fixes a crate dropped on/into the player that was used to tunnel out of the booth.
- `shovePushables` seats via `seatX`/`seatY`, which revert if the result would overlap tiles (no ping-pong/tunnelling through walls).
- `keepPlayerOnBoard`: the embed counter decays on clean frames instead of resetting only on a perfect run.
- Fixed a `ReferenceError` (`radiusPx` -> `magnetRadiusPx`) in the carried-magnet branch.

**Known limit:** an exported game cannot restart without reloading the page.

## Editor first screen fits the viewport (v2.47, Stage 1; presentation only)

**Cause of the canvas dropping below the palette:** `#gameWrap` sized to its widest child (the 1027px build toolbar and log), so palette (260) + game (about 1051) + gaps needed about 1360px; below that `#app`'s `flex-wrap` stacked the game under a palette that was itself about 1065px tall. Before: canvas top 1258 at 1280x720 and 1180x820 (bottom 1834), top 177 at 1366x768/1440x900/1920x1080 with the page scrolling (scrollHeight 1147-1870).

**Now:** `body` is a viewport-high flex column (`100dvh`, no page scroll); `#app` is a non-wrapping row; `#paletteWrap` is a 280px column with its own `overflow-y: auto`; `#gameWrap` is `min-width: 0` with centred, wrapping toolbars; the top bar stays put. `fitCanvas()` (after "Canvas setup"; window resize plus a ResizeObserver on `#app`/toolbars/log, deferred with `setTimeout` because a synchronous observer callback tripped "ResizeObserver loop" into the fatal error overlay) sets the canvas CSS size: uniform 4:3, at most native 774px incl. border, min 320. The 768x576 backing store and pointer math (`getBoundingClientRect` scaling) are untouched, `image-rendering: pixelated` stays. Under 760px wide the layout stacks and the page scrolls normally (stacked fallback, not tuned). `#placeTabs` is single-line (`nowrap`, auto-width tabs); `.swatch .lbl` is clipped with an ellipsis (long names like "large stone" are truncated, the caption bar/title still say the full name).

After (canvas top/bottom, size): 1280x720 212/694 640x482; 1366x768 175/742 754x567; 1180x820 212/794 774x582; 1440x900 and 1920x1080 175/757 774x582 (same size as before at those widths). Play mode canvas at 1280x720 is 747px (was 768); the standalone export is byte-for-byte unchanged in layout (774x582 at 1280x720, same as before). No gameplay, rules or export code changed. Test: `node tests/layout.test.js` (35 checks: bounding box inside viewport, 4:3, top bar visible, tabs on one row, a real click paints the cell under the cursor at the scaled size, scrollY 0, no errors). Stage 2 (landing screen) not started.

## Front door: landing screen, Home, page identity (v2.47, Stage 2)

**What it is.** A state of `index.html` (`body.landing`, `#landing`), not a separate page. First visit (no autosave and no URL hash) and the new 🏠 Home button show it. Layout: wordmark (`#landingWordmark`, one replaceable element), tagline, **Play a game / Make one / Surprise me**, "Now playing: <sample> · Take it apart ↗", trust line, key hint, Privacy · Contact, "Best on a computer or Chromebook". Below 700px the buttons stack. Real `<button>`s in that order, visible focus, Enter works, Escape does nothing.

**The backdrop is the real engine.** `showLanding()` holds the real project aside (`landingSaved`: snapshot + undo/redo stacks), loads a random `LANDING_HEROES` sample with `loadExampleGame(..., { skipConfirm: true })`, `enterPlayMode()` with the READY! pause skipped, and the canvas is restyled (CSS only) to cover the viewport at 4:3 under a dim wash. While `landingActive`: `claimVoice` returns 0 (no sound), game keydown handlers return early (no input, Space/Enter keep working on buttons), `saveAutosave` returns (visiting never writes the project or autosave), and the panel covers the canvas (no clicks reach it). `prefers-reduced-motion` sets `landingFrozen` after the first frame (`frame()` skips `tick`). Hidden tabs pause via `requestAnimationFrame` itself (and `dt` is clamped).
`endLanding(restore)`: restore=true (Make one, Surprise me, Home round trip) puts the held project back via `restoreProjectSnapshot`, restores the undo stacks; false (Take it apart, picking a sample) keeps what is loaded. If the held project has work (`landingHadWork`, differs from `FACTORY_DEFAULT_SNAPSHOT`), replacing it asks the usual `confirmReplaceProject` first. **Play a game** opens the Samples gallery over the landing screen; picking one loads it and starts it in Play mode (cancel returns to the landing screen). **Make one** from Home returns to the current project (empty on a first visit). Boot: `if (!lastAutosaveJSON && !bootHash) showLanding()`.

**Heroes** (`LANDING_HEROES`, chosen from 12 samples run 20 s, no input, 1280x720, real time; 65 s for the finalists): accepted **Crossing Guard** (30/30 s with moving non-player actors, 65 s alive, 3 lives), **The Gravity Switcheroo** (22/30 s, spawner balls, alive 65 s x3 runs), **Dungeon Diving** (26/30 s, lava/fire animation, one wanderer, alive 65 s x3 runs). Rejected: The Cheese Heist and Tunnel Trouble (the chaser kills the player within 20 s: game over), Sushi Train Panic (player rides the belt into the fire pit: game over), The Ice Cave (lost 2 lives), The Bomb Factory (settles: 1/30 s with motion, 3 distinct frames in 65 s), The Over-Engineered Escape (needs the first Switch; only belt skins animate, 1/30 s actor motion), Rock Bottom and The World's Worst Elevator (static, 1 distinct frame), The Monster's Den (static until the Switch is flipped; 3 frames).

**Page identity.** `APP_NAME` ("TinyGameMaker", defined next to `landingActive`) feeds the `<h1>`, the wordmark, `document.title` and the error message; the static `<head>` tags (`<title>`, description, Open Graph, Twitter) are copies for crawlers (they don't run JS), so a rename is `APP_NAME` plus those tags. Title: "TinyGameMaker — a toy for making games". Favicon: inline SVG data URI (a crate). **Placeholders the user must fill:** `PRIVACY_URL` and `CONTACT_URL` constants (empty: the footer links do nothing yet, title says so), `og:image` and `twitter:image` = `og-image.png` (not a real file), no `og:url`. **Old strings replaced:** `<title>Toy Game-Maker</title>`, the `<h1>Toy Game-Maker</h1>`, the console error "Toy Game-Maker hit an unexpected error", and the export `<title>My Game</title>` (now `My Game — made with ${APP_NAME}`; projects have no name field, so the export title uses the generic game name). The key hint is **new wording** ("Arrow keys or WASD to move · Space to jump · E to grab and throw"): the game had no existing key text. Autosave key `toyGameDesigner.autosave.v1` is deliberately unchanged.

**Export.** No landing markup or code reaches the export; the template declares `let landingActive = false, landingFrozen = false;` because the shared `claimVoice` and `frame` read them. Sample entries gained `id` fields (slugs) so heroes are a list of ids. All existing `tests/*.test.js` now open with `#t` appended so a fresh profile skips the landing screen.

**Test:** `node tests/landing.test.js [shotDir]` (43 checks: first visit at 4 viewports with no overflow, button/keyboard behavior, Take it apart, byte-for-byte project and autosave across Home -> Make one, deep links, reduced motion, 20 s per hero, zero network requests, export title/no landing/plays). No gameplay, engine or asset data changed.

## Cannon landing: control returns on touchdown (Over-Engineered Escape's belt)

Reported: after the trampoline -> upper-left cannon shot, the player lands on the top conveyor and is carried off the west end before they can walk or jump. Traced in the real level (`/tmp` harness, brown/green/blue gates open): the shot lands at x=125 (the west end of the belt, which starts at x=128), about 0.35 s after firing, with `cannonCooldown` still at 450 ms. **There is no jump delay**: a grounded jump fires the same frame (`jumpCrouch` is only a squash). The delay was the cannon's 800 ms `cannonCooldown` (there to stop an instant re-trigger), which also blocked all movement input, so ~0.45 s of dead controls after landing; the belt reverses a landed player within ~0.2 s and drops them off the end at ~0.43 s (frame 80 vs landing frame 54).

Fix (gravity rooms only): input is locked only while the shot is in flight (`ballistic`, which already clears two frames after touchdown), via `shotLocksInput` in `stepActor`. Gravity-off rooms keep the cooldown lock (their `ballistic` ends at once). Un-locking alone exposed the v2.20.1 shape (`cannonCooldown` also counts in `recentlyLaunched`, which skips the speed cap, so holding Right integrated to |vx| 583-1283), so `recentlyLaunched` now counts `cannonCooldown` only when there is no gravity or the actor is not the player (crates/bombs keep the old exemption: a first version that changed it for every actor shifted the Escape's cascade timing and failed the 144 fps booth checks). Verified: after landing, holding Right gives vx 220 (the cap) within 3 frames and walks out against the belt; Right + jump-mash stays capped; the arc is unchanged by input held during flight; the old code fails the three landing checks (control after 6 frames, max |vx| 793). Not changed: the level itself. The shot lands at the very west end of a belt that moves west at 220 px/s, so a player who reacts slower than about 0.4 s after touchdown still gets carried off; and with the brown gate (4,0) closed the shot hits it and never reaches the belt. Test: `node tests/cannonlanding.test.js` (8 checks). Also noted: `tests/movers.test.js` "palette placement" now fails (y 98.98 vs 96) with or without this change (it clicks the scaled canvas); not investigated.

Landing now matches the mock: left-aligned column over the live backdrop with a left-to-right dark gradient, wordmark in alternating colours (`APP_NAME` split on capitals; odd words get class `.w`, white), three stacked equal-width icon buttons (yellow filled "Play a game" ▶, "Make one" ✎, "Surprise me" 🎲), the trust line, a top-right "Now playing · Take it apart ↗" pill, bottom-left footer, bottom-right "Best on a computer or Chromebook" and a keycap-style key hint. Under 700px the gradient becomes a flat wash and the key hint is hidden. Tab order is unchanged except Take it apart now follows the buttons from the top-right pill (same sequence the test asserts). **Credits**: the link exists (`#landingCredits`, `hidden`) and is only shown when `CREDITS` is non-empty (it is empty today), so it does not appear yet.

Editor top bar: 🏠 Home is gone; the site name (`#homeLogo`, same two-tone treatment) sits at the top left and calls `showLanding`. Build/Play are larger (18px, 100px min width); a 48px gap separates them from Samples + a bigger 🎲 (32px), and the control cluster is weighted right. Under 760px the bar wraps (logo, then Build/Play full width, then Samples + 🎲); the media rule must come after the base rules or they override it. The palette `<h1>` is kept hidden. No gameplay, engine, or export change. Tests: `landing.test.js` (43) and `layout.test.js` (35) pass.
The top bar is capped at 1280px wide and centered, so wide monitors get the same logo/controls spacing as a 1280px window.

## Selection highlight and Surprise player look (editor only)

Collectible and mover swatches (Objects tab) now only show as selected while `paintMode === "actor"`; before, `selectedActorKind` stayed on the last collectible after picking a tile object, so it stayed highlighted (crate/stone already had this guard). Try this reads `paintMode` first, so it was already reading the tile piece; the stale highlight was what looked like two selections. Characters-tab swatches keep their old tab-memory highlight. Surprise Me / Try this: when a surprise places the project's first player (none exists anywhere), `surpriseRandomizePlayerLook(seed)` gives the player def one of Gary/Princess/Hero/Derpy/ghost/monster/skeleton/slime (seeded, so the same seed gives the same look), but only if the player def's art is still the factory default; a kid's own repaint is never replaced. If a player already exists nothing changes. Tests: `tests/surprise.test.js`, `layout.test.js` pass.

## v2.48 Stage 1: first-ten-minutes polish (landing, starter board, no-player hint, drag-start fix, key-hint pill)

1. **Landing buttons.** Order is Make one (primary, accent fill, default focus), Surprise me, Play a game. Now playing / Take it apart is unchanged.
2. **Starter board.** "Make one" from a blank held project (`!landingHadWork`) calls `loadStarterBoard()`: a floor on the bottom row, the player near the left, a goal near the right, gravity on. It is ordinary content (one undo step); Clear board and New project still give an empty board. A project that already has work is never touched.
3. **Play with no player** no longer fails silently: the caption line says "Place a player first." and the player palette row flashes (`.flashHint`). No modal.
4. **"Set player start here" is removed** (`setStartBtn`). Real bug behind it: dragging the player only updated `spawnX/spawnY`, not `startPos` or `playerRoomId`, so Reset and save/reload put the player back at the old spot. Both drag-end handlers (mouse and touch) now set `startPos` and `playerRoomId` when the dragged actor is the player.
5. **Key-hint pill** (`keyHintText`/`drawKeyHint`, drawn on the canvas, so it is shared with the standalone export): arrows/WASD always; Space jump only if the room has gravity; E grab only if the room has something carryable; follows room changes; fades a few seconds after the first key press and stays hidden for the session (reset on Play start via `keyHintFirstKeyAt`). Level resets after a death do not re-show it. Export: functions in `runtimeFunctions`, globals declared in the template.

Tests: `tests/stage1.test.js` (36 checks), `landing.test.js` (43), `layout.test.js` (35), `surprise.test.js` pass; `toyDebug` count 0. Stages 2 to 4 (vertical space, Pause, art cache) are not started.

Landing button text is now "Make a game" (id `landingMakeBtn` unchanged).

**What changed.** The Score line, room tabs, camera pan controls and the message line used to be four rows above/below the canvas; they are now ONE strip, `#statusRow` (left to right: room tabs + Duplicate Room, pan controls when the camera isn't Fixed, `#log`, `#hud`, `#autosaveHint`). The build toolbar stays one row of 8 buttons. In Play the tabs/toolbar are hidden, so the strip shows just the message and "Lives n ♥ Score: n" on one line. Paddings were tightened (`#app` 4/12/8, `#gameWrap` 4/8, gap 4, top bar margin 6) and `#gameWrap .tbtn` padding is 4px 10px (rows are 25px, were 29). No gameplay, no export change (the export has its own template).

- **Room tabs scroll sideways** inside their strip (hidden scrollbar so it adds no height; the vertical wheel is mapped to horizontal; the active tab is scrolled into view) instead of wrapping to a second row. With very many rooms the extra tabs are reached by wheel/drag, not by a scrollbar.

| 1280x590 | 0.689 | 0.779 |
| 1366x635 | 0.767 | 0.857 |

Compared screenshots of Dungeon Diving at 0.857 free vs 0.75 snapped (`/tmp/claude-0/shots4`): 0.75 is crisper (art pixels are exactly 3px) but 12% smaller; the free scale shows slightly uneven brick lines (3px/4px art pixels) that are not distracting at this size. `fitCanvas()` is unchanged (it already sized freely); a snap would be one `Math.floor(w/ (768*0.25))` style line if the uneven pixels bother anyone.

Stages 3 (Pause) and 4 (art cache) are not started.

## v2.48 Stage 3: Pause (a game clock, not per-system pause code)

**Design.** Pause is "invisible to the simulation by construction". All game time now comes from one clock, `gameClockMs` (`gameNow()`), which only advances inside `tick(dt)` and inside the frozen-state branches of `frame()` that deliberately keep delayed reactions running (room transition, win/game-over screen, READY!). `frame()` gets one more frozen branch, `gamePaused`: `tick()` does not run, the clock stands still, `renderGame()` still draws (so edits show). Every `performance.now()` that used to mean game time is now `gameNow()` (39 sites), and every game-time `setTimeout`/`clearTimeout` is `gameSetTimeout`/`gameClearTimeout` (timers live in `gameTimers`, fire from `advanceGameClock`, are cleared by `clearTransientRuntimeState`). A bomb fuse therefore holds its remaining time while paused with no special code; `Math.max(0, ...)` guards the two elapsed-time subtractions and the `safeUntil` window. `dt` is clamped to >= 0 in `frame()` (a negative first-frame dt, rAF timestamp behind `performance.now()`, ran the clock backwards and broke animated-skin lookup).

**Audit of every time use** (sim = affects the simulation):

| use | kind | now |
|---|---|---|
| `playReadyUntil`, `roomTransition.startedAt`, `safeUntil`, fuse `armedAt`/`fuseStartedAt`, `hazardCooldown`-style stamps, skin/black-hole/tile animation frames, `RECIPES` anim stamps, spawner clocks | sim / sim-visible | `gameNow()` / dt |
| levelReset (`LEVEL_RESET_DELAY_MS`), round-complete reset, popVanish removals, game-over stinger, bomb tile fuse, carried-bomb fuse, blast removal | sim timers | `gameSetTimeout` |
| `claimVoice` / `activeVoices` | audio throttle only | real `performance.now()` (audio is suspended while paused, and `claimVoice` returns 0) |
| key-hint pill fade (`keyHintFirstKeyAt`, `drawKeyHint`) | UI | real `performance.now()` (fades even while paused) |
| frame loop `lastT` | loop | real |
| autosave `setInterval`, ResizeObserver, `scrollIntoView` | UI | real `setTimeout`/`setInterval` (none touch the simulation) |

**Behaviour.** P (not Space) or the top-bar button (⏸ Pause / ▶ Resume) toggles; ignored on the win/game-over screen and in text fields. Build pause persists across edits and a Play trip (`buildPausedMemory`) but is never saved; Play always starts running; loading a sample and Surprise Me resume; a hidden tab pauses Play only (not Build) and returns still paused. While paused: all simulation and animation stop, audio is suspended (`audioCtx.suspend/resume`, new sounds refused), movement and E are ignored, Build editing (place/erase/drag/paint/rotate/undo/redo) works, Reset to start stays paused. Badge: Build gets only the "⏸ Paused" pill, Play adds a light dim. Hint pill: Build "Ctrl+Z undo · Ctrl+Y redo · P pause"; the Play/export pill ends "· P pause". Export: all functions and globals are shared (`runtimeFunctions`, hand-declared template globals); `restartStandaloneGame` clears timers and unpauses; P and `visibilitychange` are wired in the export template.

**Caveats to know.** (1) "Always live" is not violated for editing, but a paused Build sandbox is frozen by the kid's own choice; it is never entered automatically in Build. (2) Anything that needs *game time* to pass (a manual-tick test that waited on a real `setTimeout`) must now tick frames instead. (3) The Moving/lane math is unaffected: a mover's phase is `moverT`, advanced by dt.

**Tests** (`node tests/pause.test.js [runsPerSample] [shotDir]`, hook-injected temp copy with a fake clock and a manually stepped rAF at an exact 16 ms, `Math.random` reseeded per frame from the game clock; `ONLY=<sampleId>` runs just the invariance loop): the headline test runs Crossing Guard, Rock Bottom, Sushi Train Panic and The Gravity Switcheroo for 625 frames of scripted input with 1-3 pauses of random start and length (1-600 frames, scrambled keys pressed during them) and requires the final state (actors, boards hash, score, lives, channels, camera, game clock) to equal the unpaused run; each run gets a fresh page because state can leak between Play sessions in one page (unrelated to pause). Also: frozen state and canvas identical after 5 s (Build and Play), edit/undo/redo/Reset while paused, Build pause memory, sample/Surprise resume, hidden tab, win/game-over, fuse exactness for a tile bomb with a chain and for a dropped bomb actor (detonation on the same simulated frame whatever the pause), audio suspend/resume/no new voices, a real door transition paused mid-flight, hint pills, and an exported game (P freezes, resumes, hidden tab pauses). **Test-authoring traps found:** a fake `performance.now` that adds `1000/60` makes `t - lastT` round differently depending on absolute time, which flips a mover sitting exactly on a lane boundary between 0 and 576 (same place) - use an exact step; scramble keys must not collide with the scripted keys; a loop bound that includes a decremented counter silently shortens.

**Stage 3 results.** Invariance: 50 runs each of Crossing Guard, Rock Bottom, Sushi Train Panic and The Gravity Switcheroo, each with 1-3 pauses (random start, 1-600 frames, scrambled keys during them): 200/200 final states identical to the unpaused 10 s run (a 625-frame run at 16 ms). Full `pause.test.js` (58 checks incl. fuse exactness, edit/undo/redo while paused, hidden tab, win/game-over, audio, real mid-door-transition pause, Surprise Me, export playthrough): pass. Regression: stage1 36, landing 43, layout 35, stage2 104, deathdig 19, crossingguard 30, rockbottom 48, overengineered 63, cannonlanding 8, bombs, magnet, surprise: pass; movers 36/37 (the one failure, "palette placement", pre-dates this stage). Test changes forced by the game clock: death-reset waits now `advanceGameClock(...)` instead of real `sleep`; export boot anchor text is `gameNow()`; Surprise's "holesoup" spawner check counts spawns ever made (black holes remove spawned items on the game clock now; the old real-time removal timer never fired in manual-tick tests). Not covered: pause while a carried-bomb is held in a door transition; the audio is verified at the AudioContext level, not by listening.

## v2.49: let it breathe on larger screens (layout and spacing only)

Label is informal (v2.48 was the Chromebook polish; this is its mirror image). **Nothing outside layout/spacing changed**: no gameplay, physics, asset, export-runtime or data change; the 768x576 backing store and pointer maths (`getBoundingClientRect` scaling) are untouched.

**1. The canvas grows, in crisp steps.** `fitCanvas` no longer starts from the 774px native size. It measures the largest canvas the stage can hold (`freeFitW`: stage width, and stage height minus the toolbar rows actually on screen), caps it at scale 2.0 (1.75 when the window is >= `FIT_WIDE_PX` 2200 wide so the cluster doesn't spread thin), then `choose()`s: from native size (1.0) up it snaps DOWN to a multiple of 0.25 (every 0.25 is a whole number of screen pixels per art pixel: 5px at 1.25, 6px at 1.5, 7px at 1.75) unless that wastes more than `FIT_SNAP_WASTE` (12%) of the available size, in which case the free fit is kept. **Below native size the free fit is kept on purpose**: a 0.75 snap would shrink 1366x635 from 0.857 to 0.75 and 1280x590 from 0.779 to 0.75, and the brief also demands the small sizes match v2.48 Stage 2 "or better". Because the toolbar can re-wrap when the canvas width changes (it is bounded by `--stageW`, below), the fit re-measures up to 4 times until stable and then has a safety net that steps down until `#gameWrap` stops overflowing. Free scale vs snapped, by screenshot/number: 1366x635 free 0.857 (snapped would be 0.75, -12.5%: kept free); 1920x1080 lands exactly on 1.5 (free fit 1.50 after tightening the comfortable spacing by ~20px; before that it was a free 1.476, snapped 1.25 would have wasted 15%); 1728x1000 Play is a free 1.49 (snapped 1.25 would waste 16%), Build snaps to 1.25.

**2. The comfortable tier** = `@media (min-width: 1500px) and (min-height: 820px)` (both, because height is what binds the canvas; 1440x900 and 1536x735 stay compact, so do wide-but-short windows). Rules: toolbar buttons 36px tall (was 25), 14px text, 12px gaps; `#buildToolbar` and `#statusRow` get `max-width: var(--stageW)` (the canvas width, set by `fitCanvas`), so the 8 toolbar buttons wrap into two relaxed rows when they would crowd (they stay on one row at 2560/3440 where the canvas is 1350 wide); palette column 320px (was 280) with swatches 52px (canvas 40px = a whole 5x of the 8x8 art), preset swatches 44px, big swatches 66px, tabs 12px; top bar max 1760px (was 1280) with a bigger wordmark/buttons; the cluster paddings are tuned so 1920x1080 hits scale 1.5. Measured thresholds were not changed from the brief's 1500 x 820.

**3. Symmetric top bar, Build and Play.** From 1000px wide up `#topBar` is a three-zone grid `1fr | auto | 1fr` (`#topBarControls` is `display: contents`): the wordmark is left, Build/Play is on the page centre, Pause/Samples/dice are right. In Play the palette is hidden and the canvas is page-centred, so the toggle sits right above it and logo/actions mirror each other (before, measured at 1920 in Play: the toggle was centred on x=992 while the canvas was centred on x=960, and the logo/actions sat 340px/436px from the window edges). Below 1000px the old flex bar (and the <760px wrap) is unchanged.

**Results** (`node tests/breathe.test.js [shotDir]`, 260 checks): at 1366x635, 1280x590, 1440x900, 1728x1000, 1920x1080, 2560x1300 and 3440x1440, in Build, Play, Rock Bottom (72 columns, pan buttons), the Samples modal, the in-app pixel editor and the landing screen: canvas fully visible with no page scroll, 4:3, no buttons overlap or touch, no clipped button text, Build/Play centred, logo/actions mirrored, 5-cell pointer accuracy, plus the standalone sprite editor and an export. Canvas width (build scale) before -> after: 1366x635 664 -> 666 (0.857, Play 714/0.92); 1280x590 604 -> 606 (0.78, Play 654); 1440x900 774 -> 966 (1.25); 1728x1000 774 -> 966 Build / 1150 Play (1.25 / 1.49); 1920x1080 774 -> 1158 (1.5); 2560x1300 774 -> 1350 (1.75); 3440x1440 774 -> 1350 (1.75).

**The standalone export does NOT use this fitting**: its page is a fixed 768x576 canvas (774px with border) centred in a flex column, so at 1920x1080 it stays native size with blank margins (no scroll, no errors). It is out of scope here; making it scale the same way (a 10-line `fitCanvas` in the export template) is the obvious follow-up.

## Lethal and harmless NPC characters

Request: "allow wanderer characters to be lethal ... for all NPC characters, the ability to choose lethal and non-lethal; default lethal." Every NPC character def (stationary, wanderer, chaser, fleer: the four built-in ones and every custom character) now carries `def.lethal`. **Lethal** = touching it is exactly the chaser's old "caught" reaction (`triggerNpcCatch`, extracted from `resolveActorContact`): a life is lost and the level restarts, a spare Heart takes the hit instead, a Star or the flicker window protects, the last life is game over, and in Build mode it just sends you back to the start. **Harmless** = the character's own old reaction (stationary boop, wanderer oof, fleer pop and vanish), and a harmless chaser is a plain bump like a wanderer. A lethal NPC is never despawned by touch (the spawner-population pop only applies to harmless ones).

**Defaults and old projects.** New characters start lethal: the four built-in defs are seeded with `lethal: true`, and a "+" custom character too. A def with no `lethal` field (every older save, autosave, embedded sample project and the coded samples' own defs) keeps exactly its old behavior through `npcIsLethal(def)`: only a chaser reads lethal, everything else harmless. So nothing already built changes; a returning project's built-in wanderer reads "harmless" in the picker until the kid clicks "lethal". No migration or snapshot change is needed (`actorDefs` is saved and exported wholesale).

**UI.** A new row in the Characters tab, "If it touches you - <name>", with two swatches, `lethal` and `harmless` (hover captions "Touching it costs a life." / "Touching it is safe."), shown when any NPC character is selected (built-in or custom), hidden for the player and on other tabs. The choice is per character def, not per placed instance, like the voice and art.

**Export.** `npcIsLethal` and `triggerNpcCatch` are in `runtimeFunctions`; `resolveActorContact` was already shared. Verified in a downloaded game.

**Not done / flags.** No visual cue on the sprite marks a lethal NPC (the picker and the log are the only tell). The Surprise Me pets are the built-in wanderer def, so they are lethal for new projects: see the Surprise test notes below if they needed adjusting. Not changed: hazard balls, movers, spawned objects.

Tests: `node tests/lethalnpc.test.js` (31 checks: defaults, all four kinds lethal/harmless/legacy, hearts, star, last life, Build mode, the picker, snapshot round trip, export). Test-authoring trap: restoring the factory snapshot while the palette's remembered selection (`lastObjectActorKind` etc.) points at something that no longer exists makes the next tab click throw; reselect a kind after restoring.

## Half-severed door pairs are healed on load

Reported on a user project (Crystal Cave Runner): "the doors aren't working." Its red pair was room 1 (70,12) and room "room 3" (3,8), but `doorLinks` held only `6:3,8 -> 1` (plus the orange pair): room 1's door had no link at all, so touching it did nothing (`triggerDoor` returns silently without a link), while the far side still led back. Most likely left by deleting/duplicating a room (the project had a "You Survived the Wash! copy"); the exact trigger was not reproduced. `restoreProjectSnapshot` now repairs this: for every link A->B whose target B is still a door tile with no link of its own, B gets the reverse link (an existing link is never overwritten). Verified live on the user's file (before: standing on the door did nothing; after load, it moves to room 3); in-app the same repair is to erase the dead door and repaint it red. `stage1`, `deathdig`, `lethalnpc` still pass. Not done: a message when a link-less door is touched (still silent).

## Paint tiles can animate (frames + frameMs)

Request: add six user-supplied twinkling crystal Paint tiles (6-7 frames each) to a user project and fill its backgrounds. Paint (background) tiles were static `{art, name}`; they may now also carry `frames` (array of 64-colour arts) and `frameMs`. `currentBackgroundArt(def)` (used by `renderRoomContents`, in `runtimeFunctions`) picks the frame from `gameNow()` like an animated ground skin; `art` always holds frame 1, so swatches, the pixel editor and old saves are unchanged (no migration; a def without `frames` draws as before). Redrawing an animated Paint tile in the pixel editor collapses it to the single picture drawn (frames/frameMs deleted), the same rule animated ground skins follow. All tiles on a board share one clock, so they twinkle in step. The built-in palette gained no animated tiles; they exist only in that project's `backgroundTileDefs`. Verified: 12 distinct canvas frames seen over ~1.3 s, export contains the function and frame data and plays with zero errors, `stage1`/`landing` pass.

## Crystal Cave Runner replaces Tunnel Trouble in the gallery (Tunnel Trouble kept, unlisted)

`CRYSTAL_CAVE_PROJECT` embeds the user's own Runner-camera project (rooms "The Red Crystal Cave", "The Blue Crystal Cave", "The Hall of Doors", animated crystal Paint tiles), loaded via `restoreProjectSnapshot` like the other user-authored samples; `buildCrystalCaveRunnerGame()` / `drawCrystalCaveIcon`, registered in Tunnel Trouble's old gallery slot (id `crystal-cave-runner`). Actors reset to spawn; all nine `spawnerContents` keys were stale (no spawner tiles) and dropped.

**Tunnel Trouble is not deleted**: `buildTunnelTroubleGame`, its maze code and icon stay, listed in `UNLISTED_SAMPLES` (not shown in the Samples modal) so the Surprise Me work can reuse them. Verified: gallery lists 12 samples with Crystal Cave Runner, it loads and plays with zero errors; stage1/landing/overengineered pass. Not played through to the end.

## v2.48 Stage 4: art cache (performance only)

`drawArtScaled` used to run 64 `fillRect`s per tile/actor/painted cell per frame (about half of Rock Bottom's frame time). Now each distinct art is rendered once per cell size into an offscreen canvas (`artCacheCanvas`, built by the unchanged per-pixel loop, now `drawArtScaledLive`, including its +0.5px overlap) and blitted with `drawImage`. **Only used where a blit is identical**: whole-pixel dx/dy, integer cell size, `globalAlpha === 1`, no rotate/scale (`artCacheOff > 0` inside the four transformed draw sites: `drawRotatedArt`, the tile-anim recipe loop, `drawMover`, `renderActors`). Everything else (fades, squash/wobble, rotated skins, actors at fractional centres) uses the live loop, so overlays (rings, fuse flicker, arrows, erosion stages) are untouched.

**Staleness is impossible by construction**: an entry keeps a copy of the 64 pixels it was built from and compares on every use (cheap), so an in-place edit to an art array (pixel editor, preset, undo) rebuilds the next frame; no invalidation hooks. Fresh arrays per frame (tinted/recolored art) are matched by content key. The canvas scale is CSS-only (the 768x576 backing store is fixed), so scale changes need no invalidation. Bound: `ART_CACHE_MAX` (6000 entries, ~4.4KB each, ~26MB worst case; the cache resets when full). Measured: all 12 samples loaded in one page = 136 entries / 0.56MB (Rock Bottom alone 101 entries / 0.42MB). Export: `drawArtScaled`, `drawArtScaledLive`, `artCacheCanvas` in `runtimeFunctions`, cache globals hand-declared in the export template.

**One deliberate visible change (needs the user's OK): tiles are placed on whole screen pixels** (`tileOffPx = Math.round(viewOffsetPx)` in `renderRoomContents`, the fan stream and the grid), actors keep their smooth position. A Runner/Follow camera is fractional almost every frame, which defeated the cache for the whole board (Crystal Cave Runner 46 fps, 8.7 at 4x). The effect is tiles stepping 1 screen pixel instead of a fraction, at most 0.5px off from actors; revert by setting `tileOffPx = viewOffsetPx` (and the two other `Math.round` sites).

**Parity** (`tests/artcache.capture.js capture <html> <dir>`, fake clock, 123 frames: every sample in Build at 3 pan positions and in Play at 5 times, forced fractional camera positions, page screenshots, a sprite/ground/paint in-place edit before/after, palette/editor page; compare with PIL on RGB, **not** `getbbox` on RGBA, which looks only at alpha and reports 0 for opaque images). Cache alone: 40 of 123 canvas/page captures differ, **max channel difference 1 (of 255), a few hundred pixels per frame**, only the anti-aliased 1px right/bottom seam pixels (a 4.5px-wide rect's half-covered overspill is composited as premultiplied alpha, double rounding). Not zero; unavoidable without drawing the seam live. Edits show immediately (before/after captures differ and match the pre-change edit render within the same +-1). With the whole-pixel tile placement, frames with a fractional camera differ visibly by sub-pixel tile shifts (large per-pixel differences along tile edges, no layout change).

**fps** (`node tests/artcache.capture.js fps <html> [cpuThrottle]`, 1366x635, Play, holding Right 4s, real time), before -> after (no throttle / 4x throttle): Cheese Heist 59.5/29.3 -> 59.5/41.6; Crystal Cave Runner 47.4/9.6 -> 60/55; Dungeon Diving 60/42 -> 59.8/60; Monster's Den 56.8/10.6 -> 58/57; Worst Elevator 60/27 -> 60/58.5; Ice Cave 53.7/10.9 -> 60/54.7; Bomb Factory 60/38 -> 60/57.8; Gravity Switcheroo 60/44 -> 59.3/60; Sushi Train 60/25 -> 59.8/60; Crossing Guard 57.6/13.1 -> 60/55.8; **Rock Bottom 40/8.3 -> 60/47.6**; Over-Engineered Escape 46.1/9.5 -> 60/51.3. Targets met (every sample >= 55 unthrottled; Rock Bottom and Crossing Guard >= 30 at 4x). Cheese Heist at 4x (41.6) is the lowest; not profiled further since targets are met. Occasional single slow frames (50-70ms) remain in the harness, not examined.

**Time-step cap (report only, unchanged)**: `dt` is clamped to 0.033 s. Below about 30.3 fps the game falls behind real time: 30 fps runs at 99% (game clock 2970ms per 3000ms real), 25 fps at 82%, 20 fps at 66%, 15 fps at 50% (before the cache, a 4x-throttled Rock Bottom at 8 fps ran at ~25% speed). Physics differs slightly by frame rate (semi-implicit integration with per-frame collision): the same cannon-fired crate landed at x=399/395/394/394 px at 144/60/30/20 fps, and the player's jump apex is 130/127/122/122 px at 144/60/30/<=25 fps. The bomb sequence was not re-run at multiple rates. The cap and all physics constants were left alone.

## v2.50 Stage 1: Surprise Me that does something (generator upgrades; the 19 templates reworked)

(Stages 2-4 of the v2.50 brief — new loop/vignette templates, whole-room events + the authoring helper, the interaction-counter fuzz — are NOT done yet.) Surprise Me stays editor-only, deterministic by seed, non-destructive, one undo step; nothing in the engine or export changed.

**Template fields** (`SURPRISE_TEMPLATES`): `category` (loop / vignette / room / toy / nonsense), `room: { gravity: "on"|"off"|"either" (+ native), wrapH/wrapV: true|false|"maybe" (+ wrapHChance) }` (the old `gravity`/`wrapH` fields are gone), `closed`, `stooge` (legend chars of the character in the way), `expect.selfStart`. "on"/"off"/`true`/`false` are REQUIRED: never rolled, never flipped by chaos (the 15% opposite-gravity flip only applies to "either"; "maybe" rolls only when chaos is on, as before). Try-this (`chaos:false`) still builds the plain version: required wrap stays, "maybe" is off. Closed templates declare wrap false (it does nothing in a sealed box).

**Selection.** `surprisePickTemplate`: category by `SURPRISE_CATEGORY_WEIGHTS` (loop 35, vignette 30, room 15, toy 10, nonsense 10; empty categories are left out and the rest renormalize), then a template in it. **Recency**: `surpriseRecent` (editor session only, never saved) = the last 5 template ids; a dice press (no seed given) skips them, and if nothing else is eligible only the least recently used repeats. A press with an EXPLICIT seed (`surpriseMe(4821)`, `#surprise=4821`) never avoids anything, so replays rebuild exactly; `generateSurprise(seed)` itself is independent of the list. Try-this follows the same rule (fresh press avoids, explicit seed doesn't). A whole-room template that doesn't fit goes into a new room tab without asking.

**Measured mix** (1000 chained presses with recency, 19 eligible): loop 25%, vignette 46%, toy 14%, nonsense 15%, room 0% — the 35/30/15/10/10 target cannot be reached yet: there are only 3 loops (pinball, holesoup... Stage 2 adds them) and no room events (Stage 3). Zero repeats within any 5 presses.

**Multi-room templates.** `rooms: [{rows, legend, room}, ...]` with `door:<n>` pieces (exactly two cells per n, in different parts, linked both ways, each pair its own channel color). Part 0 is placed like a normal template; every later part gets a new room tab (centered); the editor stays on part 0; one undo step; floors run per part (a part without the player skips the player checks). Tested with a synthetic 2-room template (rules per room, links both ways, one player, new tabs when the room isn't empty). No shipped template uses it yet.

**Templates reworked** (all 19 were reviewed; the one-third closed cap = 6/19 closed: fanclub, conveyor, orchestra, roulette, allsprites, alltramps, each checked by the harness against its layout): *opened*: pinball (lava sink in the floor, a pet and a crate dropped over the bouncy floor so something bounces within a second — the first ball only spawns at 2.5 s, too late for the 3 s rule), holesoup (spawner moved within the hole's pull, a pet in the pull that loops back to its spawn, a gap in the roof), spawnerwar (a lava pit in front of each spawner so crates never pile up, a pet beside each pit that keeps falling in), zeropinata, chaserparade, blackholewall, conga (a gap in a wall). *Redesigned*: **bombstairs -> bombdrop** ("Bomb Drop", the old Tuesday Bomb Situation name kept): the player starts standing ON a pressure plate (new piece `playerplate`) that opens the gate holding two bombs 7 tiles up; they fall, go off on landing and punch a hole in the floor; 40/40 on fresh pages. The pet stands at the far right, NOT under the drop: any NPC under falling bombs reflects them (`reflectActorsApart`), which resets the >6-tile fall origin so they never go off (2 of 24 fresh-page runs failed that way while the pet stood under them). *Required gravity*: pinball on, holesoup off, chaserparade off, bombdrop on, conveyor on (zero-g broke its belt in chaos). Already had a stooge: fanclub, conveyor (the player rides the belt), chaserparade, magnet; the rest got a pet.

**Self-start** (`tests/surprise2.test.js`, section C; the harness wraps the engine's reaction functions in the temp copy, `tests/surprise.hook.js`, so the counter is debug-only and not in the product/export): every loop and vignette shows an interaction other than "spawn" within 3 simulated seconds, canonical and chaotic, 8 seeds each, fresh page per run: 14/14 templates x modes pass. Interaction events seen so far: bounce, plateOn, gateOpen, explosion, hit, respawn, lava, wrap, cannon, carry, buttonPress, fanPush, beltRide, pull, blackHole, bombArm, spawn.

**Found, not changed (needs a decision):** pets are the built-in wanderer, which is **lethal by default** (v2.x NPC lethal/harmless), so a roaming pet can catch an idle player: in bombdrop runs of 10 s the player lost a life in 4 of 8 fresh-page runs (the level then resets via the checkpoint). It also shows as a 'hit' interaction, which flatters the interaction numbers. Options: Surprise pets harmless (set `lethal:false` on the pet def used, or a separate harmless pet def), keep as is, or only keep pets away from the player's start. Separately: Play sessions in one page can leak transient state into the next (the falling-bomb template exposed it: 7/8 on a shared page, 8/8 on fresh pages), so the Surprise suites use a fresh page per Play run where it matters.

**Also fixed** (it showed up once footprints varied in height): overwriting part of an untouched earlier surprise left a half-built remnant that then counted as the kid's stuff; the overwrite now clears the whole untouched surprise it touches (never one that holds the player).

Tests: `node tests/surprise2.test.js [stage] [mixSeeds]` (225 checks: metadata, closed flags vs layout, stooge, spawner sinks, mix/recency, selfStart, wrap rules, multi-room), `node tests/surprise.test.js [fuzz]` (the v2.45 suite, passes: determinism, non-destructive placement, floors, overwrite, Try-this), stage1/landing pass. The expectations map gained `bombFall`.

## v2.50 Stage 2: pets harmless, chasers lethal, four new loop templates (Surprise Me)

**Casting rule (decided by the user).** In a surprise, a *pet* (`pet`, `pet*`, and anything a spawner is loaded with that is a character) is always harmless and a *chaser* (`chaser` piece) is always lethal. The kid's own character defs are never edited: `surpriseDefFor(defId, role)` gives the surprise its own copy (`sp~pet~<id>` / `sp~chaser~<id>`, a `structuredClone` with only `lethal` set) when the base def's lethality differs, created lazily at apply time (not during generate/validate, so generating stays read-only). `kit.pets` now also includes chaser defs, so a chaser can be cast as a (harmless) pet for variety (60 of 170 pets in 160 presses). The validator's "chaser within 3 tiles of the player" check uses the cast role, so a harmless chaser look-alike no longer trips it (it had made 17 of 1000 `allsprites` seeds fail). This resolves the Stage 1 note that roaming pets were lethal. Clones are ordinary `actorDefs` entries (saved/exported with the project; hidden from the palette).

**New templates (all `category: "loop"`, native rules required, selfStart true):**
- **Lemming Parade** (`lemmings`; gravity on, wrapH+wrapV): wanderers on a ledge walk off, fall through the floorless room, wrap to the top. Verified that wanderers do walk off ledges: first wrap at frame 20-152 in 16 of 16 canonical/chaos runs, 18-32 wraps in 10 s.
- **Plinko Forever** (`plinko`; gravity on, wrapV): a spawner over a staggered peg field of bouncy tiles, a crate pre-placed over a peg (first bounce at frame 19; the first spawn only comes at 2.5 s), the player in a roofed nook beside the field. About 25 bounces in 10 s; `SPAWNER_MAX_LIVE` bounds the population.
- **Falling Forever** (`fallforever`; gravity on, wrapV): no floor; the player starts at the top with a pet, crates and two floating trampolines. First wrap at frame 17-48.
- **Skating Rink** (`rink`; gravity on, **wrapH false**): a full-width ice floor, a cannon with a crate above it (the crate drops in, the cannon fires at frame ~33 and the crate slides), pets and the player on the ice. Needs `slippery` + `cannon`.

**Skipped, with reasons (both resolved in the Stage 3 round below):** Treadmill needed a sideways-wrap engine fix; Crate Tornado needed wind tiles instead of fans. Original findings kept in the next section.

**Tests.** `tests/surprise2.test.js` gained section E (160 presses: every pet harmless, every chaser lethal, the kid's own def untouched) and section F (per new template, canonical and chaos, 6 fresh pages each: expectation count within 10 s and no player hit by pets). The generic selfStart (first non-spawn interaction in 180 frames) already covers the new loops. Screenshots of each template in motion: `/tmp/claude-0/s2/shots/` (not committed).


## v2.50 Stage 3: sideways wrap fixed, Treadmill and Crate Tornado built, whole-room events and the authoring helper (Surprise Me)

**Sideways wrap carries the overshoot (engine change, approved).** `applyWorldWrap` used to reset an actor that fully exited a wrapped side horizontally to `x = -w` / `worldWidth`, i.e. fully off the board with no floor under it, so anything belt- or ice-carried fell out at the seam (and a player there flip-flopped 768 / -22 every frame). Horizontal wrap now adds/subtracts the world width (`x += W` / `x -= W`) for every actor, the same rule the mover-rider wrap already used; the vertical axis is deliberately untouched (falling already worked and several samples' traced timings depend on it). No shipped sample enables sideways wrap (checked: every embedded project and coded sample has it off, Crossing Guard is vertical only), so no existing game changes; only a kid's own project with H-wrap on, and the Surprise templates. Verified: a belt-riding crate/pet/player crosses the seam and keeps riding (Treadmill: 70-190 wraps in 10 s), Skating Rink slides across it.

**Templates changed/added from Stage 2:** Skating Rink is `wrapH: true` again. **Treadmill** (`treadmill`, loop, gravity on, wrapH, a full-width belt with crates, a pet and the player riding): the bomb from the brief was dropped (a shove-armed bomb punched a hole in the belt within 5-10 s and ended the loop) and so was the reward (collecting the only collectible wins the session in Play). **Crate Tornado** (`tornado`, loop, gravity off, wrapV, a 3-wide, 18-tall column of `none:wind-n` tiles, new piece `gust:<dir>`): crates and a pet rise at the 160 px/s non-player cap, leave through the ceiling and come back up from the floor; wrap 1-3 times in 10 s. Wind tiles instead of fans were the user's decision (a fan only reaches 5 tiles and blocks its own cell, so fans can't be chained).

**New pieces in the template format (no engine behavior):** `gust:<dir>` (open-air wind), `goal`, `warp`, `playerwarp` (player standing on a warp tile), `spawnerh:<dir>` (always the plain hazard ball), `walker` (a pet that is a real wanderer), `plate/gate/button:<color>` (an explicit channel colour, else the template's soft colour), and the catch-alls `raw|<tile key>` / `actor|<def id>` that the serializer writes for anything without its own piece name.

**Whole-room events.** `SURPRISE_ROOMS` (one block in `index.html`, merged into `SURPRISE_TEMPLATES` so every rule — requires, floors, closed/stooge/selfStart checks, recency, Try-this — applies unchanged) holds 24 x 18 entries. A footprint that is the whole room only fits an empty room; otherwise the existing "category room doesn't fit" path puts it in a new room tab and never touches existing content (tested). The piece-count bound went from 160 to 480 for them. Multi-room entries use `rooms: [...]` + `door:<n>` as before.

**The authoring helper (dev-only):** `surpriseSerialize()` in the console serializes the current room (`"all"` = every room, door pairs numbered) into an entry: row strings + legend using the soft slots (player, pets, rewards, crates, walls = wall skin slot, `plate/gate/button` with explicit colours), the room rules, `closed`, `stooge`, and `notes` for anything lossy (a custom spawner payload, a specific wall skin, a camera other than Fixed). It logs the entry as JSON and returns it; not in the UI, not in the export (verified: an exported file contains none of it). **Round-trip tests pass:** a hand-built room (colours, fan, cannon, trampoline, belts, wind, diggable, switches, goal, spawner, warp, four actors) serializes and loads back identical (board, colour layer, actor cells and kinds, rules), and Tunnel Trouble (two rooms, one door pair) loads back with identical boards and both links. `opts.mirror` (test-only) forces the left-right mirror off for these comparisons.

**The thirteen mini-levels** (`Check before building` answered by tracing, not reading):
1. **The Pinball Chaos Trap** (`pinballroom`, room, gravity on, wrapV): the brief's "H-wrap" describes falling out the bottom, so V-wrap was built (flag if H was meant). Walls on both sides, a peg field of bouncy tiles, walkers and crates dropping through it, the player in a roofed nook. Wraps at frame ~59, bounces within 0.5 s.
2. **The Cat Cannon Launchpad** (`catcannon`, room): verified that a chaser walks off a ledge in pursuit, bounces across a trampoline floor, loads into a cannon (NPCs can) and is fired onto the player's plateau (cannon fired at ~frame 210, then a catch and a checkpoint respawn, repeating). The player starts across the pit so it begins with no input. First attempt put the cannon 48 px from the plateau wall and the shot hit the wall; moved it to col 11 (trace, not a calculation). The cat is lethal by design, so an idle player is caught every ~4 s (lives drain).
3. **The Unstoppable Domino Dig** (`dominodig`, toy): holding Down on a diggable tile under the player breaks it in 54 frames (0.9 s). Falling bombs: any bomb that falls more than 6 tiles detonates, and a blast chains into adjacent bombs (35 explosion events along the floor row after the player walks a shelf bomb off the edge). Bombs are actors in a gravity room and are armed by a player shove or a long fall. Walking the bomb off the shelf, not digging, is what starts the chain; digging only drops the player. Tagged toy.
4. **The Infinite Elevator** (`elevator`, room, gravity off, wrapV): built with a wind column (`gust:n`) as with Crate Tornado, since a moving row of bouncy tiles can't exist (movers are sample-only); the player rides it too. No chaser: free-roam steering is plain 2D so a chaser would climb, but a lethal cat in a loop only ends it. No reward (it would end the loop).
5. **The Dangerous Trampoline Pit** (`trampit`, room): **a `solid:lethal` wall does nothing to a wanderer** (traced: it only blocks; NPCs are sent home only by walkable `none:lethal` overlap), so the pit's sides are lava columns over a trampoline floor: walkers fall in, bounce, drift into the lava and are sent back to their spawn (4-10 respawns in 10 s), crates burn.
6. **The Big Rink** (`bigrink`, room, gravity on, wrapH): the "Glitchy Conveyor Belt" is ice, renamed as suggested; two ice shelves plus a floor, a cannon with a crate above it as the starter, crates and pets (collectibles are inert and never slide).
7. **The Gravity Roulette** (`roulette2`, room, closed): NPCs can press the gravity Button (a wanderer standing on one flipped it: edge-triggered, once per visit); a crate dropped on a button flips it at frame ~18, then walkers keep pressing. Sealed by design.
8. **The Bomb Carousel** (`bombcarousel`, room, gravity off, both wraps): needs an actor to arm a bomb; **NPCs cannot** (enemies can't set off bombs) and a gravity button pressed early drops the bomb tiles into actors, so it uses a spawner (`spawnerh`, plain hazard ball) two cells from a row of bombs: the ball hits at 420 px/s (>= 240) and detonates the row at frame ~152 (10 of 10 seeds). The first spawn only happens at 2.5 s, so the bombs sit one cell past the ball's birth cell.
9. **The Slip 'N Slide Gauntlet** (`slipgauntlet`, room, gravity on, wrapH): no slope tiles exist in this build, so it is an ice floor with bumpers; the player starts airborne above a cannon (fires at frame ~63, lands on ice and slides across the seam).
10. **The Popcorn Box** (`popcorn`, toy, closed): bouncy box with trampolines, crates, walkers, the player in a roofed alcove (the first version killed the player with falling crates in 10 s). Collecting everything in Build only plays the celebration and puts the player back at the start (`roundComplete` is Play-only for the win), it ends nothing.
11. **The Minefield Run** (`minefield`, toy): bombs rest on the floor between the player and a goal flag; a player shove lights the fuse (verified: pushing the first bomb set off 4 explosions); each blast also removes the floor under it. Tagged toy.
12. **The Infinite Warp Loop** (`warploop`, vignette, gravity off): the warp tile is **player-only** and teleports to a random non-blocking cell (any cell, warps included; momentum is not special-cased); NPCs never warp. So the player starts on a warp (`playerwarp`) in a room that is almost all warp tiles: 1 to 337 blinks in 10 s, avg tens. No rewards (collecting them would win the session).
13. **Tunnel Trouble** (`tunneltrouble`, toy, two rooms): serialized from the unlisted sample by the helper itself (`surpriseSerialize("all")`), which doubles as the multi-room round-trip test; the cat catches an idle player in ~2.7 s.

**Tests.** `tests/surprise2.test.js` sections F (every Stage 2 and Stage 3 template: expectation within 10 s, canonical and chaos, 6 fresh pages each), G (room placement in place / new tab, both round trips), H (a Surprise exported and played: treadmill, tornado, catcannon, warploop, tunneltrouble, screen changes, zero errors). The hook counts `warp` now. Test-authoring traps found: a gravity Button pressed early converts bomb tiles into falling actors (looked like "the ball never hits"); a random pet standing near the target (or a pet clone as the spawner payload from `fills.ball`) made explosion checks flaky until the template stopped using soft slots there.


## v2.50 Stage 4: the interaction-counter fuzz, and the weak templates it found (Surprise Me)

**The measurement** (`node tests/surprise4.test.js [seeds=1000] [html] `, dev-only, not in the product or export): for seed 1..N a fresh page loads a temp copy of `index.html` with the debug counter from `tests/surprise.hook.js` (wrappers around the engine's own reaction functions: explosion, blackHole, carry, hit, respawn, buttonPress, cannon, lava, bombArm, warp, spawn, wrap, fanPush, beltRide, pull, bounce, plateOn, gateOpen), presses the dice with that seed (recency cleared, chaos on, whatever the press picks), enters Play, runs 300 frames (5 s) with NO input and counts distinct interaction types, **not counting `spawn`** (a spawner firing is not something happening between pieces). The goal: at least 90% of non-toy seeds (loop, vignette, room) show 2+ types. Toys and nonsense are reported but not held to it. Known blind spot: NPC bounces on bouncy tiles are silent by design and uncounted, so "bounce" means the player or an object bounced.

**Before (the pre-Stage-1 build `c95d2f3^`, 300 seeds) and after Stage 3, before any Stage 4 change (1000 seeds):** non-toy 67.4% and 68.9% with 2+ types (all 62-64%). So Stages 1-3 added content but did not move the rate; the number was set by a few templates that only ever showed one type (chaserparade 0%, plinko 0%, magnet 6%, bombcarousel 0%, pinballroom 33%, pinball 36%, slipgauntlet 25%, warploop 70%).

**The fix is a second, independent event in the weak templates, using plain pieces** (no engine change): a **plate** where something passes or sits by itself, so `plateOn` (and `gateOpen` when a gate of the same colour exists) happens in the first moments. Chaser Parade: the chaser starts on a plate (`plate&chaser`) and a gate holds the reward. Magnet Mischief: a plate next to the magnet where the pulled crates stop, and a gate in front of the reward. Plinko Forever: a plate under the spawner's first cell. Pinball Parlor: same, under the spawner. The Pinball Chaos Trap: three plates the first falling pieces pass. Bomb Carousel: the spawner's birth cell is a plate (a plate in the ball's path killed the shot: friction eats the 420 px/s launch within ~1.5 tiles, so the bombs must stay adjacent). Slip 'N Slide Gauntlet: a plate on the player's drop. Infinite Warp Loop: plates mixed into the warp field. A real layout bug found on the way: editing a row of identical rows by string replace changes the FIRST identical row (the plate landed on the wrong row until the edit was done by position).

**After (1000 seeds, 0 errors):** non-toy **96.6%** (761/788), all seeds 85.1% (851/1000); types per seed 0:28, 1:121, 2:435, 3:301, 4:115. Per template (>=2 types): bigrink, blackholewall, bombcarousel, bombdrop, catcannon, chaserparade, conga, conveyor, elevator, fanclub, holesoup, magnet, pinball, pinballroom, plinko, slipgauntlet, tornado, treadmill, tunneltrouble 100%; rink 94%, fallforever 91%, orchestra 89%, warploop 88%, trampit 86%, lemmings 81%, roulette2 81%, allbuttons 78%, spawnerwar 37%, zeropinata 57%. **Still at or near 0 (all toy/nonsense, not fixed, by choice):** dominodig, minefield, moat, popcorn, slippery, sticky, allsprites, alltramps, roulette (9%). Their loops are the "something the kid does" kind or have only one thing that can happen (sprites/trampolines bouncing). Lemmings and Roulette 2 dipped (81%) because a 5 s window sometimes ends before the wrap/first button press; not tuned.

**Screenshots** of five real dice presses in the editor (live sim, no input): `/tmp/claude-0/s2/shots4/press1-5.png` (not committed); the presses were Cold War Of Boxes, Everybody Walk The Plank, Fire In The Hole Cat, Hole Lot Of Trouble and The Pinball Chaos Trap, one room each (the full-room dialog's "Create a new room" was used when the room filled up).

**Also fixed (found by the Stage 3 run, shipped with it):** Try-this crashed on multi-room templates (they have no top-level `legend`); `surpriseTemplatesFor` now looks at every part's legend.

## v2.51 Stage 1: tidy editor layout (structure and alignment; presentation only)

Layout only: no gameplay, physics, asset, save-format or export-runtime change. Every old control id still exists.

- **Top bar lines up with the editor.** `alignTopBar()` (end of `fitCanvasAndAlign`, which wraps `fitCanvas` on resize/ResizeObserver) measures the palette's left edge and the canvas rect and sets `#topBar.aligned` (grid `--palCol | --boardCol`, `--topLeft` margin) from 1000px wide up. Logo spans from the palette edge; `#topBarControls` is an inner `1fr auto 1fr` grid over the board column, so the Build|Play toggle is exactly on the board centre (measured 0.0px at all 7 viewports, Build and Play, three samples) and moves with the board when the palette hides in Play. Right of the toggle: `#topBarRight` = Pause (attached, icon-only below 1400px wide via `.pl`) then Samples + dice at the board's right edge. Below 1000px the old flex bar is unchanged.
- **Toolbar groups**: `#grpHistory` (Undo, Redo) | `#grpBoard` (Reset, Clear) | `#grpProject` (New, Save, Load, Export), thin dividers (`.tgroup + .tgroup`; `markToolbarRows` drops the divider on a group that wraps to a new row), centred, width = board. Export has an accent outline; Clear and New are `.quiet` and go red on hover (confirmations unchanged). Short labels (`.ls`, no emoji `.ic`) via `#buildToolbar.short`, decided in `fitCanvasAndAlign` by window < 1400px **or board < 900px** (so 1440x680 stays one row); full names are the tooltips.
- **Room strip** (`#statusRow`, board width, centred): each tab is a `.roomTab` piece with its own close ×; "Duplicate Room" is a dashed ghost button after the tabs. **There is no add-room control in the app** (rooms come from Duplicate Room and auto-linked doors), so none was added. Score/lives/hearts/keys (`#hud`) show only in Play. Pan controls (`#cameraPanRow`) moved to a slim row under the board (Follow/Runner rooms, Build only) and are observed by the fit so the board shrinks to make room.
- **One message line under the board** (`#msgRow`, 20-24px, always reserved): `#log` (left; newest message only, fades after 5s via `log.fadeTimer`, cleared by `clearLogLine()` on entering/leaving Play) · autosave hint · `#keyHint` (right). Build: muted "Ctrl+Z undo · Ctrl+Y redo · P pause". Play: a DOM pill, fixed 12px, fades after the first key (same `keyHintFirstKeyAt` logic). `drawKeyHint` uses `#keyHint` when it exists; the standalone export has no such element and keeps the canvas pill unchanged.
- **Compact tier kept**: extra row costs ~30px, recovered with a compact-tier CSS block (tighter top bar/gaps/rows). Board widths now: 1366x635 675px (was 666), 1280x590 615 (was 606); `tests/stage2.test.js` "no regression vs v2.48" passes.
- Tests: new `tests/v251.test.js [shotDir]` (7 viewports x 3 samples x Build/Play: alignment, overlaps, clipping, group rows, no scroll, message line, HUD/pan rules; plus function checks for Undo/Redo/Clear/Reset/Save/Duplicate/close, log fade/clear, hint pill, Pause attachment, tab order). Updated for intentionally moved things: `stage2` (score is not in the tab row; pan below the board), `breathe` (toggle centred on the board, not the page). Stage 2 (DPR-aware crisp scales, 1.25 ceiling + size stepper) not started.

## v2.51 Stage 2: crisp board scales (DPR-aware), 1.25 default ceiling, size stepper; Surprise Me player fix
