Presentation only (plus one Surprise Me bug fix, below).

- **Allowed scales** (`allowedScales(max, dpr)`): multiples of 0.25 (from 0.5) where `4 * scale * devicePixelRatio` is a whole number, so every art pixel is a whole number of device pixels. DPR 1 and 2: every 0.25 step; DPR 1.5: 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2; DPR 1.25 and 1.75: 1, 2 (whole numbers only). Below 1.0 (fit under native size) the free fit is kept, no stepper (v2.48 numbers unchanged). Re-fit runs on resize and on a DPR change (`matchMedia("(resolution: Ndppx)")`, re-armed each time).
- **Default** = the largest allowed scale that fits, capped by `FIT_MAX_SCALE` (1.25, the one constant; `FIT_MAX_SCALE_WIDE` is gone). At 1920x1080 the board is 125% (the Stage 1 rows no longer leave room for 150% there anyway).
- **Size stepper** (`#sizeStepper`: − / label / +, absolutely placed at the right end of the room strip so the tabs stay centred on the board; `#sizeDownBtn`/`#sizeUpBtn`): steps through the allowed scales up to the largest that fits (hard cap 2.0, `FIT_HARD_MAX`) down to 0.5. Hidden when fewer than two sizes fit. Remembered per browser in `localStorage["toyGameDesigner.boardScale"]` ("auto" = the default; stepping back onto the default stores "auto"); every read/write is try/catch'd; a remembered size larger than fits clamps down (and returns when the window grows).
- **Pixel-grid details found by measuring** (checkerboard art tile, screenshot decoded in the page, runs of equal colour): the border must be whole CSS *and* device px (3px normally, 4px at DPR 1.25/1.5/1.75 — Chrome floors anything else, which shifted the board's content box by a fraction), the canvas width/height must not be rounded (`w = scale*768 + 2*border` exactly), and the board's outer edge must sit on a device pixel: layout centres it on fractions (and LayoutUnit is 1/64 css px, not a multiple of 1/DPR), so `snapCanvasToDevicePixels()` applies a `translate()` to the painted box after every fit (a transform is float-exact). Result: at DPR 1, 1.25, 1.5 and 2 every art pixel is exactly 4*scale*DPR device px wide and tall at every allowed scale.
- **Pointer maths** now subtracts the canvas border (`canvasBorderPx`) in `cellFromEvent`/`pxFromEvent`; before, the 3px border skewed hits near cell edges by up to ~7% of a cell at 125%. Verified at cell centres and 10%/90% offsets of corner and middle cells for every allowed scale at DPR 1/1.25/1.5/2.
- **Toolbar labels**: short labels still below 1400px wide; at wider windows they now switch on only when the full labels don't fit one row at the board's width (1920x1080 needs them at 125%).
- Tests: `tests/v251s2.test.js` (156 checks); `breathe`/`stage2` updated earlier for Stage 1 and still pass (see commit message for the final run).
- **Surprise Me player fix**: `surpriseApplySpec` decided "is there a player" from `playerRoomId == null`, which stays stale after the player is erased/cleared/undone, so no player was placed. It now checks whether a player actor really exists (hot room or any other room) and resets `playerRoomId` when none does. Verified on the old build (placed none) and the new one (places one, with the seeded random look).

## Black hole and warp animations; Teleporter art for the warp tile

Presentation only. A swallowed thing is drawn as a **ghost** (`fxGhosts`, `spawnFxGhost`, `renderFxGhosts`, called at the end of `renderActors`): a copy of its sprite that plays a recipe on its own while the real actor has already moved on or is hidden. Ghosts live on the game clock (they freeze while paused) and are cleared with `animState`.
- **Black hole** (`swallowIntoBlackHole`, recipe `blackHoleSuck`, 560ms): the sprite spins, shrinks and slides (`pull` 0->1, a new optional keyframe field, `ghost.dx/dy` in tiles) into the hole's centre, then a tiny `PARTICLE_ART` poof (`art2`/`art2From`). Objects: the actor is `destroyed` + `vortexHidden` (skipped by `renderActors`) and removed after the animation. NPCs: sent to their spawn at once, ghost at the hole, `warpIn` at home. Player: Play = game over after the animation (`vortexHidden`; the end overlay waits while a `holdOverlay` ghost plays, like the old splat); Build = back to start with `warpIn`.
- **Warp** (`triggerWarp`): `warpOut` ghost at the origin (stretched into a thin streak zipped upward, no spin, so it reads differently from the black hole), `warpIn` on the player at the destination (sparkle burst `PARTICLE_ART` for the first 20%, then the sprite pops in with an overshoot). `playRecipe(entity, name, art, opts)` gained `opts` (`artFrom`/`artTo` fractions limit when the override art shows). `warpPop` is now unused.
- **Warp tile art** is the 7-frame `TELEPORTER_FRAMES` (`TELEPORTER_FRAME_MS` 110), animated like the Black Hole; the swatch is frame 1. The crystal Paint-tile frame sets pasted alongside it were not added (not asked for).
- Export: functions in `runtimeFunctions`, `TELEPORTER_*` constants line, `fxGhosts` declared in the template. Test: `node tests/vortex.test.js [shotDir]` (13 checks). Not listened to or reviewed in motion; only still frames were looked at.

**Black hole sound**: `CHIP_DESIGNS.blackHole` (user-supplied per-frame data, 44 frames, ~0.7s; `sounds.blackHole`, limit 200ms/1 copy) replaces the splat for every black-hole capture (player, NPC, object). Never listened to by me; retune in the sound designer if needed.

**Surprise Me always gives its room a player (fix)**: a surprise built into a room with no player (a new room tab from a whole-room event or "Create a new room", or any room while the project's player lives elsewhere) used to leave that room player-less ("your player lives in another room"). `surpriseApplySpec` now moves the project's one player into the surprise's room (`removeAllPlayerInstances()` then place, one undo puts it back). A room that already has a player is untouched. `tests/surprise.test.js` "new room option" now expects the player to be placed.

## v2.52 Stage 1: gravity direction, "up" (a room rule; Stages 2-4 not done)

**What it is.** `gravityDir` ("down" default, or "up"; `GRAVITY_DIRS`) is a per-room rule next to `gravityEnabled`, wired into every
place that rule already lives: `createEmptyRoom`, `activateRoom` (both halves), `snapshotCurrentRoomState`, `duplicateRoom`,
`restoreProjectSnapshot` (a missing or unknown value loads as "down", so every old save and embedded sample is unchanged), the Play
snapshot (rooms are cloned wholesale), and the export (rooms in `GAME_DATA`; the template declares `let gravityDir`, `GRAVITY_DIRS` has a
`constantsSource` line, `gravSign`/`updateGravIndicator`/`flipArtRows` are in `runtimeFunctions`). Surprise Me and the starter board reset it
to "down" when they set gravity (their layouts are built for ordinary gravity; Stage 4 gives Surprise Me its own direction).
**Palette:** under Game rules, four arrow buttons (`#gravityDirRow`, `renderGravityDirSwatches`) appear only while gravity is on; ↓ ↑ work and
apply instantly (`setGravityDir`), ← → are shown dimmed ("coming soon"). **Play:** a small fixed-size arrow (`#gravIndicator`, in the HUD, updated
once a frame) shows only when gravity is on and isn't down. The standalone export has no such element (its function returns early).

**Approach, and a change from the Stage 0 recommendation.** Stage 0 recommended running everything in a rotated local frame. Reading the code
again, that fails for one concrete reason: tile coordinates are used as KEYS and write targets (`digProgress`, `bombArmed`, `tileAnimState`,
`board[cy][cx] = null` in dig/trap/bomb, locked doors, spawner state), so a half-turned frame would need every one of those mapped too.
Instead the y-axis sites multiply by one sign, `gravSign()` (+1 down, -1 up; always +1 while gravity is off). `x * 1` is exact, so at "down" the
arithmetic is bit-identical (proved below). Sites: gravity integration, MAX_FALL clamp, jump, `resolveAxis` y branch (land vs ceiling, bounce,
trampoline minimum launch, jump assist, `steppedOnTop`), `applyBouncyWallKick`, `triggerBouncyLaunch`, `shovePushables`,
`bounceOffTrampolineObjects`, `cellsUnderneath` (the row gravity pulls toward, used by conveyor/ice/mud under the feet), `isStuckToSticky` (the side
away from gravity), the riding step in `tick()`, the carry-follow (carried object sits on the side away from gravity), drop spots, throw's upward
arc, the default hazard knockback, the crush rule, the bomb >6-tile fall rule (`fallStartY` is now "distance along gravity"), the gravity-off pop
(away from the old gravity), the fell-off-the-world check, and the sprite. Stage 2 (left/right) swaps axes, so it will have to generalize these same
sites from a sign to an axis: expect that to be the big job, not a copy of this.

**Controls (character-relative).** Keys keep their meaning for the character: Up/W/Space = jump away from the surface, Down = toward it (dig).
Only Left/Right are mirrored on screen while gravity is up (`mirrorLR` in `stepActor`), so Left key = screen right. Hold keys across a flip and the
meaning follows the current direction. Chasers/wanderers steer in screen terms as before.

**Sprites.** Characters (the player, wanderer/chaser/fleer, stationary NPCs) are drawn with their rows reversed (`flipArtRows`) and a mirrored
lean; objects (crates, bombs, collectibles...) keep their art. Rows are reversed in data and drawn upright rather than flipped with
`ctx.scale(…, -1)`, because the engine's +0.5px cell overlap would otherwise move to the opposite edge and the flipped sprite would not be the
ordinary sprite's mirror pixel for pixel. Characters are still drawn at fractional positions like everyone else.

**Found, not changed (not caused by gravity direction).** Cells are scanned in ascending x, so when an actor's box spans two blocking cells the
left one resolves first. A 180° turn reverses that order, so walking onto a Trampoline floor tile launches at a different moment depending on
which side you come from (also true today in down gravity). Tie-sensitive discrete events (a crossing that lands within 1e-12 px of a cell edge)
can land one frame apart between a world and its half-turned copy; the invariance scenarios avoid exact ties.

**Not supported yet in non-down rooms:** Movers ("Traffic and floaters", sample-only) were not looked at; nothing was changed for them.

**Tests.** `node tests/gravdir.test.js <baseline index.html>` (A: every sample, 625 frames, every actor x/y/vx/vy/grounded + board + score +
lives + camera hashed per frame, baseline v2.51 vs new at "down" = identical; B: eight half-turn scenarios — platform run/jump, bouncy floor,
crate+stone stack, conveyors, falling bomb with chain blast, dropped trampoline, sticky wall, cannon, ice — the half-turned world with up gravity and
the SAME key presses mirrors the upright one to ~1e-12 px for 330 frames, with the boards mirrored at the end). `node tests/gravdir_ui.test.js` (buttons,
real Play, indicator, controls, save/load/old save/unknown value, rooms, duplicate, Play round trip, exported game played with up gravity, flipped
sprite cell by cell and pointer accuracy at DPR 1, 1.5, 2).

## v2.52 Stage 2: gravity left and right (Stages 3-4 not done)

`GRAVITY_DIRS` is now `down, up, left, right` and all four palette buttons work. "Left" = feet on the LEFT wall (gravity pulls toward -x), "right" = feet on the right wall.
Several statements in the Stage 1 section are superseded: gravity is no longer only a sign.

**From a sign to an axis.** Three tiny helpers are the only definition of "which way is down": `gravAxis()` ("y" for down/up and for every gravity-off room, "x" for left/right),
`gravSign()` (+1 toward +x/+y, -1 toward -x/-y) and `rightSign()` (which way the character's own right points along the axis it walks on, in screen terms: down +1, up -1, right -1, left +1).
Every Stage 1 site that multiplied by the sign now reads an axis too: `stepActor` has `gAx`/`cAx` (along / across gravity) and `aV`/`cV` (their velocity keys): input and steering go
to the across axis, friction and the run-speed cap act on the across axis, gravity/MAX_FALL/jump/wind-along act on the along axis, and the axes move across-first-then-along
(`AXES_XY`/`AXES_YX`; down is still x then y, bit-identical). `resolveAxis` branches on `axis !== gravAxis()` (a wall, with the bouncy wall kick) vs along (land / ceiling / bounce / trampoline
minimum launch), `shovePushables` and `bounceOffTrampolineObjects` work on any axis through position/size/velocity keys, `cellsUnderneath` and `isStuckToSticky` pick the column/row for the axis,
and the riding step, carry-follow, drop spots, throw arc, crush rule, hazard default knockback, bomb fall rule (`fallStartY` is distance along gravity), fell-off-the-world check and the
gravity-off pop all read the axis. `triggerImpact` thuds only for hits along gravity.

**Controls.** Character-relative, from the table in the brief: Left key pushes along `-rightSign()` on the walk axis. Down: Left=screen left. Up: Left=screen right. Right (wall on the right): Left=screen down, Right=screen up, Jump=screen left.
Left: Left=screen up, Right=screen down, Jump=screen right. `facingDir` is the screen sign of motion along the walk axis (so throw and drop go where the character is visibly heading);
the drawn facing is converted with `rightSign()`.

**Sprites.** Quarter turns are done in DATA like the half-turn: the art is mirrored for facing if needed, then rotated (`rotateArt90CCW` for right gravity, `rotateArt90CW` for left) and drawn upright, so the pixel grid is exact
and the engine's +0.5px seam overlap is the ordinary one. Only characters turn (player, wanderer/chaser/fleer, stationary NPCs). Lean and the along-gravity squash use the character's own axes.

**One more orientation-dependent thing fixed: the cell scan order.** `resolveAxis` used to visit cells row-major from the top-left. When a box spans two different blocking cells (a crate sliding off a belt into a gap next to a wall) the first visited wins, so a turned world
could resolve against the other cell and end a whole tile off. The cells are now visited in the character's own order (from the side away from gravity toward it, left to right in its own terms); at down it is exactly the old order. After this the Stage 1
trampoline-order quirk is gone too for turned worlds (down itself is unchanged).

**Export.** `AXES_XY/AXES_YX` and `APP_NAME` are in `constantsSource` (the exported game had thrown on the first frame because `stepActor` read `AXES_*`; and the fatal-error handler itself read `APP_NAME`, which did not exist in an export, so the real error was hidden behind a second one);
`gravAxis`, `rightSign`, `mirrorArtH`, `rotateArt90CW`, `rotateArt90CCW` are in `runtimeFunctions`.

**What was checked and what behaves how** (`tests/gravdir.test.js` section B2, `tests/gravdir2.test.js`, `tests/gravdir_ui.test.js`): every sample at down still identical to v2.51 over 625 frames; eight scenarios
(platform run/jump, bouncy floor, crate+stone stack, conveyors, falling bomb with blast, dropped trampoline, sticky wall, ice) inside the central 18x18 region, turned a quarter turn each way with the same key presses, mirror the upright run to ~1e-12 px
over 330 frames, with the boards turned. The control table is verified in all four directions, a flip in mid-air with a key held (the held Right follows the new direction), chasers and wanderers walk along the wall, carry/drop/throw on a wall, a spawner's content falls to the wall,
a fan blowing away from the wall holds a crate off it, h-wrap with left gravity keeps a crate coming round, and a Follow camera follows a player on the far wall. Quarter-turned sprites are the upright sprite turned, cell by cell, at DPR 1, 1.5 and 2.
**Not turned (by design):** cannon shot angles are fixed absolute directions (steep, `CANNON_DIR_X/Y`), so a cannon scenario is not rotation-invariant and was not in the quarter-turn set; wind and fans push in absolute directions, so a belt on a wall carries the character up or down the wall.
Runner camera with left gravity pins the player to the left edge, which is the Runner fail line (expected, untouched). Movers were not looked at.

## v2.52 Stage 3: the gravity Button's options (toggle / set a direction / random) (Stage 4 not done)

An uncolored Button (palette name "switch", geometry `pushbutton`) now has a "when touched" option: **toggle** (the default and everything that existed before), **down / up / left / right** (turn gravity on and point it that way) or **random** (a direction different from the current one). Colored Buttons, plates and gates are unchanged.

**Storage (additive).** `room.gravButtons` = `{ "x,y": option }` on the room object; absent means toggle (`gravButtonOptAt(gx, gy)` reads the hot room's entry, anything unknown reads as toggle). It rides along wherever rooms are cloned wholesale (undo, autosave, JSON save/load, the Play snapshot, the export's `GAME_DATA`); `duplicateRoom` copies it; an old save has no field and loads as toggle Buttons. No board-key, geometry or migration change. A stale entry is harmless (only read for an uncolored Button) and a fresh placement overwrites it. Known edge: a Button created by Surprise Me/the starter board over a cell that once held an option inherits that stale option (not handled).

**Behaviour** (`triggerFlip`'s uncolored branch -> `applyGravButtonOption(opt)`): set-direction turns gravity on and sets `gravityDir`, clears the bomb fall origins (as `setGravityDir` does), logs and refreshes the palette. The same direction when gravity is already on and pointing there changes nothing; the flip sound and `gravityFlip` animation already fire at the top of `triggerFlip` (500 ms cooldown, edge-triggered as before), which is the feedback; no new sound. Random = `gravRandomDir(n)`: stateless seeded hash (`GRAV_RANDOM_SEED`) of the press counter `gravRandomCount`, picking among the three directions that are not the current `gravityDir` (so it never repeats, also when gravity was off: it compares with the stored direction). The counter resets in `clearTransientRuntimeState` and the export's `restartStandaloneGame`, so every Play session sees the same stream. No weightless pop when a direction Button turns gravity on; toggle-off keeps its pop. Reset, door and room switch behave as the toggle does: the Play snapshot restores each room's own gravity on leaving Play, gravity set by a Button stays with its room.

**On the tile.** `drawGravOptBadge` draws a small dark disc in the tile's top-right corner with a white arrow (direction) or five dice dots (random); toggle shows no badge. The lever reads "on" for a direction Button while gravity is on and pointing at that direction, for a toggle/random Button while gravity is on. `renderRoomContents` passes `extra.gravOpt` for live uncolored Buttons. Vector drawing, no new sprites or pieces.

**Picker.** With the Switch selected and no channel color, a "Switch: when touched" row (`#buttonOptSwatches`, glyphs ⇅ ↓ ↑ ← → 🎲) sets `selectedButtonOpt`. Painting a fresh Switch stores the selected option; clicking a placed uncolored Switch with a *different* option selected re-sets it to that option; with the same option it does today's "set the starting state" click (toggle: flip gravity; set-direction/random: apply it).

**Export.** `GRAV_BUTTON_OPTS`/`GRAV_RANDOM_SEED` constants line, `gravButtonOptAt`, `gravRandomDir`, `applyGravButtonOption`, `drawGravOptBadge` in `runtimeFunctions`, `gravRandomCount` declared and reset in the template.

**Tests.** `node tests/gravbutton.test.js [shotDir]` (53 checks: palette row and real canvas painting; every option in Build and Play; toggle and re-point; same direction gives sound + animation and no change; badge pixel; chain of two Buttons; edge-trigger; 1000 random presses never repeat and reach all four, same stream twice; Play round trip, restart resets the stream, Reset keeps options; Duplicate Room, room switch; save/load, old save, exported game played). Regression: `gravdir.test.js` against the v2.51 baseline (every sample, 625 frames, identical at down) 87, gravdir_ui 39, gravdir2 40, stage1, deathdig, lethalnpc, landing, layout, stage2, vortex, bombs, magnet, crossingguard, cannonlanding all pass. Test trap: a test that wraps `sounds.flip`/`playRecipe` in the editor page must restore them before exporting (the export serializes functions by source). Surprise Me's `surpriseSerialize` does not know about Button options (Stage 4).

## v2.52 Stage 4: Surprise Me with gravity direction (editor only; the v2.52 patch is complete)

Surprise Me (dice and Try this) can now build rooms that fall up, left or right, and two loop templates carry a random gravity Button. Nothing in the engine or the export changed; every template without a `dir` builds the same spec as before (checked, below).

**Template field `room.dir`**: `"down"` (default, absent = down), `"up"`/`"left"`/`"right"` (fixed: the layout is built for that wall; a fixed left/right template mirrored left-to-right falls the other way, `mirrorDir`), or `"any"` (a layout that works whichever way it falls, e.g. a closed box or a room that wraps on all sides). For `"any"`, a chaos press rolls a non-down direction `SURPRISE_DIR_CHANCE` (25%, measured 26%, all three directions seen); Try this (canonical) is always down. The roll comes from its own stream (`mulberry32(surpriseHash(seed, attempt + 5001))`) so no other roll of any template shifts. `surpriseApplySpec` sets `gravityDir = spec.dir` when gravity is on (down otherwise). Templates marked `any`: Falling Forever, Oops All Trampolines, The Popcorn Box. Fixed: the new **The Upside-Down Picnic** (`upsidedown`, toy, closed, up; it carries a `gbutton:down` that brings everything down). Gravity-"either" chaos (the 15% flip of on/off) is unchanged and independent.

**Reachability under gravity** (`surpriseValidatePart`): when a spec's gravity is on and its dir is not down, "at least 15 free tiles reachable" uses a gravity-aware flood: walk across, fall along gravity, and rise about 3 tiles against it only from a standing spot. Down keeps the old 4-way flood on purpose, so no existing seed is re-rolled. Tested on a deep narrow chamber under a ledge: passes at down, fails at up.

**New piece `gbutton:<opt>`** (`down/up/left/right/random`): an uncolored Button with a gravity option, written into the room's `gravButtons` when the spec is applied (a plain `button0` deletes any stale entry on its cell; clearing a surprise's rectangle clears entries inside it; left/right options mirror with the layout). `surpriseSerialize` writes `gbutton:<opt>` for option Buttons and the room's `dir` for a non-down room (closing the Stage 3 "serializer doesn't know" note), and a serialized room rebuilds the same (canonical, `mirror:false`).

**Template changes**: **Falling Forever** (loop) now wraps on all four sides, is `dir: "any"`, and has a random gravity Button right under the player's start, so the first fall turns gravity (down, left, up and right all seen in one run). **The Gravity Roulette** (`roulette2`) swaps two of its toggle Buttons for random ones (it stays fixed down, because its self-start is a crate dropping onto a Button). Honest cost: a random Button can drop a crate on an idle player (about 6 of 30 canonical roulette2 runs cost a life in the 10 s no-input window, 1 of 60 for Falling Forever), so the "pets never hurt the player" check in `surprise2.test.js` now counts only character catches (`npcHit`, new in `surprise.hook.js`); crate crushes are not counted there. A full small sample game was not built (optional in the brief).

**Re-runs**: `gravdir.test.js` against the v2.51 baseline (every sample, identical at down) 87/87 and gravbutton 53/53, stage1, landing, `surprise.test.js` (ALL PASSED), `surprise2.test.js` 575/575. The v2.50 interaction fuzz (`surprise4.test.js`, 1000 seeds, 0 errors): non-toy seeds with 2+ interaction types **98.5%** (776/788; it was 96.6%; the counter now ignores the `npcHit` duplicate of `hit`, which had briefly shown 99.0%), all seeds 85.8%, 38 seeds with none (toys such as dominodig, minefield, popcorn, upsidedown, sticky, slippery are not held to it).

**New test**: `node tests/gravsurprise.test.js <pre-stage-4 index.html> [shotDir]` (43 checks): dir metadata; the roll (26%, three directions, canonical down, fixed up always); specs of the 34 unchanged templates identical to the previous build over 40 seeds each (ignoring the new `dir`/`gravOpt` fields); gravity-aware flood; the Picnic stands on the ceiling in Play; random Buttons in Falling Forever and Roulette fire and turn gravity; every `any` template played 8 s in all four directions (12 runs, no NaN/off-board); serializer and rebuild round trip; clearing a rect clears options; **an exported Falling Forever played**: the random Button travelled, the player fell through it, gravity turned, no errors; five non-down presses screenshotted (Falling Forever up and right, Oops All Trampolines left, Popcorn Box up, Upside-Down Picnic up; looked at two of them: the layout sits against the right wall for the rolled direction and the gravity arrow is lit).

**Not done / flags**: sideways "any" boxes have stuff falling to a side wall, so a pet can end in a corner the player is in; Movers and cannon arcs stay absolute (as in Stage 2). `toyDebug` count 0.

## v2.52 Stage 5: the view turns with gravity; stored directions are relative to gravity; Surprise Me stays mostly down

Direct feedback after Stage 4: controls felt broken in turned rooms ("no walking or jumping"), "everything should orient to the direction of gravity" with keys unchanged (Left stays Left, so in an upside-down room Left walks the character toward the other side of the *room*), cannons "and everything" should work in gravity's orientation, the gravity Button's options should read like the color pickers, and Surprise Me should mostly stay down. **Supersedes** the Stage 1/2 sprite handling (characters were turned in data to stand on the wall) and the Stage 4 "25%" chance. Physics is untouched: the 87-check v2.51 baseline comparison (every sample, 625 frames, identical at down) and the turned-world invariance suites still pass.

**"Controls not working": not reproduced as a code bug.** With real key events walking and jumping work in a fresh project and in all four gravity directions (and an export). The likely cause was the unturned view: in an up/left/right room the character was drawn lying on a wall while the world stayed put, and Surprise Me can build rooms with no floor at all (Falling Forever) where there is nothing to walk or jump on. If it still happens, a saved project or the exact steps are needed.

**The view turns.** `viewK()` is 0 (down or gravity off), 2 (up), 1 (left: view turned counter-clockwise), 3 (right: clockwise). `renderGame` draws the world (`renderWorld`) through `applyViewTransform` (half turn about the centre; a quarter turn is drawn at 3/4 size, centred, black bars beside it, still a whole 3px per art pixel, smoothing off) and draws every overlay (READY!, pause, game over, key hint, empty-room cue) afterwards in plain screen space. `viewportPxFromEvent`/`cellFromEvent`/`pxFromEvent` apply the inverse (`worldPxFromCanvasPx`), so painting, dragging, hover badges and the wind picker hit what is drawn; a click in a black bar is outside the board. Physics, `editorPanCol`/camera and saved data stay in world coordinates, so a project turned to left gravity is the same project. Build mode turns too (clicking a gravity direction button turns the room on screen). The pan buttons still move world columns, so on a sideways room they scroll vertically (not adapted).
- **Keys** keep their screen meaning; the Stage 1/2 character-relative mapping (`rightSign`) already was exactly that, so `stepActor`'s input code is unchanged. Facing is drawn as `rightSign() * facingDir`.
- **Art stays upright.** `artViewK` (set to `viewK()` only while the world is drawn) makes `drawArtScaled` turn art back in data (`turnArtBack`: half turn = reverse, quarter turns = the existing rotate helpers), cached per (art, cell size, k). Characters and every actor are drawn by un-rotating the context locally (`ctx.rotate(-viewAngle)`) and are then written in screen terms (lean from screen vx, squash on the screen axis, fall stretch from screen vy, offsets in screen axes); the old `flipArtRows`/quarter-turn data rotation of characters is gone from rendering. **Ground and scenery turn with the room** (plain/skinned solid, painted skins, Paint backgrounds: `drawGameplayTile` zeroes `artViewK` unless the tile is a non-solid object without a skin) so connected walls/textures stay connected (first version turned them back and broke the maze walls). Movers draw their own heading from the (turned) world vector with no art turn. Vector overlays (rings, badges, arrows, fan stream) are drawn in world space and so turn with the room.
- **Stored directions are relative to gravity.** Cannon `ne/se/sw/nw`, fan, belt/wind `n/e/s/w`, Gate, spawner, mover heading and the Moving Platform axis all mean what the picture shows on the screen. Implementation: `CANNON_DIRECTIONS`, `WIND_DIRECTIONS`, `FAN_DIRECTIONS`, `SPAWNER_DIRECTIONS`, `BARRIER_DIRECTIONS`, `MOVER_DIRS` are kept as **live tables of world vectors**, rebuilt in place by `syncViewTables()` from the pristine `VIEW_BASE_TABLES` whenever `viewK()` changes (called at the top of `tick`, `stepActor`, `renderGame`), so every existing consumer (physics, fan stream, Gate slide, spawner launch/target cell, wind arrows and picker, cannon fire) works unchanged. A platform's axis goes through `platformWorldAxis`. **Anything that reads these tables outside a tick must call `syncViewTables()` first** (the Surprise validator uses `specSpawnerDir` with the spec's own direction instead). The export ships the pristine tables (`VIEW_BASE_TABLES.*`) and `VIEW_LIVE_TABLES`; `viewK`, `worldVecFromScreen`, `screenVecFromWorld`, `syncViewTables`, `platformWorldAxis`, `turnArtBack`, `applyViewTransform`, `renderWorld`, `drawGameplayTileInner` are in `runtimeFunctions`; `artViewK` and `viewTablesK` are declared in the template.
- **Consequence for tests/authoring:** to mirror a world with turned gravity the stored directions stay the same and only positions are turned (`tests/gravdir.test.js` no longer flips wind/cannon keys). Old rooms with directional pieces and non-down gravity (only built by Stage 1-4 tests/Surprise Me, none of the samples) now mean the relative reading.
- **Button picker.** The "when touched" row (toggle / down / up / left / right / random) now sits directly under the Color row it belongs with and reads "Gravity: when touched (color: none)"; unchanged otherwise (`gravButtons`, colored Switches still use channels).

**Surprise Me.** `SURPRISE_DIR_CHANCE` 0.25 -> 0.12 (an `any` template under chaos), and the fixed-up Upside-Down Picnic has `weight: 0.25` (`surprisePickTemplate` picks inside a category by weight; all other templates weigh 1 and the pick is identical to the old uniform one). Measured over 900 seeds, about 3% of presses turn gravity (all three directions seen); Try this is always down. Because the view turns, a turned surprise is the authored room shown rotated, not a different-looking room.

**Tests.** New `node tests/gravview.test.js` (86 checks: all six tables in all four directions; Left/Right/Jump on the screen; pointer round trip incl. the black bars; cannon/fan/belt/spawner/platform behave as drawn; an exported left-gravity game boots and runs; Surprise Me share). Updated for the new model: `gravdir.test.js` (no direction flipping), `gravdir_ui.test.js` (the character is the same pixels in every direction; pointer in the turned view), `gravdir2.test.js` (spawner/fan stored in screen terms), `gravbutton.test.js` (measure the badge at down), `gravsurprise.test.js` (rate band 6-20%). Not re-run at DPR other than the ones in `gravdir_ui`.

**Not done / flags.** A quarter-turned room is drawn at 3/4 size with black bars (the board is 24x18, the screen 4:3). The gravity indicator in the HUD still shows the *world* direction. Sprite-art tiles whose picture has a built-in "up" (a Ground skin with grass on top) turn with the room by design. Movers and Surprise `any` templates were only checked by the suites above.

## v2.52 Stage 6: the board stays put again; gravity really changes direction and the characters turn to stand on it

Direct feedback on Stage 5: "gravity doesn't change direction, only the orientation of the board. I don't want the board to rotate; I want the sprite walking on the walls or ceiling as gravity calls for", with an animated turn, controls unchanged. **Supersedes the Stage 5 default** (the Stage 5 section above documents an optional mode now).

- **Board view: not turned.** `viewFollowsGravity` (a `let`, default `false`) gates `viewK()`; with it off `viewK()` is 0 everywhere, so the render transform, the pointer inverse, the art turn-back and the direction tables are all identity. Stored directions (cannon, fan, belt, Gate, spawner, mover, platform axis) are **absolute again** — exactly Stage 4 behaviour. The Stage 5 machinery is kept intact behind the switch (the export declares `let viewFollowsGravity = false`); flip it from a test/console to get the turned-board mode.
- **Characters turn.** `renderActors` rotates a character's sprite (player, wanderer/chaser/fleer, stationary NPC; objects keep their picture) by the angle for its wall: down 0, up half a turn, left wall +90°, right wall -90° (`gravK = viewKFor(gravityDir, gravityEnabled)`; `turnable` is false whenever the board view is turned). The shown angle is `actor.dispAng`, eased toward the target by shortest path (`exp(-dt/0.07)`, about a quarter second, on the game clock so Pause freezes it; the first draw snaps; the transient fields `dispAng`/`dispAngT` ride along in snapshots harmlessly). Lean, squash, fall stretch and impact squash are computed in the sprite's own frame (`screenVecFromWorld(vx, vy, kLocal)`), and facing is `rightSign() * facingDir`, so a character facing "its right" on the ceiling faces screen-left. Mid-turn frames use the live (rotated) draw path; settled frames are pixel-identical to the old data-rotated sprites (the original cell-by-cell `gravdir_ui` sprite tests pass unchanged).
- **Controls** are the Stage 1/2 character-relative mapping (unchanged since): Left key walks to the sprite's left (screen-right on the ceiling), Up/W/Space jumps away from the wall (screen-down on the ceiling), Down digs toward it.
- **Tests.** `gravdir.test.js`, `gravdir2.test.js`, `gravdir_ui.test.js` are back to their pre-Stage-5 versions (absolute directions, sprites turned, pointer unrotated) and pass (87/40/39; every sample still frame-identical to v2.51 at down). `gravview.test.js` now opens each page with `viewFollowsGravity = true` and covers only the optional turned-board mode (86 pass). Verified by screenshot: the board stays, the player stands upside-down on the ceiling and on the left wall, and the angle eases from 0 to pi in about 5 frames of 60 ms. Not covered: an automated check of the turn animation itself (observed through `dispAng`), the export (the flag and `viewKFor` are shipped; an export test with ceiling gravity was not re-run after this change beyond the unchanged `gravdir_ui` export check).
- **Flag for the user.** "Cannons and everything work in the direction of gravity" from the earlier message is not met in this mode: pieces keep fixed screen directions (a cannon marked NE still fires NE whatever gravity is). Making them gravity-relative without turning the board would need their art turned too; not done.

## v2.53 Stage 1: weight matters (non-player actor-vs-actor hits)

Part of the "When things meet" patch (Stage 0 matrix harness: `tests/pairs.matrix.js`; tests for this stage: `tests/weight.test.js`).
**Weight classes** (one constant block next to `reflectActorsApart`, fixed, no per-object settings): `MASS_LIGHT` 1 = every character and the hazard ball, `MASS_MEDIUM` 2 = crate, bomb, carried trampoline/magnet/cannon, `MASS_HEAVY` 6 = Large Stone (`actorMass(actor)`). The player is never weighed; its contacts are unchanged.

- **Exchange by weight.** `exchangeAlong(a, b, vk, s)` trades momentum along the contact axis with restitution `MASS_RESTITUTION` (0.5), at least `MASS_MIN_SEPARATION` (60) of separation (the old floor), capped at `MASS_MAX_SPEED` (500). A hit above the NPC walking cap sets `bouncyCooldown` (so the NPC speed clamp doesn't flatten it) and a flung character gets `displacedCooldown`. Used by `reflectActorsApart` (non-player pairs only; any pair with the player keeps the old 50/50 split) and by `shovePushables`.
- **Along gravity, a body standing on the ground is immovable** (`MASS_ANCHORED`), so a hit from above bounces off instead of driving a resting pet into the floor.
- **`shovePushables` (the path crates/stone/bombs actually take against each other) changed**: a non-player pushed into another *object* (not a wall) along the walking axis now passes its momentum on (`exchangeAlong`) instead of just zeroing, so a stone's push travels through a row of crates; a non-player lighter than a Large Stone can no longer shove it positionally (it stops flush and trades momentum, so a crate bounces off a stone and a chaser can't walk a stone across the room — Stage 0 showed it could); the player's pushing is untouched. Landing speeds below `MASS_TRANSFER_MIN_SPEED` (40) are plain stops, so resting stacks of 3 and 6 stay perfectly still.
- **Sound.** Any non-player hit involving a medium/heavy object plays `thud(closing / HIT_THUD_FULL_SPEED)` instead of the old flat boing (also for a medium/heavy object landing on another from `MASS_LAND_THUD_MIN_SPEED` 120+); enemy-vs-enemy bumps stay silent.
- **Three robustness fixes found by a chaos test (24 actors at up to 450 px/s, 30 seeds; baseline v2.52 had ~380 stuck-out-of-room frames, ~25 stuck-in-wall frames, ~100 one-frame embeds; now 0/0/0):** `pushApart` never nudges anyone into a tile; `resolveAxis` ignores overlaps thinner than `COLLISION_SLIVER_PX` (1) on the *other* axis for non-player bodies (a sliver of overlap used to resolve the body out the far side of the wall or a tile up); the riding step no longer drags a rider into a wall. A first attempt, "ignore a push when you overlap the object from behind", broke the Rock Bottom recovery test and the stacked-bomb test and was removed.
- **Export**: `actorMass`, `exchangeAlong` in `runtimeFunctions`; one `constantsSource` line for the MASS_*/HIT_THUD/COLLISION_SLIVER constants. Verified a scene with the new functions exports and runs 3 s error-free (the exported run does not exercise a hit: velocities reset on load).
- **Known limit**: ground friction (1800 px/s²) stops a flung pet within ~1-2 tiles, so "sends pets flying" mostly shows in the first few frames and in the air; a hit at 500 px/s moves a row of pets a few tiles at most. Changing friction was out of scope.
- **Behavior changes to report, not retune**: NPCs can't push Large Stone any more (Rock Bottom's stone puzzles never relied on that); crate-vs-crate hits at 40+ px/s now exchange momentum instead of stopping dead.

## v2.53 Stage 2: squash works both ways (crate/stone onto NPCs)

A falling crate or Large Stone now flattens a wanderer, chaser (lethal or not), fleer or stationary NPC, the mirror of the existing crate-on-player crush. Rule (`tryCrushNpc`, called first in `resolveActorContact`'s non-player branch): gravity on; neither body carried or cannon-loaded; crusher is `PUSHABLE_KIND` or `LARGE_STONE_KIND`; along-gravity speed > `CRUSH_MIN_VY` (400, ~2 tiles of fall; a 1-tile drop, ~305 px/s, does not squash); crusher's centre on the gravity side of the target's centre; a fresh spawn burst (`spawnerKey` + `bouncyCooldown`) never squashes. Unlike the player crush, an object the player just dropped/threw DOES squash NPCs. Not targets: the player, collectibles, crates, the stone, bombs. Speed is measured along gravity only, so a horizontally thrown crate does not squash.

Reaction (`squashNpc`): `squashed` flag (actor frozen in `stepActor`, with a `squashFailsafe` deadline so a snapshot restored mid-animation cannot freeze it), `sounds.splat()` (the existing sound, no new one), `RECIPES.squashFlat` (260 ms, wide and flat, fades; skipped when `reducedMotion()`, which also zeroes the delay), log "Squashed!". After `SQUASH_FLATTEN_MS` on the game clock (`gameSetTimeout`, so Pause freezes it): a placed NPC returns to its own spawn with `caughtBounceIn` and `squashImmuneUntil` = `SQUASH_COOLDOWN_MS` (900) so a crate resting there cannot repeat it; a spawner-made NPC is destroyed with `popVanish` like a popped one. Nothing is ever deleted outright; the player ignores a squashed NPC. `resetActorsToSpawn` clears both fields.

Interpretation calls to flag: the fleer is squashed and sent home like the rest (its own pop is still the reaction to the player touching it); `splat` was reused.

Export: `reducedMotion`, `tryCrushNpc`, `squashNpc` in `runtimeFunctions`; one `constantsSource` line for the two constants.

Samples: all 12 run 60 s idle and with a scripted pass (`tests/squash.samples.js`): 0 squashes, 0 errors, so no sample changes; Rock Bottom's beast and Large Stones never meet. No exemption flag was needed.

Tests: `node tests/squash.test.js` (drops from 1, 2 and 4 tiles on each NPC kind, thrown crate, held crate does not crush the player, placed vs spawner-made, resting crate does nothing, cooldown, Pause mid-animation, reduced motion). Regression suites (rockbottom 48, overengineered 63, weight, bombs, deathdig, lethalnpc, stage1, crossingguard, cannonlanding, magnet, vortex) pass.

## v2.53 Stage 3: a fast hit sets off a bomb actor; big hits are felt (puff + shake)

**1. Bomb actors by impact (`tryImpactDetonate`, called from `shovePushables`).** Stage 0 showed a fast object into a bomb ACTOR did nothing (only the tile rule in `resolveAxis` existed, and since v2.43 bombs are actors while gravity is on). Now a physics object (`PHYSICS_OBJECT_KINDS`, so crate, stone, hazard ball, a thrown magnet...) moving toward a bomb actor at `BOMB_IMPACT_DETONATE_SPEED` (240) or more calls the existing re-entrancy-guarded `detonateCarriedBombAt`; blast radius unchanged, chains follow the old rules. The check lives in `shovePushables` (not only in `resolveActorContact`) because a bomb is a STANDABLE object: a falling crate is seated on top of it and its speed zeroed before the contact pass could see it. The player's touch still arms the fuse (never reaches this), a bomb never sets off another bomb by weight (blast only), slow shoves and resting stacks never detonate (measured: a bomb under a 3-crate stack and a stone for 30 s, no event), and a `detonating` bomb is skipped by the shove so things fall through it instead of landing on a ghost. A drop of 1 tile (~305 px/s) already clears the threshold, so every drop from 1 tile up sets a bomb off. **Deviation from the brief, kept on purpose**: "or NPC" was not added. The decided rule "enemies can't set off bombs" (see that section) stands; a flung character into a bomb actor just bounces. Say the word if NPCs should detonate.

**2. Cannon-launched hits: nothing was missing.** A fired crate (`ballistic`) landing on a wanderer squashes it by the Stage 2 rule (its fall speed is far above `CRUSH_MIN_VY`, `splat`, sent home); a crate that hits an NPC's side trades momentum by the Stage 1 weights. Traced at three columns around the landing spot: squashed at the landing column and one before, plain hit one past it.

**3. Big hits are events (`bigHitFx`).** A medium/heavy non-player hit with closing speed >= `BIG_HIT_MIN_SPEED` (360, ~2 tiles of fall) spawns a dust puff (5 crisp 3-4 px squares, whole-pixel positions, no art, `BIG_HIT_PUFF_MS` 320, one per `BIG_HIT_PUFF_GAP_MS` 120, at most `BIG_HIT_PUFF_MAX` 6 alive) and a shake (`BIG_HIT_SHAKE_PX` 3 decaying over `BIG_HIT_SHAKE_MS` 160 in whole pixels, at most `BIG_HIT_SHAKE_PER_SEC` 2 starts per second). Both are off under `reducedMotion()`, run on the game clock (Pause freezes the puffs; `shakeOffset()` returns null while paused) and the whole thing is one switch, `BIG_HIT_FX`. Called from the same sites that play the weight `thud` (three in `shovePushables`, one in `resolveActorContact`). Only actor-vs-actor hits count; a crate landing on the floor does not. The shake translates only the world draw in `renderGame`, so overlays (READY!, hints) stay still. No new sound or sprite. Resting piles never fire it (30 s test).

**Export**: `tryImpactDetonate`, `bigHitFx`, `shakeOffset`, `renderPuffs` in `runtimeFunctions`; one `constantsSource` line for the BIG_HIT_* constants; `fxPuffs`, `bigHitTimes`, `lastPuffAt`, `shakeAt` declared in the template and reset with the other transient state. Verified: a three-bomb row set off by a dropped crate inside a downloaded game (3 bombs -> 0, no errors).

**Samples**: all 12, 60 s idle and scripted, old build vs new: identical bomb-detonation counts (the only ones are two in the Over-Engineered Escape's script, by blast, not impact), 0 squashes, no errors. **Frame time** with 40 mixed actors piled and moving: ~0.9-1.2 ms per tick both before and after (within noise).

**Test-authoring trap**: exporting a scene whose crate hangs in the air blows it up in the Build page before the export (detonated bombs are baked as `detonating`, a known narrow edge); stub `requestAnimationFrame` in the loading page so nothing simulates before export.

Tests: `node tests/chain.test.js` (21 checks: crate from 1/3/5 tiles and stone from 2/4 tiles onto a bomb, stone and crate shoved in fast vs slow, three-bomb chain, a bomb in a resting stack, cannon-launched crate into a wanderer, puff/shake once for a big drop, burst limited to 2 shakes, paused and reduced-motion, exported game). `tests/weight.test.js` now expects a stone into a bomb to detonate it (Stage 1 had asserted the opposite). `tests/squash.samples.js` also counts bomb detonations.

## v2.53 Stage 4: counting the new reactions, the pile room, three Surprise vignettes, and the matrix re-run

No engine change in this stage; it measures what Stages 1 to 3 built and puts it on display in Surprise Me.

**1. The counter.** `tests/surprise.hook.js` (the debug-only interaction counter, wrappers injected into a temp copy of `index.html`, not in the product or export) gained five event types: `shove` (a momentum exchange, `exchangeAlong`), `squash` (`squashNpc`), `impactBomb` (`tryImpactDetonate` returned true), `bigHit` (`bigHitFx` with closing speed >= `BIG_HIT_MIN_SPEED` and a medium/heavy body; counts real events, not every call) and `chainBlast` (a detonation started inside another). Each wrapper is guarded with `typeof`, so the hook still loads against older builds (the baseline run needs that).

**2. The pile room** (`node tests/pile.test.js [seeds] [html] [baselineHtml]`): one spawner each dropping crates, a bomb and a (harmless) pet, a Large Stone, a placed pet, horizontal wrap on and a lava drain in the floor; 20 simulated seconds, 100 seeds, fresh page per seed, `Math.random` seeded per seed.

| event | total | per seed | seeds with >= 1 |
|---|---|---|---|
| shove | 6338 | 63.4 | 100 |
| bigHit | 1031 | 10.3 | 100 |
| chainBlast | 157 | 1.6 | 83 |
| squash | 28 | 0.3 | 23 |
| impactBomb | 1 | 0.0 | 1 |
| explosion / respawn / lava / wrap | 1260 / 525 / 36 / 239 | | |

Distinct event types in the worst 10 s window of each seed (spawn excluded): 3 in 8 seeds, 4 in 57, 5 in 30, 6 in 5; **0 of 100 seeds below the target of 3**. Resting stack (bomb under three crates plus a stone, 20 s): zero events, largest per-frame movement 0.0000 px. Frame time, mean tick over the 100 seeds: 0.265 ms against 0.259 ms for the pre-Stage-1 build (ratio 1.02; the later 0.306 ms figure was taken while other suites were running). 0 errors. **Honest reading**: in this room `impactBomb` almost never fires, because the bomb spawner's bombs usually go off by the more-than-6-tile fall rule first; `squash` is rare for the same kind of reason (pets wander out from under things). The 3-types target is met mostly by shove, bigHit, explosion and chain blasts.

**3. Three Surprise vignettes** (all `category: "vignette"`, native gravity on and required, no wrap, existing pieces only, none uses Movers): **Rock Concert / Stone Cold Landing** (`stonepets`: a Large Stone drops down a one-tile well onto a pet and flattens it; a free pair of pets nearby), **Crate Rain Delay / Conveyor Of Regret** (`craterain`: crates hang over a belt, drop on the pet riding it, the rest ride into a lava pit, so the spawner has a real sink), **Row, Row, Row Your Bomb / Domino Effect** (`bombrow`: a crate lands on a row of three bombs, one goes off by impact and the rest by chain). `expect.check` is `squash`, `squash`, `impactBomb` and all three declare `selfStart`. Layout lessons found by tracing, worth reusing: wanderers drift out from under a falling stone within the ~0.3 s fall (a 5-pet row failed 20% of the time, even in a wide pen the pets pile against the walls), so the stone gets a **one-tile well** with the pet in it (24 of 24 runs, canonical and chaos); the crate-rain pet starts against the left wall and the crates hang over where the belt carries it (24 of 24); the Surprise validator needs a spawner to have a sink, so a spawner template needs lava, a black hole or required wrap in its legend. Measured with `tests/probe_s4.js`: first squash/impact at frame 19 to 28 in every run.

**Re-runs.** `tests/surprise2.test.js` 633 passed / 0 failed (it now includes the three new ids in its expectation map: >= 1 squash or impact in 10 s, canonical and chaos, 6 fresh pages each, pets never hurt the player); `tests/surprise.test.js 200` ALL PASSED (determinism, non-destructive placement, floors, overwrite and Try-this); the v2.50 interaction fuzz `tests/surprise4.test.js 1000` ran with 0 errors: **non-toy seeds with 2+ interaction types 99.7% (786/788)**, all seeds 93.6%. Caveat: the counter now has five more types, so this is not the same yardstick as the 98.5% from v2.52 (shove and bounce alone make many scenes count); the 31 seeds with no event are toys (dominodig, minefield, slippery, sticky and some moat/popcorn presses). Mix: the new templates took the vignette share to about 31%.

**4. The Stage 0 matrix re-run** (`HTML=<older index.html> node tests/pairs.matrix.js out.json`; the script now accepts `HTML=`): 1170 cells, Stage 0 build against now. **689 cells identical, 20 cells changed outcome, 309 changed only which sounds play, 152 only moved a little (displacement or peak speed).** The 20 outcome changes are exactly the intended ones and nothing else: *squash* (crate or Large Stone falling on a wanderer, chaser, fleer or stationary NPC, gravity, fast: `squashFlat` plus `caughtBounceIn`, 8 cells) and *impact detonation* (crate, Large Stone, hazard ball, trampoline, magnet or cannon striking a bomb fast: 2 detonations, the bomb removed, `bombBlast`; 12 cells, in both gravity and free-roam). No cell gained a catch, a reset, a hazard hit or a removed mover; NPC strikers still do not set bombs off (the decided rule). Sound sets: `thud` newly present in 302 cells, `boom` in 12, `splat` in 8; `boing` gone from 96 cells and `trampolineBoing` from 1 (the weight thud replaced the old flat boing for heavy/medium hits). The remaining 152 motion-only changes come from the weight exchange (momentum now passes through a row instead of stopping dead).

**Export.** A game built from each of the three templates was exported and played for 6 s (`node tests/export_s4.js`): stonepets squashed its pet, craterain squashed its pet and the rest rode into lava, bombrow set off all three bombs (3 detonations), zero page errors. Pitfall for such a test: export from a page whose engine functions are NOT wrapped by the counter, because the export serializes functions by source and a wrapped function becomes an anonymous `function () {...}` ("Function statements require a function name"). Nothing new was added to `runtimeFunctions` or `constantsSource`.

**New test files**: `tests/pile.test.js`, `tests/probe_s4.js`, `tests/export_s4.js`. `grep -c toyDebug index.html` is 0.

## v2.53 follow-up: a character walking into a crate or Large Stone no longer tangles (thud stream, creeping stone)

Reported after Stage 4: NPC sprites and objects like the crate and Large Stone "get entangled", with an endless stream of sounds and a stuck sprite. Reproduced (`node tests/entangle.js`: a chaser or wanderer walking into a crate or stone, 10 s): a chaser against a Large Stone made **190 thuds**, and the stone crept along ahead of it. Cause: the Stage 1 weight code treats any non-player moving at 40+ px/s into an object as a hit. A steering character re-creates walking speed every single frame, so every frame was a new "hit" (`exchangeAlong` + thud + big-hit effect in `shovePushables`), and `resolveActorContact`'s reflect-apart (every 500 ms, with `pushApart`) kept nudging the pair. Below the 40 px/s threshold the plain shove path also pushed the stone positionally, a few pixels a frame.

Fix: `npcJustWalking(actor)` = a steering character with no bounce, cannon or displaced cooldown running and speed at or under `NPC_MAX_SPEED`. For such a character: against a Large Stone it stops flush, silently, with no momentum exchange and no positional shove (the stone does not move); an object-blocked shove is a plain stop, no exchange or thud; and `resolveActorContact` skips the reflect/pushApart/thud when one side is just walking and the other is a medium/heavy object (`shovePushables` already handles that pair). A flung or launched character (cooldowns running, or faster than walking) still trades momentum by weight, so Stage 1's behaviour for real hits is unchanged. A chaser can still be blocked by a stone it cannot get around (no pathfinding, as before); it now just stands there quietly. After the fix: chaser into stone 190 thuds -> 3, stone stays put; wanderer into stone 9 -> 4; crate cases unchanged. `npcJustWalking` is in `runtimeFunctions`. Regression: weight, squash, chain, bombs, deathdig, lethalnpc, stage1, rockbottom (48), overengineered (63), crossingguard (30), surprise (40 fuzz) all pass. Not re-run: the 100-seed pile room and 1000-seed fuzz. Not listened to: only sound counts were checked.

## Crates are smashed by bomb blasts and by a Large Stone dropped on them

Reported as "the crate appears indestructible now". Earlier code never did either: a blast only knocked actors back and only lethal ground (or a black hole) destroyed a crate. It is a new reaction, added on request. `detonateBombAt` destroys any `PUSHABLE_KIND` actor in its radius (same shape as lethal destruction: `destroyed`, splat, `popVanish`, delayed removal; log now says "Smashed!"). `tryStoneCrushCrate` (called from `shovePushables`, next to `tryImpactDetonate`, because the stone is seated on the crate before the contact pass) destroys a crate that a Large Stone lands on at `CRUSH_MIN_VY` (~2 tiles of fall) or more along gravity. Stones resting on crates, slow shoves, and crates falling on stones do nothing. Large Stone, bombs and other objects are not affected by a blast beyond the old knockback. Export: `tryStoneCrushCrate` in `runtimeFunctions`. Test: `node tests/cratesmash.test.js` (6 checks).

## Bomb no longer drops through the Escape's upper belt (the sliver rule keeps a side that is being moved into)

Reported: in the Over-Engineered Escape the first bomb, fired by the cannon, no longer bounced off the trampoline onto the upper belt. **Not the weight class**: traced the bomb every 0.1 s through the recorded recipe on the pre-weight build (bdeb846) and now; the paths match until t=7.0 s, where the old bomb slides off the trampoline's ledge onto the corner of the belt tile (col 19) and rides west, while the new one dropped straight down. Setting the bomb to light mass changed nothing (identical trace); setting `COLLISION_SLIVER_PX` to 0 restored the old path. Cause: the Stage 1 sliver rule (`resolveAxis` ignores a tile overlapped by under 1 px on the other axis for non-player bodies) made the landing pass skip the belt corner while the bomb was still sliding onto it, after which the x pass saw the tile as a wall and stopped it. Fix: a side only gets the slack when the body is not travelling into it (per-side `slackL/R/T/B` from the velocity on the other axis). The bomb's whole 50 s trace through the recipe is now identical to the old build; weight, squash, chain, bombs, overengineered (63), rockbottom (48), deathdig, cratesmash pass. The chaos test inside weight.test.js (24 actors, no stuck-in-wall) still passes.

## v2.54 Stage 0-2: Surprise Me that builds machines (what the machines depend on, causal metric, variation operators)

**Stage 0 (report only, `tests/machines.stage0.js`)**, traced at 60 fps: spawner -> cannon works in a gravity room (spawn frame 3, loaded 14, fired 33, 19 frames loaded = `CANNON_LOAD_MS` 300; peak 3.7 tiles above the cannon; Large Stone never loads; with gravity off the 420 px/s spawn dies to friction before reaching a cannon two rows away). Plate: crate, stone and ball hold a channel for 13 s; a pet only while frozen (an unfrozen wanderer leaves in 18 frames); a gate that closes on a pet standing in it lifts it onto its top (no crush, no embed). Tile trampoline treats pet, chaser, crate and ball the same (5-tile drop launches at 555 px/s, 3.2 tiles, 8 decaying bounces); Large Stone does not bounce. Fan lifts crate/ball/pet to a hover 1.7 tiles above itself (peak 3.2); not stone. Belt seam and belt-to-lower-belt handoff 20/20. **Two facing magnets never oscillate** (ball sticks to the nearer one; started dead centre it sits still) so "Magnet Ping-Pong" was dropped. Black hole captures a slow crate/ball in free-roam within ~1 s from 5 tiles; not a resting crate under gravity. A blast under a Large Stone's support tile drops it onto the plate below (frame 43); `indestructible` stops it; the crate that triggers it is itself smashed. A crate dropped 1/3/5 tiles on a bomb detonates it (frames 14/22/28). Reset to start restores crate, plate/gate state and a blasted bomb, but a bomb whose fuse was already lit keeps its timer through Reset (only entering Play clears it).

**Stage 1: causal tagging (`tests/causal.hook.js`, `tests/causal.test.js`, dev-only, not in the product or export).** Wrapper-based like `surprise.hook.js`: every event gets an id and the id of its cause; an actor carries the id of the last event that acted on it (`actor.__cause`) so a cause travels actor -> plate -> gate -> actor. Types: spawn, cannonLoad/Fire, shove, squash, impact, bombArm, detonate, blastClear, blastHit, fall (only when a blastClear/gate opening removed what held it), bounce, trampolineLaunch, fanLift/Push, beltCarry, pull, plateOn/Off, gateOpen/Close, buttonPress, hit, lethal, respawn, capture, warp, wrap. Repeats of one type on one actor collapse into one link. **A press passes ("strict") if some cause chain has >= 2 links (>= 3 events) and >= 2 different types**; "lenient" = >= 1 link. Payoff beat = frame of the first non-spawn event that happened to a character (player or steering NPC); the first 15 frames ignore start-up landing noise (shove/bounce/fall/blastHit). `node tests/causal.test.js [seeds] [html] [out.json] [frames]`, env `TEMPLATE=<id>` and `VARY='{"payload":true}'` force a template / operators. Baseline on the Stage 1 build (1000 seeds, 10 s, no input, chaos on): strict 70% overall, 77% non-toy, lenient 86%; 13% no chain at all; lowest: blackholewall, dominodig, magnet (strict), minefield, slippery. The metric counts spawn -> bounce chains only once (collapsed) so a bouncing ball alone does not pass.

**Stage 2.**
- *Box with a crack*: a template is closed if >= 90% of its footprint border is solid (`perimSolid`, `tests/templates.probe.js`, checked by `tests/surprise5.test.js` and `surprise2.test.js`); only toys may be closed. Reclassified: fanclub and conveyor (top opened over the chamber), holesoup, blackholewall (right side/top opened), allsprites and alltramps (a gap in the middle of every side) were boxes and now are not; roulette2 became a toy (a sealed arena by design) and zeropinata is flagged closed (it was 97% solid).
- *Variation operators* (`template.vary`, closed tables, each from its own random stream so no older seed moves): `mirror:false`, `colors:true` (one consistent permutation of the explicit channel colours `plate:red`...), `payload:{char:[pieces]}` (verified swap list), `skins:false`, `sprites:false`, `drains:[[x,y]]` (declared-safe lava spots) and, in `room`, `dirs:[...]` (verified gravity directions). Chaos presses apply each allowed operator with `SURPRISE_OP_CHANCE` (0.6); `opts.vary` forces them (tests); Try-this (`canonical`) applies none. Declared today: `payload` on pinballroom, trampit, bigrink, slipgauntlet, elevator (crate/stone/hazard ball); the other operators are implemented and tested on a synthetic machine (colours stay pair-consistent and distinct, payload only from the list and one choice per char, drains only at declared cells, a direction off the list is refused, deterministic by seed) and wait for the Stage 3 machines. Every spec carries `variant` (a signature) and `ops`.
- *Weights*: `SURPRISE_CATEGORY_WEIGHTS` room 40, loop 30, vignette 15, toy 10, nonsense 5 (measured over 1000 chained presses: room 38, loop 32, vignette 15, toy 10, nonsense 6). A dice press avoids the last 5 templates and the last 6 variants (`surpriseRecentVariants`, editor session only); an explicit seed never avoids anything so replays rebuild exactly. A repeated variant is re-rolled with the next attempt number (same template).
- *Character in the path*: every non-toy template must show a character-involved event within 5 s in >= 80% of runs (payoff metric above). Fixed: fanclub, chaserparade, magnet and allbuttons pass once fan/pull episodes count; bombcarousel (pets beside the bomb row), bombdrop (the player's plate is next to the landing column so the blast shoves the player), plinko (a pet where balls land).

## v2.54 Stage 3-4: three machines in Surprise Me, and the re-run

**Machines** (`SURPRISE_ROOMS`, whole-room events, existing pieces only, `expect.selfStart`, `expect.chain` = ordered event types along ONE causal path, each with `requires`, `vary.payload` and three names; authored in `tests/machine.defs.js`, traced with `tests/machines.build.js`, reliability with `node tests/machines.rel.js <defs.js|index> <id> [runs] [fps] [secs]`):
- **The Domino Effect** (`domino`): spawner drops a crate on a bomb; the blast clears the diggable floor under a Large Stone, which falls on a plate that opens a gate. Chain spawn, impact, detonate, blastClear, fall, plateOn, gateOpen (~3.4 s). 100/100 at 30, 60 and 144 fps.
- **Crate Opens The Door** (`opensdoor`): spawner drops a crate into a cannon, fired onto a plate row, the gate opens and pets are released. Chain spawn, cannonLoad, cannonFire, plateOn, gateOpen, released (~4.1 s). 100/100 at 30, 60 and 144 fps. **Shortened on purpose**: the first design ended with the released pet being carried onto a trampoline; that failed (3% at 30 fps, 57% at 60) because a wanderer does not reliably walk onto the belt, so the chain now ends at "released".
- **Crate Carousel** (`carousel`): two opposed belts, a fan and a west gust carry 3 crates round a loop; chain beltCarry, fanLift, beltCarry (2.2 to 5.1 s to first full lap). 100/100 at 30, 60 and 144 fps.
- **Magnet Ping-Pong was dropped** (Stage 0: two facing magnets never oscillate).

**Stage 4 numbers** (`node tests/causal.test.js 1000`, 10 s, no input, chaos on), Stage 1 baseline then now: strict pass overall 70% -> 92%, non-toy 77% -> **98%** (loop 96, vignette 99, room 100), lenient non-toy 93% -> 100%, "no chain" non-toy 5% -> 0%, median first payoff 0.22 s -> 0.30 s (target < 4 s), no payoff non-toy 14% -> 0%. Still low (all toys/nonsense, not held to the target): dominodig, minefield, roulette, sticky 0%, spawnerwar 40%. Seeds run 1000, errors 0; operators active on 174 payload and 513 mirror presses, 497 distinct variants. Closed boxes are toys only (enforced by `tests/surprise5.test.js`), so the closed share cannot exceed the toy share.
**Tuning that got non-toy from 82% to 98%**: gates under the crate in rink/bigrink (a plate player start, a released crate), a button + crate + gate in slipgauntlet, a plate pad and gate in stonepets, plates and gates mixed into warploop, extra crates and a pet in alltramps.
**Suites**: surprise5 142/0, surprise2 674/0, surprise.test.js ALL PASSED. Exports of each machine with a variation played 9 s with zero errors (`node tests/stage4_s54.js <dir>`; screenshots of each machine in motion, their exports and ten random presses are in `prototypes/stage4-shots/`).
**Not done / caveats**: the ten random presses each used a fresh page, so recency across them was not exercised in that script (it is covered by surprise5); machine screenshots are a single frame, not looked at in motion beyond two stills.

## v2.55: six user-built contraptions in Surprise Me, spawners never blocked, actors drawn at hitbox size

**Six whole-room entries** (`SURPRISE_ROOMS`, category `room`, so they share the room category's 40% weight): `cannoncat` (Cannon Cat), `fanloop` (The Fan Loop), `monsterescape` (Monster Escape, gravity left), `byebyecupcake` (Bye Bye Cupcake), `gravity1` (Gravity Trampolines) and `chaos1` (Chaos One). They came from the user's uploaded project files (`*surprise*.json`, kept in the repo root) through `surpriseSerialize("all")`. A new template flag `authored: true` marks a hand-built room: `surpriseValidatePart` skips the "hazard/chaser/black hole near the player", "15 free tiles reachable" and "player cannot move" floors for it (the author's room may start the player boxed in or next to danger on purpose; Bye Bye Cupcake does both). Two new pieces, `spawnerb:<dir>` (drops bombs) and `spawnerp:<dir>` (drops pets), because the serializer had written those spawners as default hazard-ball spawners. Known losses: stacked actors in one cell collapse to one (so Bye Bye Cupcake has 19 actors, not 22), and gravity-1's moving platforms are written as `actor|movingplatform`. `expect` is only `selfStart`; no causal chain was authored. All six start something within 1 s (frames 1 to 59) in canonical and chaos presses.

**"Random level restarts" in the contraptions (no. 5 gravity-1 and no. 6 chaos-1): not a bug.** Every restart in a spy on `consumeLifeIfPlaying`/`restartLevelKeepingLives`/`respawnAtCheckpoint`/`keepPlayerOnBoard` over 90 s random-input runs was paired with a real life loss: Chaos One has 26 lethal chasers plus 48 bombs and kills an idle player within 2 s (every restart puts the player back among the same chasers); Gravity Trampolines drops spawner crates at max fall speed, and its random gravity Buttons can turn gravity so a crate lands on the player (crush rule). No stale timer, no guard teleport, no double reset. Left unchanged.

**Spawners are never blocked by what sits in their launch cell.** `tickSpawners` no longer skips a cycle when an actor is in the cell it faces: `ejectFromSpawnCell` shoves every occupant out along the facing (one cell further if that spot is free of tiles, plus a `SPAWNER_LAUNCH_SPEED` impulse, `bouncyCooldown`, and `displacedCooldown` for non-players) and then spawns. A Large Stone is the only thing that holds a spawner shut (`spawnCellHeldByStone`). A blocked facing tile (wall/edge) still stops it, as before. **`SPAWNER_MAX_LIVE` 8 -> 60** (spawned content no longer respawns, which was why the cap was added). Test: `node tests/spawner.test.js`.

**Actors are drawn at hitbox size, with their feet on the hitbox.** The picture used to be a full 32px tile centred on the 22px hitbox, so feet sank 5px into the floor and a character on a crate overlapped it by 10px. `ACTOR_DRAW` (24px, 3px per art pixel) now sets the drawn size and the picture is drawn feet-flush in the sprite's own frame, so it also stands correctly on a ceiling or side wall. Effect: every character/object is 25% smaller on screen. Movers and tiles are unchanged. `ACTOR_DRAW` is in the export's `constantsSource`. `tests/gravdir_ui.test.js` sprite checks were updated for 3px cells (0 cells differ in all four directions).

**Chaos One's chasers are harmless.** New piece `softchaser` (a chaser look-alike cast with the pet role, so the surprise's own clone has `lethal: false`; the kid's chaser def is untouched). Chaos One's `C`/`D` legend entries use it: all 23 chasers read non-lethal (checked through `npcIsLethal`). They still steer toward the player; touching one is a plain bump. A real `chaser` piece elsewhere is still lethal.

## "Stay Alive" added to Surprise Me (whole-room entry no. 7)

`stayalive` (category `room`, `authored: true`) from the user's `Stay Alive - surprise me 7.json` (kept in the repo root), serialized with `surpriseSerialize("all")`. Gravity on, wrapH, six chasers (the project's chaser def has no `lethal` field, so they are lethal, as the title implies; unlike Chaos One they were NOT made harmless). Two spawners that carried bombs were rewritten as `spawnerb`; stale spawner-content keys in the upload were ignored. Builds, plays and starts something in canonical and chaos presses. Tests: surprise5 163/0.

## Actors back to 32px; the Escape's bomb path no longer depends on the frame rate (as far as it can be)

**Sprites.** `ACTOR_DRAW` is 32 again (`ART * 4`, a full tile); the picture is still drawn feet-flush with the 22px hitbox (so feet no longer sink into the floor), but it extends ~5px past each side and ~10px above the hitbox. Characters standing on a crate therefore still overlap it by ~5px. Shrinking the hitbox to match would change every gap, jump and sample timing, so it was not done. `tests/gravdir_ui.test.js` is back to its 4px-cell version (39 pass).

**The Over-Engineered Escape's bomb (traced at 30-240 fps, `/tmp` scripts: load the sample, hold Left 20 s, sample the bomb every 0.5 s).** The cannon-1 -> trampoline-side bounce -> west upper belt -> lower-left belt ("the X") -> cannon-2 -> magnet route depended on the frame rate in three knife-edge places, all fixed:
1. *Cannon 1 clips the end of the belt above it.* The shot leaves a few px from the belt tile's corner; at 144 fps it clipped it and slid the wrong way. Cannon shots (`actor.ballistic`) now get `CANNON_CORNER_SLACK_PX` (8) of corner forgiveness while rising: the side being left behind on the y pass, and the top on the x pass (`resolveAxis`).
2. *The trampoline-side bounce depended on a ~1.3px overlap* (a 60 fps path hit the trampoline's corner, a 120 fps path passed it). The level got one tile: `solid:bouncy` (lwoIron skin) at (22,2), so every frame rate bounces off it. **This edits the user's sample level.**
3. *Landing on the lower-left belt needed x within 0.2 tile of col 11.* Two belt tiles (west, lwoBeltW) were added at (12,5) and (13,5), closing the gap between the two row-5 belts. **Also a level edit.**
4. *The magnet hold reach was 2px*, less than a frame of a 343px/s shot at 60 fps and more than at 144 (the bomb bounced off the wall instead of clinging). `magnetHoldsActor` reach is 8px.

Result: the bomb completes the X at 48, 60, 72, 90, 100, 110, 120, 125, 144 and 240 fps (and 30, 75); at 165 it flies straight to the magnet from the first cannon (it skips the X, still ends held). Not solved: the underlying cause is semi-implicit integration (trajectories differ by ~3px between frame rates); a jittery real-world dt will still vary the path a little. `overengineered` 63/63 (it had failed 2 at 144 fps mid-way and now passes), magnet, bombs, weight, chain, cannonlanding, rockbottom pass.

## Surprise Me v2.56 Stage C: true machines (existing pieces only; no engine change)

`tests/reliability.test.js` runs each template alone (`scene:false`, chaos on, no input, 10 s) and reports **honest%** (loop-free trigger-mechanism path with >= 2 mechanisms), **strong%** (honest AND the path ends in an effect: gate, cannon, bomb, fan, trampoline, gravity) and **freed%** (a character or object released/dropped by a gate opening on the path). A **true machine** = non-toy, strong >= 95%. The hook now emits `gravityFlip` for an uncolored Button so a gravity Button counts as a mechanism.

**Edits (rows/legend/requires only):** pinball, plinko, bombcarousel, craterain, carousel, pinballroom, treadmill, lemmings, trampit each got a second mechanism from existing pieces (a plate under the first spawn plus a gate that holds something, or a plate row feeding a gate). Categories: craterain -> vignette; carousel, pinballroom, trampit -> room; treadmill, lemmings -> loop. bombcarousel's reward became a pet behind gates, so its `discover` is now the pet (`w`).

**Result:** all non-toy templates strong 100% in 1300 reliability runs, 0 errors; 1000 chained generation presses: machine share 83.6% (target 25%), bounding box >= 40% of the room 97.9%, scene share 57.8%, repeats 0 within 5 presses. Suites: scenes 37/0, surprise2 761/0, surprise5 155/0, surprise.test ALL PASSED.

**Honest caveats:** in chaserparade, magnet, orchestra, warploop and trampit the gate is decorative (freed% < 100%: nothing physical is released), so the "machine" there is plate -> gate with a visible but not essential payoff. Toys that could not become chains and stay toys (or are retire candidates): holesoup, tornado, bombrow, elevator, fallforever, cannoncat, catcannon, fanloop. Scene share is capped near 57% because whole-room 24x18 templates cannot take fillers; room weight and the over-represented loops (five templates take about 27% of presses) should be retuned before Stage D.

## Surprise Me v2.56 Stage D: weights retuned, final measurement

`SURPRISE_CATEGORY_WEIGHTS` is now room 14 / loop 22 / vignette 40 / toy 15 / nonsense 9 (was 30/30/25/10/5). Whole-room 24x18 entries cannot take fillers, so cutting their share is what lifts the scene share; five loop templates had been taking ~27% of presses. Measured over 1000 chained presses: room 11-14%, loop 20-22%, vignette 39-42%, toy 15-17%, nonsense 9-11%. Test `surprise5` mix check updated to these targets.

**Re-measure (1000 seeds, 10 s, no input, chaos on, 0 errors):** non-toy honest chain 100% (target 85%); all presses 84%; bounding box >= 40% of the room 97% (target 80%), median box 85%; true machines (honest >= 95%) 76.5% of presses (target 25%); scenes with fillers 69.8% (target ~70%); repeat within 5 presses 0. Before (Stage A baseline): non-toy honest 59%, room-use 50%. Suites: scenes 37/0, surprise2 761/0, surprise5 155/0, surprise.test ALL PASSED. Montage of 12 presses at 1366x635 (start and 5 s, all PASS): `prototypes/surprise-stageD-montage.png`.

**Caveats carried over:** decorative gates (chaserparade, magnet, orchestra, warploop, trampit); toys that stay toys (holesoup, tornado, bombrow, elevator, fallforever, cannoncat, catcannon, fanloop); toy/nonsense categories pass the honest chain only 31%/39% by design. The montage presses each used a fresh page (recency cleared), so repeats there (domino, rink, allbuttons) are not repeats within a session.

## Two more whole-room Surprise entries, and placed NPCs now stay dead in lava in Play

`oopscannons` (Oops All Cannons, from `Oops cannons - surprise me 9.json`: a lattice of NE cannons, wrap on both axes, pets and the player) and `minilevel1` (Mini Level 1, from `Mini Level 1 - surprise me 8.json`: bombs, magnets, a fan, buttons and a gate over a lava pit, goal at the right) join `SURPRISE_ROOMS` (category `room`, `authored`). Serialized with `surpriseSerialize("all")`; the one stacked `pet&pet` cell was collapsed to one pet. Both build, start something within 1 s (cannon/shove, pull) and keep one player in canonical and chaos presses. Suites: surprise5 161/0, surprise2 787/0, scenes 37/0, surprise.test ALL PASSED, lethalnpc 31/0.

**The "NPCs die in the lava and respawn, even in Play" report was real, and was documented behavior.** In Mini Level 1 the brown gate holds the pets above the lava; once its button is pressed they fall in. `triggerNpcLethalReset` sent every hand-placed character back to its spawn in Build AND Play, so they walked straight back into the same lava forever (171 resets in 12 s, brown channel on). Now, in Play, a hand-placed character killed by lethal ground is destroyed like a spawner-made one (splat, pop, removed; the level restart brings it back); Build keeps the send-home sandbox rule. Mini Level 1 in Play: 9 deaths, 0 pets left, no respawn. Samples: 12 samples, 60 s idle and scripted, show 1 such death in total (a Sushi Train wanderer in the fire pit); nothing else changed. Black-hole capture of a placed NPC still sends it home (not asked).

## Tool panel: no layout jumps, styled scrollbars (presentation only)

**Stage 1 (board no longer resizes on a camera-mode change).** The culprit was `#cameraPanRow` (the Pan row under the board, 19px at compact sizes, 36px on large screens): `display:none` in Fixed, shown in Follow/Runner, so at 1366x635 the board stepped 674x507 <-> 646x486 and at 1280x590 614x462 <-> 586x441 (the larger viewports had slack in their stepped scale and did not move). The row is now always laid out in Build and only `visibility:hidden` when the camera is Fixed (`renderCameraPanRow`); Play still removes it. **Cost:** the Fixed-camera board is permanently 4% smaller at the two compact sizes (1366x635 scale 0.833, was 0.857; 1280x590 0.763, was 0.779); the alternative that keeps the old size (pan buttons moved into the message line) was not built. Tests updated for that (`stage2` floor 0.83, `breathe` compact baselines, `v251` pan-row check now reads visibility).

**Stage 2 (scrollbars).** `#paletteWrap` has `scrollbar-gutter: stable`, so its content width never changes when a tab's content starts/stops overflowing. Thin, rounded scrollbars built from the page tokens (thumb = accent mixed into `--panel-light`, accent on hover, transparent track, no arrows) on `#paletteWrap`, the Samples dialog (`#samplesModalCard`, 82vh) and the landing overlay; the exported game's `#log` (the only scrolling element there) got the same. Chromium/Safari use `::-webkit-scrollbar` (8px; Chromium ignores those rules if `scrollbar-width/color` are set, so the standard properties are applied only under `@supports not selector(::-webkit-scrollbar)`, i.e. Firefox, which therefore has no accent hover). **Edge fade:** a CSS mask on the palette with two scroll-driven animations (`@property --fadeT/--fadeB`, `animation-timeline: scroll(self)`) fades 22px at the top/bottom only while there is more to scroll that way; engines without scroll timelines (Firefox) and `prefers-reduced-motion` users get no fade. It also fades the scrollbar thumb itself near the ends. The editor's `#log` (overflow hidden) and `#roomTabRow` (scrollbar hidden on purpose) needed nothing; the stacked (<760px) page scrollbar is the browser default. **Not tested in Firefox (not installed here).** Test: `node tests/jump.test.js` (48 checks, real scrollbars, 4 viewports: camera swatches never move the board; ~100 swatches over all tabs cause zero board moves, zero palette content-width/gutter changes and zero swatch-row re-wraps; without the gutter rule the same test fails with 10-18 flips per viewport). Screenshots: `prototypes/panel-scrollbar-shots/` (Objects tab at 1366x635, top/middle/end, and the Samples dialog).

## "Show me how" tutorial, Stage 1: spotlight layer + step engine (editor only; Stage 2-3 not done)
