Stage 0 audit findings (nothing changed then): swatches have no ids, only `title`; swatch DOM is rebuilt by every `renderAll()`, so a tutorial must re-query its target each frame; the palette scrolls at every supported size (overflow 142-428px) but every target scrolls fully into view (never in the 22px edge fade, never covered); there was no `Escape` handler in the editor; autosave runs every 4s in Build only.

**Engine** (one block before the Boot section of `index.html`, plus a CSS block at the end of the style sheet; not in `runtimeFunctions`, so it never reaches the export): `startTutorial(steps, opts)`, `quitTutorial()`, `finishTutorial()`, `tutTick()`. A step is `{ name, done(), target(), applies?() }`; `target()` returns `null`, `{ sel, prep? }` (DOM element; `prep()` brings it on screen, e.g. clicks the palette tab and `scrollIntoView`s) or `{ cell: {x,y,w,h} }` (board cells, mapped through the canvas rect, border and fit scale). Steps follow BOARD STATE: each frame the first step whose `done()` is false is current, so out-of-order building skips ahead and nothing is ever undone or scolded. `prep` runs when the target changes or when the target is not visible (at most every 500ms), never every frame, so a kid's own scrolling isn't fought; a clipped or missing target hides the spotlight and the dim entirely (nothing is ever left dimmed).
**Layer**: `#tutLayer` (fixed, `pointer-events:none`, z-index 900): four dim rectangles around a hole (plain translucent fill), a square ring (3px border, stepped pulse), one 40px arrow (crisp SVG, whole-pixel positions, rotated by multiples of 90) on the side with the most room, stepped bob. `#tutClose` (the only interactive part, fixed top-right) and `Escape` quit. Reduced motion = no animation. Input is never blocked (decision: blocking only-the-hole would stop palette scrolling and out-of-order placement). Pause and tab-hide are not special-cased; the layer keeps tracking.
**Isolation (changed from the Stage 0 recommendation, by the user)**: no scratch project and no keep/back choice. The tutorial uses the current room if it is blank (`isCanvasEmpty()`), otherwise a new empty room named "Tutorial"; either way gravity is turned on for that room. `startTutorial` takes a snapshot (plus the undo/redo stacks), and `quitTutorial` restores it wholesale, so ✕/Esc put the project back exactly (the Tutorial room disappears). Autosave is off while it runs (`tutorialActive`, same guard as the landing screen). `finishTutorial` (after the last step is done and `finishDelayMs`, default 2500ms, so the existing win feedback plays) leaves the board in place, returns to Build, and, if the kid's player lived in another room, puts that player back there (placing a player removes any other, project-wide).
**Not done yet**: the six real steps, the landing button, the death path, `data-tut` attributes on swatches (recommended; the real steps will use `title` selectors until then), and the Stage 3 target test. The only step list is the dev one inside `tests/tutorial.test.js` (hook-injected temp copy, not shipped).
Tests: `node tests/tutorial.test.js [shotDir]` (58 checks at 1280x590, 1366x635, 1920x1080: ring on a swatch in a scrolled palette, follows palette scroll / window resize / canvas size step, board-cell step, skip-ahead by state, Tab never focuses the layer, Esc and ✕ quit at every step with nothing left and the project identical, Tutorial room for a busy board and removed on quit, spotlight still shown while paused, finish ends cleanly, reduced motion). Test trap: a live starter-board player keeps simulating in Build, so compare project snapshots only after it has settled (and ignore `impactWobbleT`/`dispAngT`). `landing`, `layout`, `stage1` suites still pass.

## "Show me how" tutorial, Stage 2: the six real steps and the landing button (Stage 3 not done)

**Entry.** `#landingShowBtn` ("Show me how", 💡) is the LAST button on the landing screen only; click = `endLanding(true)` (put the held project back) then `startShowMeHow()` -> `startTutorial(TUTORIAL_STEPS)`. Nothing on the board or in Build/Play. Finishing stores `localStorage["toyGameDesigner.tutorialDone"]` (try/catch, convenience only; the button just gets a tooltip). No keep/back choice at the end (user decision): after the win animation the tutorial layer goes, the board stays, and Build returns.

**Steps** (`TUTORIAL_STEPS`, state-driven as in Stage 1): `player` (a player actor exists), `ground` (>= 4 `solid:` cells), `enemy` (any non-player stationary/wanderer/chaser/fleer actor), `goal` (a goal tile), `play` (`appMode === "play"`), `win` (`playSessionEnded === "win"`). Each build step points at its swatch first (`tutSwatchTarget`: switches the palette tab and scrolls the swatch to the centre), then, once that piece is the selected one, at a board spot from `tutLayout()`: relative to the player's SPAWN cell (not its live position: in Build it falls while there is no ground yet), floor = 8 cells under it, enemy 3 to its right, goal 6 to its right. Build steps `applies` only in Build, so in Play only `win` can be current. `win` rings the goal cell; if the run was lost (`playSessionEnded === "lose"`) or the kid is back in Build it rings the Play button (the existing restart). A life lost with lives left restarts the level by itself and the ring simply stays on the goal. Skipped steps (anything already placed, in any order) just fall through; out-of-order placing never gets scolded.
**Two engine details found by tracing, both worth keeping.** (1) A tab click selects that tab's last piece, which could already be the target (the ring would jump straight to the cell). `startTutorial` and every tab switch from prep set `paintMode = "erase"` (highlights nothing) so "swatch first" is honest; the one coincidence left is a kid who picks the right swatch himself, which is fine. (2) `prep` used to re-run whenever the target was hidden, which snapped a kid who had deliberately switched tabs back to the old tab mid-click (a real clicking failure in the out-of-order test). It now re-runs for a hidden target only after `TUT_IDLE_MS` (1.5s) without pointer/key/wheel input (`tutNoteInput` capture listeners), and always when the target itself changes.
**Words needed: none.** Every step is a swatch, a ring and an arrow. One thing to know, not a word: the player falls to the bottom of the room while the ground does not exist yet (live sim); it is put back on its spawn cell by Play, and the ground ring is drawn at the spawn row, so the order works, but the first frames look like the player is far from the ring.
Tests: `node tests/tutorial2.test.js [shotDir]` (80 checks, real mouse clicks through the real palette and canvas at 1280x590, 1366x635, 1920x1080: button is last, every swatch and cell ring position, click-drag floor, Play, teleport-to-goal win, finish clears the layer and keeps the board and sets the done flag; out-of-order goal/enemy/player; skip-ahead; lost run rings Play; Home round trip keeps the tutorial; quit at each of 5 steps leaves the project byte-identical). Also updated for the fourth landing button: `tests/landing.test.js`, `tests/stage1.test.js`. `tutorial`, `landing`, `layout`, `stage1` suites pass.

## "Show me how" tutorial, Stage 3: the target test (the tutorial patch is complete)

`node tests/tutorialtargets.test.js` (not shipped; Chromium, plus Firefox automatically when Playwright has it installed — it did not here, so Firefox is untested) starts the tutorial from the landing button at 1280x590, 1366x635, 1366x768 and 1920x1080 and, by really clicking through the six steps, checks at each swatch step that the target exists, is visible, is inside the window and the palette, is not inside the 22px scroll-fade at either end, is not covered (`elementFromPoint`) and that the ring sits on it; at each board-cell step that the ring is on the board and inside the window. Failures are named by viewport and step (`1280x590 step "goal" (swatch): target element missing`; verified by breaking a selector). 232 checks pass. **When a control a tutorial step points at is renamed or moved** (a swatch `title`, a tab id, the Play button, the board layout `tutLayout`), update `TUTORIAL_STEPS`/`tutSwatchTarget` and run this test; the tutorial uses `title` selectors, so a label rename silently loses the target. Export check: a downloaded game contains none of `tutLayer`, `startTutorial`, `TUTORIAL_STEPS`, `tutTick`, `quitTutorial`, `startShowMeHow`, `landingShowBtn`, `tutorialDone`, `tutClose`, `tutorialActive`.

## Tutorial: ground first, a second level of ground, the hero settles onto its spawn

`TUTORIAL_STEPS` now run **ground, ledge, player, enemy, goal, play, win**. Ground is first so the hero lands on the floor; once the hero has settled in Build, `tutSettleSpawns` makes its spawn and start position the resting spot (live position == spawn, measured 0/0 at 1280x590, 1366x635, 1920x1080). The **ledge** is the second level: a 4 x 2 block on the right end of the floor (`tutLayout().ledge`); the enemy (a wanderer, kept) is placed on the floor in front of it and the flag sits on top, so the hero has to jump past the enemy and up onto the ledge (traced: hold Right, one jump, win). The wanderer can still walk off the floor's left end. Ground/ledge never advance on a timer: ground is done when the ringed floor is full, 8 solid tiles are down, or 4+ are down and another piece was picked; ledge when the block is full, 16 are down, or 12+ and another piece was picked. Both latch. Tests: `tutorial2` (88), `tutorialtargets` (244).

## Surprise Me: flatter picking, and hand-built rooms keep the author's own look (v2.57)

**Picking.** `SURPRISE_CATEGORY_WEIGHTS` is now a weight per TEMPLATE (room/loop/vignette 1, toy 0.6, nonsense 0.5), not a share per category, and `SURPRISE_RECENT_MAX` is 10 presses. Before, 5 loops and 8 vignettes took ~5% of presses each while 20 toys took under 1% each, so 100 presses kept showing the same dozen set-ups (one template came up 7 times). Now no template takes more than ~3% (measured over 1000 presses, 54 eligible templates, top 3.0%); the category mix follows template counts (room ~31, toy ~28, vignette ~19, loop ~12, nonsense ~8). Cost: fewer presses are machines (toys and nonsense are ~37% of presses, up from ~24%).

**Exact rooms.** The nine hand-built whole-room entries (cannoncat, fanloop, monsterescape, byebyecupcake, gravity1, chaos1, stayalive, oopscannons, minilevel1) carry an `exact` block taken from the original project files in the repo root: the author's ground texture per cell (`skins`, with rotation), Paint tile per cell (`backs`, Stay Alive's eight custom backgrounds), the custom wanderer/chaser looks (`actors`), the textures and Paint art they use (`grounds`, `bgs`), the hero look (`playerLook`) and the reward id. They no longer take a random wall texture, a random pet or a random hero look, and they never mirror. At placement `surpriseInstallExact` copies the defs into the project under `sx~<template>~<id>` names (never over the kid's own), so the room is the author's, not whatever the project's palette holds; the hero look is applied only over the factory look or a look a surprise gave (`def.surpriseLookSig`), never over a kid's own drawing. Pets stay harmless and chasers lethal by role (`surpriseDefFor` clones the author's art with only `lethal` set). To add a new authored room, serialize it as before, then add its `exact` block (see `/tmp`-style generator in the commit message: read the project JSON's `groundSkinBoard`, `groundSkinRotationBoard`, `backgroundBoard`, the used defs). Tests: `node tests/exact.test.js` (180 checks: textures, Paint tiles, character art and hero look equal the JSON for 3 seeds each), `surprise5` (163), `surprise2` (787), `scenes` inventory check allows `sx~` ids.

## Tutorial fixes, stage 2: the goal must be supported; game over points back at the goal

The tutorial's goal step is done only when a goal tile sits on or beside a solid tile (below, left or right: `tutGoalSupported`/`tutGoalDone`), so a flag floating in mid-air keeps the ring on the goal step. If a Play run ends in game over (`playSessionEnded === "lose"`), after a 1.6 s beat the tutorial returns to Build (`tutorial.lostRun`) and rings the goal cell instead of the Play button; moving/repainting the goal (its position key changes) or pressing Play again clears that state. Editor-only (not exported). Tests: `tests/tutorial2.test.js` (89, lost-run assertion now expects Build + ring on the goal), `tests/tutorialtargets.test.js` (244). Stages 3 and 4 of the tutorial fixes are not done.

## Tutorial fixes, stage 3: a wordless key cue during the win step

While the tutorial's `win` step is current and the run is in Play, a small cue of three keycaps (← → ␣, `.tutKeys` inside `#tutLayer`) hovers above the hero (`tutKeyCue`, positioned from the player's box through `tutBoardRect`, bobbing in two steps) until the first movement key (arrows, WASD, Space) is pressed in Play (`tutorial.keyed`; other keys don't count). It is hidden once the run ends, outside Play, while finishing, and the flag resets when Play is left, so a retry shows it again. Under `prefers-reduced-motion` the bob is off (the keycaps just sit there). Editor-only, nothing exported. Tests: `tests/tutorial2.test.js` (93: shown before any key, on screen, a non-movement key keeps it, first movement key hides it), `tests/tutorialtargets.test.js` (244). The reduced-motion rule is CSS only and was not exercised by a test. Stage 4 (last ring on the Objects tab, arrow overlap, Play-ring clip) is not done.

## Tutorial fixes, stage 4: a last ring on the Objects tab, arrows that avoid neighbors, no clipped rings

- **Last step.** After the win (`tutorial.won` latch, so `play` and `win` stay done once reached) the tutorial goes back to Build after a 1.6 s beat and adds a final `objects` step: one wordless ring on `#modeObject`; clicking that tab (capture listener sets `tutorial.objClicked`) ends the tutorial (board kept, done flag stored). Only the real `TUTORIAL_STEPS` have it; dev step lists in tests are unaffected.
- **Arrow.** `tutDraw` tries every side with room for the arrow and takes the one that covers the fewest other controls (buttons, swatches, inputs, the canvas; anything overlapping the ring itself is ignored), instead of just the side with the most room.
- **Ring clip.** Ring padding is clamped per side to the window (`min(6, distance to the edge)`), fixing the ~3 px cut-off on the Play button at the top of the page.
- Tests: `tutorial2` 99, `tutorialtargets` 244 (ring check now allows the clamped padding), `tutorial` 58, `landing` 43. Firefox not installed here, untested.

## Three more whole-room Surprise entries: Mini Level 2, Mini Level 3, Gravity Tunnel

`minilevel2`, `minilevel3` and `gravitytunnel` (from `Mini Level 2 - surprise me 11.json`, `Mini level 3 - surprise me 12.json`, `Gravity Tunnel - surprise me 10.json`, kept in the repo root) join `SURPRISE_ROOMS` as `authored` rooms with an `exact` block (the author's ground textures, hero look and custom chaser, no mirror), made with `surpriseSerialize("all")` plus a small throwaway generator. Mini Level 2 (cannon row, red gate/plate, a chaser pen, a spawner that dropped a chaser) and Gravity Tunnel (a spiral of 19 gravity Buttons, `gbutton:down/up/left/right`; the hero falls onto the first one by itself) start something with no input. **Mini Level 3 is category `toy` (`needsInput`)**: its only moving parts are Large Stones raining into a lava floor, so nothing interactive happens in the first seconds; the player must walk it. New piece `spawners:<dir>` (a spawner loaded with a Large Stone); the serializer now writes stone and bomb spawners with it / `spawnerb`, and a spawner loaded with any character as `spawnerp` (with a note), so Mini Level 2's chaser spawner drops harmless pets (Surprise casting rule), using the author's chaser art. Tests: `exact` 228, `surprise5` 172, `scenes` 37, `surprise2` selfStart for the new rooms.

## Stand-on-actor lift (drawing only)

Actor pictures are 32 px but boxes are 22 px (`ACTOR_SIZE`/`ACTOR_DRAW`, both unchanged, no saved size changed), so a picture standing on a crate/stone/bomb/platform used to be covered by the lower picture's 10 px overhang. `renderActors` now shifts the upper picture away from gravity by exactly the overlap, cumulative up a stack (`actorLiftShown`, `pictureOverhang`, `artEmptyEdges`). Overlap = the lower picture's overhang toward `-gravity` (10 px up for a floor-gravity object, minus its empty top rows) + the upper picture's own overhang toward `+gravity` (0 for a character's feet, 5 for an unturned object under sideways gravity) + the lower's own lift. Support = a `STANDABLE_OBJECT_KINDS` actor whose box touches along gravity (gap within 1.5 px, across overlap > 2 px) and the upper moves slower than 60 px/s along gravity. Carried, cannon-loaded, ballistic, destroyed, `bouncyCooldown` actors and anything mid-air get none; the optional turned-board mode (`viewFollowsGravity`) gets none. The lift eases at `LIFT_SPEED_PX_S` (160, ~4 frames for 10 px, whole pixels, game clock so Pause freezes it) and is instant under `reducedMotion()`. State lives in a `WeakMap` (`liftState`), not on the actor, so nothing is saved or snapshotted; export: the three functions in `runtimeFunctions`, `LIFT_SPEED_PX_S` constant line, `liftState` declared in the template.

**Proof physics is unchanged:** `tests/gravdir.test.js` against the previous build (every actor x/y/vx/vy/grounded, board, score, lives, camera, 625 frames on all 12 samples, plus the half-turn scenarios) 87/87; `weight`, `chain`, `cratesmash`, `squash`, `bombs` pass.

**Still overlapping (px, computed from picture/box geometry):** side by side actors 10 (5 + 5; a hero pushing a crate overlaps it by 10); any actor against a wall 5 per side (bomb 1; its art is 6 columns wide); crate/stone under a ceiling tile 10, hero 6; hero's head into an object above it 6, a crate or stone above anything 10; feet on a floor 0. Not addressed.

## Tutorial rework: the picture, never cut off, a gliding spotlight, words (editor only)

Feedback after playing "Show me how": place things like the attached project (`my-project_13.json`), the controls dialog was illegible, step changes were jerky and rushed, boxes were not waited for, and the ending jumped to Objects without saying so. **This reverses the earlier "no words" rule on purpose**: the tutorial now says short things.

- **Layout** (`tutLayout`, relative to the hero's square, default col 6 row 13): an 11-wide floor (cols 2-12, row 14), a 4 x 2 step on its right end (cols 9-12, rows 12-13), the wanderer at the far left of the floor (col 2, three empty squares before the hero), the flag on top of the step's right end (col 12, row 11). The wanderer def is made harmless (`lethal = false`) when the tutorial starts; quitting restores the snapshot, finishing keeps it. Clamped so everything stays on the board.
- **Never cut off.** Ground and the step are done only when EVERY ringed square is painted (`tutShapeFull`); the old "8 tiles anywhere" / "picked something else" shortcuts are gone. Escape hatch: the note card has a **Skip** button on the ground, step and wanderer steps (`tutorial.skipped`). Win-to-Build wait is 2.6 s (was 1.6) and the lost-run wait 2 s, so the end screen can be read.
- **A beat between steps.** Finishing a build step fades the spotlight out and shows "Nice!" for `TUT_HOLD_MS` (900 ms), then the next step glides in.
- **A gliding spotlight.** The hole, ring and arrow follow a smoothed copy of the target rect (`TUT_GLIDE_RATE` 11 per second, about 0.2 s to settle, `tutorial.shown`); dims fade (0.35 s CSS opacity) instead of toggling; the ring and arrow pulse with ease-in-out instead of 3-4 step animations. Reduced motion snaps and has no transitions.
- **The note card** (`#tutNote`, `tutNoteDraw`, one `say(t)` per step): 16 px bold text, cream card with an accent border, placed on the side of the ring that covers the fewest controls and not on the arrow or the controls card. Texts are per step and per phase (pick the tile / do it on the board).
- **Controls card** (`.tutKeys`, replaces the three keycaps): "← → walk / Space jump" in 16-18 px text, a pointer under it toward the hero, shown above the hero until the first movement key in Play.
- **The ending.** After the win the tutorial goes back to Build and the last step shows the note "Well done! Now make it your own! Open the Objects tab to find more pieces." with the ring and arrow on the Objects tab; clicking the tab ends the tutorial (700 ms, was 2.5 s).
- **Tests:** new `tests/tutorial3.test.js` (91 checks: layout equals the picture, notes legible and inside the window, nine of ten squares for 3 s never advances, the Nice beat, the ring glides, whole step drawn advances, controls card text, the ending message, Skip, reduced motion; at 1280x590, 1366x635, 1920x1080). `tutorial.test.js`, `tutorial2.test.js`, `tutorialtargets.test.js` were updated for the new coordinates and run with a fast hold/glide (`TUT_GLIDE_RATE`/`TUT_HOLD_MS` replaced in the test copy).

## Known limitations

- **Tall stacks under a low ceiling.** A stack of 4 or more crates under a low ceiling makes a hero standing on top look like it is clipping into the ceiling, although physically it fits. The lift draws stacks as tall as full tiles while the boxes stay 22 px. The fix, if it matters in a real room, is to cap the lift so a picture never enters a solid tile above it. (Measured: 4 crates are 88 px of box but 128 px of picture; with the ceiling bottom 128 px above the floor the stack's picture touches it, and a hero on top would be drawn about 28 px into it.)

## Landing backdrop: really centered, less zoomed

The backdrop canvas sat in the bottom-right quarter of the screen: `snapCanvasToDevicePixels` (editor pixel snapping) wrote an inline `transform` that overrode the CSS `translate(-50%, -50%)`. It now returns early while `body.landing` is set, and the landing CSS transform is `!important`. Zoom: width is `max(contain, 0.75 * cover)` (was full cover), so more of the room shows; on very wide or tall windows black bars appear on the short axis. In Follow-camera rooms, `updateCamera` eases `cameraX` to keep the player centered while `landingActive` (Runner and Fixed rooms are unchanged; a Fixed room can't center a player who isn't mid-room). Editor only, nothing exported beyond the one shared line. Tests: landing 43, layout pass.

## Aesthetics pass, stage 1: swatch labels (presentation only)

A labelled swatch (`.swatch:not(.big):has(> .lbl)`) is now 58x70: the picture whole (40px canvas) on top, the name in its own dark band underneath (11px bold, no text-shadow, 18px high), four per row in the 280px palette (`.swatch-row` gap 4px; the comfortable tier keeps 8px). Flat colour swatches without a label and the big game-rule swatches are unchanged. Display names that truncated are shortened through `DISPLAY_SHORT`/`dispName()` (large stone -> stone, moving platform -> platform, trampoline -> bouncer, locked door -> lock, black hole -> hole, crystal (pink/cyan) -> crystal / crystal 2, indestructible -> tough, stationary -> statue, shopping cart -> cart, rubber duck -> duck, sleeping crocodile -> croc, spiked mine -> mine); the swatch `title` (and so the tutorial's `title` selectors) and the hover caption keep the full name. Every label site passes through `dispName`. Screenshots of each tab at 1366 and 1920: `prototypes/aesthetics-shots/`. Tests: jump 48, tutorialtargets 244, layout 35.

## Aesthetics pass, stage 2: the first board (presentation only)

`loadStarterBoard()` (landing "Make a game" on a blank project) now paints a sky backdrop with a few clouds on the Paint layer (`paintSkyBackdrop`, defs `bgSky`/`bgCloud` created on demand by `ensureSkyBackdropDefs`), a 3-row floor with `gndGrass` on top and `gndDirt` below, the flag on the grass, and sets `idle: true` on the player def. `idle` is an opt-in def flag like `spin`: a grounded, standing-still character breathes (a few percent taller, `renderActors`; off under reduced motion), so only projects that started from the starter board have it. A room made by placing a new door gets the same sky. Sky and cloud count as empty (`isRealBackground`) in `isCanvasEmpty`, `surpriseRoomIsEmpty` and the Surprise occupancy map, so blank-room checks, the tutorial and Surprise Me placement are unchanged. Saved projects, samples and `createEmptyRoom` are untouched. Tests `stage1`/`landing` now expect the goal at row `ROWS-4`. Before/after: `prototypes/aesthetics-shots/stage2-*.png`.

Stage 2 follow-up: the tutorial's last step (ring on the Objects tab) had no scroll-into-view, and the taller stage-1 swatches left the tab scrolled off the top of the palette; it now scrolls the tab into view (`prep`). tutorial2 99, tutorial3 94, tutorialtargets 244, stage1 36, landing 43, layout 35, surprise, scenes 37 pass.

## Aesthetics pass, stage 3: scale (presentation only)

`FIT_MAX_SCALE` went 1.25 -> 2.0 (equal to `FIT_HARD_MAX`): the default is now the largest crisp scale that fits (steps of 0.25 whole at the device pixel ratio, as before; below 1.0 the free fit is kept; the size stepper and its `localStorage` pref are unchanged). 1080p only reached 125% because the rows around the board used ~50px too much, so a late `@media (min-width:1500px) and (min-height:820px)` block (placed after the base rules so it wins) tightens the comfortable tier: top bar margin 8, app padding 6, gameWrap gap 5/padding 6, toolbar/room-strip buttons 28px, Pan row buttons 22px, message line 20px. Board scale (Build) before -> after: 1920x1080 1.25 -> 1.5; 2560x1300 1.25 -> 1.75; 2560x1440 and 3440x1440 1.25 -> 2.0; 1728x1000, 1440x900, 1366x635, 1280x590 unchanged. Tests: `layout` 35, `v251` 485, `breathe` 239, `stage2` 104, `v251s2` 153 (its "default capped at 1.25" assertions now say 2). Screenshots: `prototypes/aesthetics-shots/stage3-{before,after}-{1920,2560}.png`.

## Aesthetics pass, stage 4: panel order and chrome (presentation only)

- **Order**: palette is now What to place (tabs) -> Game rules + Camera chip row -> caption bar -> the tab's content. Before, Game rules and Camera took ~200px above the tabs.
- **Compact row** (`#rulesBlock`): gravity / h-wrap / v-wrap as 36px chips (40px in the comfortable tier), a thin separator, then the three camera chips; the gravity-direction arrows (only while gravity is on) are the same chips on a second line. The old 60px "big" swatches and text labels are gone for these (titles and hover captions remain; new `CAPTION_TEXT` keys `rule-gravity`, `rule-wrapH`, `rule-wrapV`). All render functions and element ids are unchanged.
- **Camera pixel icons**: `CAMERA_ICONS` (8x8 `pat()` art): Fixed = a frame with a dot, Follow = corner brackets around a dot, Runner = a dot with a motion arrow.
- **Toolbar**: already grouped (history | board | project, with dividers, short labels when narrow) since v2.51, so no change this stage.
- Screenshots: `prototypes/aesthetics-shots/stage4-{before,after}-{1366,1920}-{objects,rules}.png`.

## Room rules heading and group labels (presentation only)

The compact rules row (aesthetics stage 4) now has a "Room rules" section title, and each chip group a small label: "Gravity & looping" (gravity, h-wrap, v-wrap) and "Screen" (Fixed, Follow, Runner). Kid-friendly renaming of the chips themselves (e.g. wrap -> loop) was not done; titles and ids are unchanged. Screenshot: `prototypes/aesthetics-shots/stage4b-after-1366-rules.png`.

## Lethal ground for placed characters is per-tile (supersedes "placed NPCs now stay dead in lava in Play")

In Play, a hand-placed character on lethal ground now depends on the tile's Ground skin: **lava swallows** it (destroyed like a spawner-made one; the level restart brings it back), while **fire, spikes, mines, custom lethals and plain `none:lethal` send it back to its start** (the original rule). Build always sends home. `LETHAL_SWALLOW_SKINS = ["lthLava"]` is the one list to extend; `checkNpcLethalContacts` reads the cell's `groundSkinBoard` entry and passes `swallow` to `triggerNpcLethalReset`. Spawner-made characters are still always destroyed. This restores Sushi Train's fire pit and Monster's Den's fire respawn while keeping Mini Level 1's lava deadly to pets. Black-hole capture of a placed character now follows the same rule as lava: destroyed in Play, sent home in Build (`triggerGravityWellCapture`). Export: one `constantsSource` line. Test: `node tests/lethaltile.test.js` (11 checks).

## Aesthetics pass 2, Stage 1: sky and cloud are Paint presets; clouds drift in Play

`BACKGROUND_PRESETS` now holds `ground`, `sky` and `cloud`; the entries with an `id` (`bgSky`, `bgCloud`) are the single source: `ensureSkyBackdropDefs()` builds the Paint defs from them, so the starter board (`loadStarterBoard`) and rooms made by a new door (`paintSkyBackdrop`) cannot drift from what a kid paints. Clicking the sky/cloud preset in the Paint tab *selects* that tile (creating it on demand) instead of overwriting the selected one. `isRealBackground` is unchanged: both still count as empty.

**Cloud tile = sky underlay (`art`) + a transparent-surround `overlay`.** `bgCompositeArt(def)` is what swatches and the pixel editor show; redrawing a cloud in the editor drops the overlay (a plain still picture, like frames). `drawCloudOverlays` (called from `renderRoomContents` after a Paint-layer pre-pass, before tiles and actors; the pre-pass replaced the old per-cell bg draw, same pixels) draws each overlay pixel at a whole-pixel offset: 0 in Build, `CLOUD_DRIFT_PX_S` (8) px/s in Play from `cloudDriftT0` (set wherever `playReadyUntil` is), wrapping at `roomCols()*TILE`, game clock (Pause freezes), 0 under reduced motion; a pixel is drawn only if the cell under it is `bgSky`/`bgCloud`. Old saves: `migrateLegacyCloud` upgrades an unedited legacy cloud (`LEGACY_CLOUD_ART`, sky and cloud baked in) on restore; a still frame is pixel-identical to before (0 differing bytes tested); an edited cloud is left alone. Export: `cloudDriftOffset`, `drawCloudOverlays` in `runtimeFunctions`, `CLOUD_DRIFT_PX_S` constant line, `cloudDriftT0` declared/reset in the template.

Test: `node tests/clouds.test.js [shotDir]` (17 checks incl. Build still, ~32px per 4 s in Play, reduced motion, no cloud over other Paint, legacy save, export). Screenshots: `prototypes/aesthetics2-shots/`.

## Aesthetics pass 2, Stage 2: idle life is a render rule (the old breathing never ran)

**Finding:** the starter hero's `idle` flag never did anything: it was read only in the non-posed branch of `renderActors`, and the player def (Hero) is posed, so Build and Play frames at 0/1.5/3 s were pixel-identical. The flag (`def.idle`, set in `loadStarterBoard`) is gone; nothing is stored on a def.

`idleLifeFor(actor, def, now, kLocal)` (drawing only, any art, honors `reducedMotion()`, game clock so Pause freezes it) returns the extra drawing for an actor:
- **Breathing, Play only**: the player, stationary NPCs and steering characters that are grounded and nearly still (speed < 8, no jump crouch, displacement or impact wobble). The middle art row (`IDLE_STRETCH_ROW` 4) grows 0/1/2/1 px every 240 ms with the feet fixed (`drawArtStretched`, whole pixels, so the art stays crisp; works for posed and kid-drawn art; per-actor phase). Measured: head 2 px higher at the top of a breath, feet unmoved.
- **Collectibles (Build and Play, keyed on the built-in def ids, anything else gets the float)**: `colCoin` width in quarter steps (0.25-1), `colGem` a white plus sparkle near its top right for 0.4 s of every 1.5 s (`drawSparkle`), `colHeart` pulse 1/1.06/1.12 scale, every other or kid-made collectible a 0/1/2/1 px float. Samples and the landing demo get all of it for free (same render path). This replaces the `def.spin` use; the `spin` field is still saved but no longer read.
Export: `idleLifeFor`, `drawArtStretched`, `drawSparkle` in `runtimeFunctions`, one `constantsSource` line. Physics untouched: `gravdir.test.js` against the previous commit (12 samples, 625 frames each) 87/87. Test: `node tests/idle.test.js [shotDir]` (15 checks: Build vs Play, kid-drawn character, kid-made collectible, steps, moving hero, pause, reduced motion, export). Screenshot: `prototypes/aesthetics2-shots/idle-play.png`.

## Aesthetics pass 2, Stage 3: landing without bars, no grid in Play, a livelier starter board

- **Landing demo, no black bars, same size.** The demo canvas keeps its size (`max(contain, 0.75 * cover)`); it now fades at its edges with a CSS mask (12% sides, 7% top/bottom), over `#landingBackdrop`: a 64x48 canvas (first child of `#gameWrap`, shown only while `body.landing`) refreshed from the live game every frame by `landingBackdropLoop` (own rAF loop, started in `showLanding`, ends with `landingActive`) and stretched over the window with smoothing, so the sides are a soft, colour-matched blur of the game itself. No per-frame CSS blur filter (costly on Chromebooks). The wash gradient is lighter on the right (0.88 -> 0.6 at 34% -> 0 from 66%). The sample is still whatever hero was picked (the Gravity Switcheroo is a dark level, so its right side is dark by nature).
- **Sample icon row.** `#landingSamples`: six text-free 64px buttons (`LANDING_PICKS`: Crossing Guard, Rock Bottom, Gravity Switcheroo, Sushi Train Panic, Dungeon Diving, Worst Elevator) using the Samples gallery's own icons; name only in `title`/`aria-label`; click = `landingPickSample` (same confirm rule as the gallery). Tab order is now ... Take it apart, six icons, Privacy, Contact (`tests/landing.test.js` updated).
- **No editing grid in Play.** `renderRoomContents` draws the grid only when `appMode === "build"`; an exported game and the landing demo therefore have none either.

All of these Paint ids count as empty (`STARTER_DECOR_IDS` in `isRealBackground`) and cloud pixels may be drawn over every sky band (`SKY_CLIP_IDS`, exported). Rooms made by a new door get the banded sky only. Old saves are untouched; the Paint tab's tile list gains these tiles once a starter board exists. `tests/stage1.test.js` now expects 6 actors on a fresh starter board.
- Tests: `landing` 43, `stage1` 36, `layout` 35, `clouds` 17, `idle` 15; the tutorial suites are re-run for this stage (see the commit message). Screenshots: `prototypes/aesthetics2-shots/landing-*.png`, `starter-build.png`, `starter-play.png`.

## "Make a game" never shows a black void; tutorial gets the sky and grass (editor only)

**Bug**: reload with a saved project, Home, "Make a game": `landingProjectHasWork` compared the autosave byte-for-byte with `FACTORY_DEFAULT_SNAPSHOT`, so a cleared project (never byte-identical) was restored as-is and showed a black empty board. **Fix**: `snapshotIsEmptyProject(snap)` is a content test: one room, no painted tiles, no backgrounds except the default sky/cloud/scenery (`isRealBackground`), no actors, no doors/spawner content, no custom or edited characters/ground textures/Paint tiles (compared with the factory defs by name/temperament/art/poses/frames) and no extra slot ids. Empty -> "Make a game" loads the starter; otherwise the project is restored. Samples keep their replace confirmation (`landingHadWork`). A project holding only a placed hero counts as work (an actor), as does a starter board left untouched.
**New project** now restores the factory snapshot, clears history/autosave, paints the sky and loads the starter board (`loadStarterBoard`, one undo step; undo lands on an empty sky board). **Clear board** is now one undo step and repaints the sky (`paintSkyBackdrop`), so no button leaves a black void.
**Tutorial look**: `startTutorial` calls `paintSkyBackdrop` (the same shared function the starter board and new-door rooms use) on whichever room it builds in, so a quit restores the snapshot and a finish keeps the sky. The ground and step-up steps point at the **grass** swatch (note text "Pick the grass tile…"); once any solid ground is selected the ring moves to the board cells (a plain `solid` pick is accepted too, not sent back), and done-checks only read the board key `solid:*`, so skinned tiles count (they always did).
Tests: `node tests/blankboard.test.js [shotDir]` (33: first visit, cleared+reload, New project+undo/redo, single tile, redrawn sprite, sample confirmation, tutorial quit/finish, old-format save), `node tests/tutoriallook.test.js [shotDir] [html] [label]` (45: happy path at 1366x768 and 1920x1080, grass counts, out of order, Esc/✕ blank and busy, reload mid-tutorial); `tutorial2` takes `VPS='[[1366,768],[1920,1080]]'`. tutorial/2/3/targets, stage1, landing, layout pass. `tests/lethalnpc.test.js` fails "export boot anchor present" on the previous commit too (the export boot line changed in the Pause patch); not touched. Screenshots: `prototypes/blankboard-shots/`, `prototypes/tutorial-look-shots/{before,after}-{first,finished}-{1366,1920}.png`.

## "Make a game" opens on an empty field; Clear board is a true blank (supersedes parts of the two sections above)

`loadStarterBoard()` now paints only the sky (bands, clouds), the hills/bushes on the Paint layer and a 3-row grass/dirt strip, with gravity on. **No hero, no flag, no coins**: the kid's first act is placing the hero; the drifting clouds (Play) are the only motion. The `goal`/player/coin placement and the "walk to the flag" log line are gone (log: "A sunny field. Place a hero to get started."). **Clear board** no longer repaints the sky: `initBoard()` gives a plain empty board (still one undo step). New project still starts from the starter (undo lands on a sky-only board). Consequences: the grass strip is real board content, so an untouched starter counts as "has work" (`snapshotIsEmptyProject`, `isCanvasEmpty`): "Make a game" restores it as-is, and the tutorial uses a new "Tutorial" room on top of it instead of the current room. A cleared project (no sky) is still "empty", so "Make a game" reloads the starter. Tests updated: `blankboard`, `stage1` (starter = sky+grass; a placed hero stands on it; export test places a hero first), `landing`.

## Spring Buddy: a coiled character preset with four poses

`CHARACTER_PRESETS` gains **Spring Buddy** (after Derpy), user-drawn art (`SPRING_BUDDY_HEAD` rows 0-3, coil rows 4-7, `SPRING_BUDDY_LEGEND`) as a four-pose set like Gary's (`SPRING_BUDDY_POSES`; poses are chosen by `currentPoseFor`, so there is no walk cycle, only stand/stride/air/crouch). **stand** = the art as drawn; **stride** (moving on the ground) = the coil squeezed up one row with the head kept level (the base row is empty, so he hops along 4 px off the ground); **air** = a long, thin coil; **crouch** (landing impact) = head dropped onto a flat coil. A continuous contract-then-expand through a jump is not possible with state-switched poses (a take-off squash would need `jumpCrouch` wired into `currentPoseFor`; not done). Preset only, no migration; characters that already applied another look keep it. The sprite editor's snapshot (`GAME_SPRITES`) was not refreshed. Checked: poses render, 64 cells each, no errors on load or in Play with the preset applied; the jump/air pose was not observed in Play (the test's Space press didn't register).

## "Stonekeeper's Vault" (v2.59), Stage 1: rooms 1 and 2 of a top-down block-puzzle dungeon

`buildStonekeepersVaultGame()` / `drawStonekeepersVaultIcon`, registered last in `SAMPLE_GAMES` (id `stonekeepers-vault`; not yet in `LANDING_PICKS`). Coded sample, no engine change, no new piece or sound. Gravity off, fixed camera, four-direction pushing (verified: crate and Large Stone both push on x and y with gravity off). **Plates, not Switches, for crates**: a Switch is a permanent toggle flipped on every entry, so a crate sliding across it and the hero stepping on it cancel out (traced); a Plate holds its channel while anything rests on it and a stopper wall behind each plate keeps a pushed crate from overshooting. Gate and plate share a channel color (red, blue), which is the only "label".

- **Room 1 "The Entry Hall" (warm)**: hero, one crate on a red carpet lane, red plate (11,8) with a 3-tall stopper behind it, red Gate (14,8) in a divider, door (23,8) to room 2. A spare crate sits in the open.
- **Room 2 "The Cold Vault" (cool)**: two chambers' worth of gates in series. Crate at (7,11) is pushed straight up onto the red plate (7,4); the lane crate (4,8) goes along row 8 through the red Gate, then down column 15 onto the blue plate (15,12); the blue Gate (18,8) opens toward the exit door (23,8). The exit door is NOT linked yet (room 3 comes in Stage 2). One spare crate in the first chamber (a spare past the red gate was removed in Stage 3: the solver showed it let a kid skip carrying a crate through the gate).
- **Dead states**: no pull exists, so a crate pushed into a corner is gone. Policy chosen with the user: open floor around every lane and spare crates; the existing Play restart is the last resort. The Stage 3 solver has to confirm no reachable dead end.
- **Art**: `svKit(palette, tag)` builds sample-scoped defs per mood: four wall skins (brick, torch, banner, pillar) as `groundTileDefs` and five floor tiles (two flagstones, cracked, lane/carpet, pebbles or moss) as `backgroundTileDefs`. `dress()` gives every one of the 24x18 cells either a wall skin or a floor tile (torches/banners along the top rim, torches down the side rims, pillars in the open). Nothing is empty.
- **Asymmetry note**: the red-before-blue order is enforced by the gates, not by crate identity; any crate can go to either plate.

Verified so far: all 12 older samples frame-identical (625 frames each) against the previous commit; layout and landing suites pass; scratch trace in room 1 (crate onto plate opens the gate) and room 2 (red plate crate, lane crate through the gate to column 15). Screenshots of both rooms at 1366x768 looked fully covered. Stage 3 owes the solver, bot, old-autosave, landing and both-viewport screenshots.

### Stonekeeper's Vault, Stage 2: Room 3 and the goal

Room 3 "The Stonekeeper's Chamber" (royal purple/gold) is linked from Room 2's exit door (both ways). Rooms: 1 -> 2 -> 3, each with a door pair; checkpoints at each arrival.
- **Green**: a crate pushed straight up onto the green plate (7,4) opens the green Gate (10,8). A spare crate waits in the open.
- **The stone**: a full-height lava moat (col 14, rows 5-11) cuts the chamber. A crate burns in it (so crates can't do this job) and, top-down, nothing can be PUSHED across it: whoever pushes a stone into lava ends up standing in the lava (the hero's box overlaps the cell by ~10px at the end of the push, traced by geometry, not just reasoned). So the stone is grabbed (E) and thrown across onto a 3x5 block of purple plates; a landing anywhere on the block, or even straddling its edge, holds the channel, and an undershoot that stays in the first lava cell is within grab range from the bank, so it can be picked up again. A second stone waits at (12,4). Traced: a full-charge throw from (13,8) lands the stone at column 16.7 on the plates and turns purple on; the throw works only because the engine's throw is horizontal (`facingDir`), so the moat is a column.
- **Purple Gate** (14,14) in the wall below the moat lets the hero into a corridor (the plate pocket above it is sealed by a wall row), which holds a yellow Key; the yellow Locked Door (18,14) in the next divider opens on touch; the goal flag sits at (21,14). Win is the existing unconditional goal win.
- Honest deviation: the brief's "a stone too heavy for one of the pushes" cannot be built, since the player's pushes are never weight-limited. The design-around is the stone's real difference (lava-immune) plus the throw. **The throw needs a hint**: the only cue is the existing key-hint pill ("E grab and throw") and the stone being the one piece that is not a crate; Stage 3 should report this as a step that would need a word.
- Verified by scratch trace: green plate -> grab -> throw -> purple on -> corridor -> key -> locked door -> goal, `playSessionEnded` = "win", no lives lost; 12 older samples frame-identical; layout/landing pass.

### Stonekeeper's Vault, Stage 3: proof

Scripts (not shipped, in `tests/`): `vault.dump.js` (dumps the three rooms from the real builder), `vault.solver.js` (cell-level push solver: BFS for the shortest solution, exact reachable-state dead-end count where small, random push-history sampling otherwise; Room 3 has a throw macro for the stone), `vault.bot.js` (plays the solver's solutions through real key events in Play, then the key and goal; `export` as 5th argument plays a downloaded standalone file instead).

**Solver results** (push-only; "stuck" = the exit can no longer be reached by pushing alone): Room 1 solvable in 5 pushes, 20,100 reachable states, 1,431 of them stuck (both crates jammed against walls; 12 of 12 sampled histories could still win). Room 2 solvable in 22 pushes (425k states searched; the whole space is too big to enumerate); sampled 12 random push-histories: 6 can still win, 3 stuck, 3 undecided at the search cap. Room 3 solvable in 8 steps (7 pushes plus the throw); sampled 12: 7 can still win, 5 stuck. So pushing alone CAN strand a kid in all three rooms (mostly after jamming the crates needed for the plates). The recoveries are the existing ones: grab a jammed crate with E and carry or drop it (any crate can be grabbed from a corner, not modelled in the push-only numbers), or restart Play. A standalone export has no restart button (known limit), so a kid who never grabs and jams everything has to reload the page. Room 1 and Room 3 have one spare crate, Room 2 has one spare (see below).

**Real findings fixed in this stage:** (1) a spare crate past Room 2's red gate let a kid skip the carry-through-the-gate puzzle (shortest solution was 12 pushes and never used the lane crate); removed, shortest is now the intended 22. (2) Arriving by a door put the hero ON the door tile, and after about a second of standing still the door sent them back (found by the bot, which waited); doors now arrive one cell inside the room (`toX` 1 or 22). (3) An existing trap worth remembering: a bot (or a kid) holding a movement key through a door carries on in the new room and pushes whatever stands in the way.

**Played end to end:** the bot follows the solver's solutions through real key events (push, throw, key, locked door, goal) and wins with all 3 lives, in the live editor and in a downloaded standalone game, with zero errors.

**Other checks:** all older samples play frame-identically to the pre-vault build (625 frames each, gravdir.test.js against the commit before the vault); an autosave from the pre-vault build loads and plays; "Play a game" on a fresh profile lists the vault and opens it in Play; screenshots of all three rooms at 1366x635 and 1920x1080 (`prototypes/vault-shots/`): 0 uncovered cells, board inside the window, no errors. The sample is in the gallery, not in `LANDING_PICKS` (a seventh icon would change the landing layout tests; say if it should be).

**Steps that would need a word:** (a) the stone throw in Room 3: hold E to charge and release; the only cue is the existing key-hint pill, which fades after the first key, and a kid has to guess that the stone, unlike a crate, survives lava. (b) Nothing tells a kid that a jammed crate can be picked up and put somewhere else (same hint pill). (c) The yellow key and yellow locked door share a color, which is the only link. Everything else reads from color matching (plate, Gate), the lava, and the flag.

## "Cloud Hopper" (v2.60), Stage 1: Rooms 1 and 2 of a meadow-to-sky climb

`buildCloudHopperGame()` / `drawCloudHopperIcon`, registered last in `SAMPLE_GAMES` (id `cloud-hopper`; "Cloud Hopper" is a placeholder name; not in `LANDING_PICKS`/`LANDING_HEROES`, Stage 4 needs approval). Coded sample, **no engine change, no new piece, sound or rule**. Hero is the default Gary (`playerDefId`), by the user's choice over Mrs. Pecks. Two fixed 24x18 rooms, gravity down, so far: "The Meadow" and "The Climb" (a door pair links them; room 3, the exit door and the flag are Stage 2).

**Everything is dressed with the shared routines**: `paintSkyBackdrop` (banded sky, clouds that drift in Play), the starter hills/bushes (`bgHill1-3`, `bgBush`) on the Paint layer, `gndGrass`/`gndDirt` for ground. The only sample-scoped art: `chCloud` (a solid Ground texture, geometry unchanged, a kid can repaint with it) and `chDeep` (a Paint tile for pit interiors). Mousecop (chaser) and Wheebuns (fleer) are the built-in defs with `lethal` turned off inside the builder (the same switch the Characters tab has), so nothing here costs a life.

**Jump numbers measured at 30/60/144 fps** (tick-by-tick, a throwaway harness): apex 122/127/130 px, standing jump reach 176-178 px, running jump 183-188 px, trampoline apex 264-276 px, trampoline reach 271-275 px, a moving platform adds no speed to a jumper (its 59 px of travel per jump just shifts the landing). Design limits with 25% margin from the 30 fps worst case: step up <= 2 tiles, flat gap <= 4, trampoline rise <= 6, gap to a moving platform <= 3.

**Room 1** (ground row 15): a flat coin run, a 3-wide, 2-deep dip (cols 10-12; you can jump back out), a cloud step 2 up (cols 16-18, row 13), a second 2 up (cols 19-23, row 11) and the door at (23,10). Mousecop starts near the hero and bumps along behind; it falls into the dip when the hero jumps it (harmless, stays there). Traced: every standing/short jump over the dip and onto both clouds lands, at 30 and 60 fps.
**Room 2** (ground row 15, door (0,14)): drop into a 1-tile pit with a trampoline at (9,16), which lifts the hero to a 7-wide cloud (cols 13-19, row 10) with Wheebuns on it, then a Moving Platform (x axis, centred col 19, row 8) carries up to the top ledge (cols 20-23, row 6). Traced: holding Right from the pit and releasing at the edge both land on the cloud at 30 and 60 fps; the platform leg (jump toward it from the cloud) works for roughly 45% of the platform's 7.3 s cycle per takeoff spot (it fails when the platform is overhead or more than 4 tiles away; a kid just waits); from the platform, a jump to the top ledge lands for every platform position left of col 19.3 (about half of the cycle). The ground strip means any fall lands somewhere recoverable.
**Found while testing**: the built-in fleer and chaser defs are lethal by default now, so Wheebuns on the route cost a life (and restarted the room) until it was made harmless; and a bot that holds Right the whole way through a jump flies past a 3-wide cloud (the reach is 5+ tiles), which is a test-control matter, not a level fault.

Not done: screenshots at 1920x1080, the fall test from every platform, frame-identical sweep result, old-autosave check, first-frame test (Stage 3); Room 3 and optional side routes (Stage 2). Screenshots: `prototypes/cloudhopper-shots/`.

### Cloud Hopper, Stage 2: Room 3, doors, the flag, and optional side routes

**Room 3 "The Sky Island"** (fixed camera, gravity down): a two-row cloud floor across the whole bottom (any fall from any height lands there, never at the start), a door back at (0,15), and a staircase of clouds, each 2 tiles up with gaps of 1-2 tiles: floor -> A (cols 4-6) -> B (9-11) -> C (14-16) -> D (18-20) -> the island (21-23, row 6) with the flag (a plain `goal`) at (22,5). Wheebuns (harmless) wanders the cloud floor. A coin trail arcs up the whole route. **Doors**: Room 2's top ledge now has a door at (23,5) linked both ways to Room 3's (0,15), arriving one cell inside (`toX` 1 / 22, the vault lesson). Win is the existing unconditional goal win. Not hearts-for-points: a goal tile exists, so collecting everything does not win.

**Optional side routes (extra coins, a heart each), chosen to stay out of the main route's jump space.** Room 2: a low route up the right side (clouds at cols 18-20 row 13 and 21-23 row 11, coins and a heart). Room 3: a low route off the cloud floor (cols 21-23 row 14 then cols 17-19 row 12, coins and a heart). Room 1 has no side route on purpose (it is the gentle room, and see below). **Lesson found by tracing**: any cloud within about 3 tiles above a launch point caps the jump (the hero's head hits its underside), and a 2-tile step needs 64 of the ~122 px apex: a first Room 3 high route put a cloud right above the first cloud and made the next jump impossible from every takeoff column; a Room 2 route over the moving platform's lane cut the platform-to-ledge window from 53% to 35% of the cycle; a Room 1 route above the second step made a takeoff from its left end fail. All three were moved or removed; side routes now sit beside, never over, a launch zone.

**Traced (30 and 60 fps, hold-time sweeps through real key states)**: every main step in Room 3 lands for a wide band of hold times (floor->A 0.20-0.56 s, A->B from its right end 0.26/0.32-0.74 s, B->C 0.44-0.80 s, C->D 0.20-0.68 s, D->island 0.20-0.80 s), both side-route legs in Rooms 2 and 3 land, Room 1's pit and cloud steps still land (18/18), Room 2's pit-bounce-to-cloud still lands, and the moving-platform-to-ledge window is unchanged (about half the cycle; landing on the ledge near the door also counts, since the door is the way on). Walking through Room 2's door arrives in Room 3 at (1.2, 15.2); standing a hero on the island and walking right wins.

Not done (Stage 3): the end-to-end bot and its reachability check, the fall test from every platform, the frame-identical/old-autosave/export checks, screenshots at both viewports, and the first-frame test. Screenshots (Build, all three rooms): `prototypes/cloudhopper-shots/`.

### Cloud Hopper, Stage 3: proof

Scripts (committed, not shipped): `tests/cloudhopper.bot.js [html] [fps list] [full|fall|both]` (a bot plus the fall test), `tests/cloudhopper.reach.js` (reads the real boards and checks every required jump against the measured limits). The bot sets the same `keys[...]` the keyboard handlers set and steps `tick()` by hand with `requestAnimationFrame` off, so runs are deterministic; it knows only which platform it stands on (walk to a takeoff x, jump, steer toward the next platform, let go) and, after a miss, simply starts again from wherever it lands. **Test-authoring traps found**: with manual ticking `roomTransition` never ends (`frame()` ends it), so a bot must advance the game clock and clear it itself after a door; a bot that repeats the same timing against the moving platform can loop forever (at 30 fps it kept overshooting onto the optional cloud and dropping back, a deterministic cycle), so the hop moment now varies after each miss, as a kid's would.

**Full run, start to flag (Mrs.-free, default hero Gary), 3 lives kept, zero errors:** 30 fps 29.4 s, 60 fps 19.5 s, 144 fps 19.5 s. Route: 11 hops (the 11 required jumps: Room 1 dip, cloud A, cloud B; Room 2 trampoline bounce, hop onto the platform, hop to the ledge; Room 3 five cloud steps), about 45 to 56 tiles walked (Room 1 5.2 s, Room 2 6.1 s at 60 fps and 13.0 s at 30 fps because the moving platform needs a second try, Room 3 6.6 s).

**Reachability** (`cloudhopper.reach.js`, all ok): every ground/cloud step is a 64 px rise (48% margin against the 30 fps apex of 122 px); the widest flat gap is 96 px over the Room 1 dip (36% margin) and the widest cloud gaps are 64 px (50% margin); the trampoline bounce rises 192 of 279 px (31% margin) to the big cloud. The two timed legs (platform hops) are measured by simulation, not computed: the hop from the cloud onto the moving platform lands for about 45% of the platform's 7.3 s cycle per takeoff column, and the hop from the platform to the ledge for about 53% (39/74 and 40/74 sampled start phases at 30/60 fps); a kid waits for the right moment, and the cloud floor below makes a miss free.

**Fall test**: every standable cell of every platform in all three rooms (202 start spots, walking off each side), at 30, 60 and 144 fps: all 202 end up grounded in the same room or in the next one through a door (never at the start of the game, never on a lost life, never off the board), and the bot then finishes the room from wherever it landed within 120 s: 0 failures at each frame rate. Findings the test surfaced and what they were: a hero that walks into the Room 2 pit with no steering **bounces on the trampoline forever** (the player's bounce never decays, 0.97 restitution plus a minimum launch; the other actors decay), so the pit is recoverable only by steering out, which is what a held direction does; a hero landing on the ledge's very left edge (x 19.4) was misread by the bot; the island's far right is past the flag (the flag is at column 22, the island ends at 23), so a bot, like a kid, must step back; these were test-controller issues, not level faults, except the pit bounce, which is expected trampoline behavior and noted.

**Other checks**: the 13 older samples are frame-identical (625 frames each) against the commit before Cloud Hopper (`ebd2077`); an autosave written by the pre-Cloud-Hopper build loads, plays and still opens Cloud Hopper from the gallery; screenshots of every room at 1366x635 and 1920x1080 (`prototypes/cloudhopper-shots/room{1,2,3}-{1366x635,1920x1080}.png`): 0 uncovered cells (every cell has a Paint tile or a skinned tile), 0 missing skin or Paint defs, the board fully inside the window with no page scroll. **First-frame test** (real time, no hooks): from the landing screen's "Play a game" pick, 235 ms after the click the canvas is 69% sky, grass is present (about 11,800 grass pixels) and the next sample 220 ms later already differs from the first in 7,600 pixels (coins bobbing, Mousecop moving; the clouds drift once play starts); the same numbers hold in the editor (Samples, then Play). So sky, grass and motion are all there inside one second.

**Not done**: an exported standalone game of Cloud Hopper was not played (it uses only already-exported pieces, but this was not verified); the fall test does not cover falling out of the Room 2 trampoline bounce mid-air (only landings); Mrs. Pecks was not tried as the hero (Gary was chosen).

The title stays "Cloud Hopper". No engine change. `tests/landing.test.js` passes (39; it runs one hero now). Before/after screenshots at 1366x768 and 1920x1080: `prototypes/landing-cloudhopper-shots/`. Not retested: the earlier hero-selection numbers (the previous three heroes were chosen by 20 s idle runs; Cloud Hopper's idle motion was checked by the landing test only: 20/20 s with motion, hero alive).

## Mousecop's Garden (v2.61), Stage 1: the maze and the dressing (no actors yet)

A wrap-around chase for small kids: a top-down garden, gravity off, **wrap on both axes**, fixed camera, one 24x18 room, Mrs. Pecks as the hero (Stage 2). `buildMousecopsGardenGame()` / `drawMousecopsGardenIcon`, registered **last** in `SAMPLE_GAMES` (id `mousecops-garden`; not in `LANDING_PICKS`/`LANDING_HEROES`). Stage 1 builds only the board; there is no hero, Mousecop, Wheebuns or coin yet (Stage 2). Stage 0 audit numbers that drive the design (measured, throwaway harness): hero 220 px/s, Mousecop/Wheebuns 160 px/s; a chaser steers straight at the hero with no pathfinding, so any wall between them pins it until the hero moves off its row/column (a 1-thick wall pins it even at a 1-tile offset; a 2x2 pillar, a U-pocket and a hedge ring pin it for good); the chaser ignores the seam (it runs the long way round) so the hero crossing a seam makes it reverse; the fleer runs straight away and stops at a wall, but in a wrapped room it keeps crossing the seam (47 crossings in 20 s); a wall in the cell on the far side of a seam blocks the crossing (hero pinned half off-screen at x = -4); a catch costs a life and restarts the room with every coin back; wrapped sprites slide out then pop in whole on the other side (no half-and-half drawing).

**Layout.** `MG_BLOCK_ROWS` x `MG_BLOCK_COLS` define twelve isolated hedge blocks (rows 3-5, 9-11, 15-16 by columns 3-5, 9-11, 15-17, 21-22; 88 hedge cells), separated by lanes at least 3 tiles wide (rows 0-2, 6-8, 12-14, 17; columns 0-2, 6-8, 12-14, 18-20, 23). The outermost row and column are open, so every seam crossing is a clear lane (across a seam a 1-wide edge lane joins a 3-wide one, 4 in all). No long walls, pockets or dead ends: blocks are at most 3x3. Rules: gravity off, wrap H and V, fixed camera.

**Dressing** (every cell has a Paint tile; hedge cells also carry a solid Ground skin). Hedges: six sample-scoped solid Ground textures (`mgHedgeA/B/Berry/Bloom/Top/Base`, art only, geometry solid, a kid can repaint with them; berry and blossom variants by a deterministic hash, a lighter rim on a block's top edge and a dark base on its bottom edge). Floor, all on the Paint layer: three lawn tiles plus a shaded one under a hedge (`mgLawnA/B/C/Shade`), three flower tiles beside hedges (`mgFlowerRed/Pink/Yellow`), sandy path tiles along the lane centre lines (rows 1, 7, 13 and columns 1, 7, 13, 19, `mgPath/mgPathB`) and stepping stones at the twelve crossings (`mgStones`). The greens and browns come from the Ground grass/dirt textures' own colours. **Deviation from the brief:** the shared sky backdrop and the cloud/hill/bush presets were not used (a top-down garden has no horizon; those presets carry a sky background that would show in the corners); the "sky/grass look" is the lawn built from the shared grass colours. Flag it if you want the sky preset somewhere.

Verified: the board has 0 empty cells and 0 missing defs (every cell a Paint tile or skinned hedge), 88 hedge cells, wrap [true,true], gravity off, fixed camera, 0 actors, no errors; screenshots at 1366x635 and 1920x1080 (board fits, no scroll) and two stitched seam pictures (right edge beside left edge, bottom beside top, magenta line = the seam) showing paths, lawn and flowers continuous across both; landing and layout suites pass; `toyDebug` count 0. Not done yet (Stages 2-3): the cast and win, speeds/start-safety tuning, the bot, wrap checks with actors, frame-identical sweep, old-autosave check, first-frame check. Screenshots: `prototypes/garden-shots/`.

### Mousecop's Garden, Stage 2: the cast and the rules

Added inside `buildMousecopsGardenGame()`, no engine change, no new piece, sound or rule. **Hero: Mrs. Pecks** (the Characters-tab preset's four poses copied onto the player def the way the swatch click does it). **Mousecop** = the built-in chaser def, `lethal = true` (a catch costs a life); **Wheebuns** = the built-in fleer, `lethal = false` (touching him just pops him, kept as decided). **Win = collect all 16 coins** (no goal tile exists, so full collection wins); one optional Heart (a spare hit) sits one lane up from the start and is not part of the win. A catch restarts the room with every coin back, as decided. McDinglechud is not in the garden (the Stage 0 audit: a random-heading lethal hazard is unreadable for a 6-year-old).

**Placement** (cells): hero (1,16), Mousecop (22,1), Wheebuns (13,7), Heart (1,13), coins on the path lines: row 1 cols 4/10/16, row 7 cols 4/10/16/22, row 13 cols 4/10/16/22, col 7 rows 4/10, col 13 rows 4/10, col 19 row 10. The coin trail along the paths is the only instruction; there is no text. `room.startPos` = the hero's cell.

**Fairness, measured (a hero who never moves is the worst case; the READY! freeze adds 1.2 s before any of this):** the first catch of an idle hero takes 6.5 s (it was 5.1 s with the hero at (1,13) and Mousecop at (19,1); the start was moved to the bottom-left corner lane and Mousecop to the far top-right, which puts hedge blocks between them, and a swept table of 27 start pairs is the basis: the chosen pair is the best "far and blocked" one that is not a trap). Speeds are not tuned because they cannot be without touching the engine: the hero is 220 px/s against Mousecop's and Wheebuns's 160 (1.375x), already the fairness rule asked for. A catch puts the hero back at the start with Mousecop back at his far corner, with READY!. No lethal tiles, no dead ends (no hedge pocket), lanes at least 3 wide, so the hero can never be cornered by geometry; Mousecop can be outwitted behind a hedge or through a seam.

Verified: all 14 older samples frame-identical to the previous commit (gravdir.test.js, 625 frames each; its one failure is the garden itself, which now has actors); scripted win: a hero teleported over each of the 16 coins with Mousecop parked wins (`playSessionEnded = "win"`, lives 3); a real-time Play screenshot (`prototypes/garden-shots/stage2-play-1366x635.png`) shows the garden, the hero, Mousecop, Wheebuns and the coins; landing 39 and layout 35 pass; `toyDebug` count 0. Not done (Stage 3): the bot (win rate, time to first catch, chaser-stuck and softlock counts), wrap checks with actors, frame-identical/old-autosave/first-frame checks, screenshots at both viewports with the cast.

### Mousecop's Garden, Stage 3: proof

Scripts (committed, not shipped): `tests/garden.bot.js [html] [runs] [bots|wrap|both]` (scripted bots + wrap checks with actors; `STRATS=a,b` picks strategies, `M0/SLOPE/FLOOR` tune the smart bot's risk margin), `tests/garden.check.js <pre-garden index.html> [shotDir]` (old autosave, gallery/first frame, coverage and screenshots at both viewports, export). Both use a hook-injected temp copy, `requestAnimationFrame` off, manual 60 fps ticks, `Math.random` seeded per run (only the fleer's wander uses it, so runs differ little). READY! has to be skipped by the harness (`tick` is frozen for it in the real loop).

**Bots** (results table in the report; 240 s of game time max per run): idle, hold-right, hold-diagonal, random keys, greedy (BFS to the nearest coin through the hedge lanes, wrap-aware, ignores Mousecop), smart (same but avoids a 2-cell zone round Mousecop) and smart2 (time-aware: a cell is usable only if the hero reaches it clearly before Mousecop's straight-line run could; keeps a target for half a second; its margin shrinks the longer it goes without a coin). Findings: every non-winning strategy loses all 3 lives (no softlock, no stalemate); idle is first caught at 6.5 s (+1.2 s READY!), greedy at 3.3 s, smart at 5.3 s; smart2 with margin 0.3 -> -0.2 wins 6/6 and 20/20 with zero catches in about 35 s. **The first smart2 (margin 0.55 -> 0) never won**: it hid at a seam while Mousecop ran back and forth across the board, the stalemate of a bot that is too timid, not a game softlock, and the same bot with a slightly smaller margin wins. A catch puts every coin back, so a win needs 16 coins in one life stretch (or a heart); the winning bot does it without ever being caught.
**Chaser stuck**: Mousecop never ends stuck for good; a chaser pinned behind a hedge block while the hero is far lasts seconds (the tuned winning bot saw 1 s in 6 runs) and ends when the hero moves. Parked in the middle with the hero in a corner it runs straight to the hero in about 8 s (checked from three hero/Mousecop pairs).
**Wrap checks** (real key states for the hero, momentum for the others, 15 corner placements): the hero crosses all four seams on a clear lane and keeps walking; Mousecop and Wheebuns cross all four seams when pushed (Mousecop keeps chasing afterwards, 100 to 480 px of movement after crossing); sprites pop whole on the far side (a jump of 598 px vertically, 768 px horizontally in one frame, never half and half); every actor placed in each of the four corner cells stays on the board for 8 s and the hero can leave every corner. Known and harmless: Wheebuns (the fleer) with the hero far away keeps flipping across a seam (flees straight, wraps, flees again), so he sits in the seam zone; he is harmless. A chaser cannot cross a seam by its own steering (it steers by raw dx/dy), only by being pushed.
**Other checks**: the 14 older samples are frame-identical to the commit before the garden (`NS=14 gravdir.test.js`, 8750 frames, now with an `NS` env var so the test can compare against a build that lacks the newest sample); an autosave written by the pre-garden build (Cloud Hopper, 3 rooms) loads in the new build into the editor and the garden is last in the gallery (15 cards); first frame about 0.7 s after the click is 65% green, 0% black, and a 250 ms-later frame already differs (coins bobbing and the pets), Play motion within 400 ms; 0 uncovered cells and 0 missing defs, board inside the window with no scroll at 1366x635 (646x486) and 1920x1080 (1158x870); the exported garden plays 4.5 s with held keys, no errors, and contains no sample builder. Screenshots: `prototypes/garden-shots/stage3-*.png`.

## Sample fixes: Worst Elevator room 2 ladder re-placed for the new cannon; Cheese Heist tidied (Pac-Man look)

**The World's Worst Elevator, room 2.** It was unsolvable since the cannon redesign (fixed arc: about 4 tiles up, 9 across, `ballistic` keeps vx 343 through the whole flight). Traced at 30/60/144 fps: the shot from the cannon at (5,13) hit the west face of the trampoline at (12,10) and bounced back. Edits to the embedded project (room 2 only): trampolines at (12,10), (15,8), (18,6) removed; trampolines added at (12,12) and (13,12) (the shot lands there at x about 12.8 on every frame rate and the player bounces about 8.7 tiles); the upper cannon moved from (18,3) to (16,5) (the bounce rises into its underside, its shot peaks at row about 1.3 and skims the top of the goal block); goal widened to a row, (20..22,1) (the apex is a knife edge against the block at (21..23,2), so a single goal tile missed at 30 and 60 fps, same lesson as before). The trampoline at (9,12) was kept. Because ballistic flight ignores input, all four input patterns (hold after the shot, release at the bounce, press only after it, never) win at all three frame rates; jumping into the first cannon from stand positions 142-172 px works, 185 px (only 7 px of overlap) misses. Not retraced: the Lobby (untouched).

**Cheese Heist.** The maze is now left-right symmetric (built from a mirrored left half, 1-thick wall lines, no dead ends, all open cells connected), the central chamber keeps its gaps top and bottom (through-passage, not a pen). Wall textures are chosen by neighbours (pipe pieces: straight, corners, T, cross; the old hand-picked skins are replaced). Cheese is 41 pieces in straight dotted lines on a 3-cell lattice (was 94 scattered), plus two bonus cheeses in the top corners (was three gems near the start); cats at (10,9) and (13,9), player start unchanged. Fairness is on par with the old layout (idle first catch 3.7 s, was 4.0 s). **Not proven winnable**: the scripted bots lose in both layouts (cats catch them within about 8 s of a life and a catch restarts every cheese); a smarter bot or a human playtest is still owed.

## Cheese Heist touch-ups; Mousecop's Garden becomes a 72-wide Follow-camera level with a fountain and a fence; vertical wrap no longer flip-flops

**Cheese Heist.** The two small vertical walls in the bottom middle now run down to the lower wall (cells (10,13..16) and (13,13..16)), as in the old layout, which splits the bottom lane and leaves a pocket between them. Eight cheeses added: one in each gap between the three horizontal bars on the middle left and middle right ((5,7), (5,9), (18,7), (18,9)), and, because the lane between each pair of mini vertical walls is two cells wide and one cheese can't sit in the centre, two side by side in the top gap ((11,2), (12,2)) and the bottom gap ((11,14), (12,14)). 49 cheeses in all. Idle first catch and the scripted bots behave as in the previous layout; still not proven winnable by a bot (human playtest owed).

**Mousecop's Garden** (`buildMousecopsGardenGame`). Camera is now Follow and the garden is the full 72 columns (hedge blocks and path columns repeat every 6 columns; the old 24-wide layout is the first third). A 3x3 fountain, one 24x24 picture cut into nine Paint tiles (`mgFountain00..22`), sits on the lane crossing at (36..38, 6..8): painted only, walkable. A full-height picket fence (`mgFence`, a solid Ground texture) stands in column 55, so the strip east of it (columns 56..71) can only be reached by walking off the west edge and wrapping round. Mousecop starts in that strip and, with the hero in the west, runs up and down the fence line (a chaser has no pathfinding); the hero arrives at column 71 when wrapping, about 15 tiles from it. 21 coins (15 west, 6 east of the fence), hearts at (1,13) and (67,7). Win is still collecting every coin. Traced: the hero can't pass the fence (stops at x 54.3), walking left from column 0 arrives at column 71 and the camera sweeps from 0 to 48 in about 0.3 s (not snapped; flag if it feels jarring), a path-following bot (wrap-aware BFS with a time-aware avoid of Mousecop) wins in about 33 s with no life lost for four different avoidance margins, `tests/garden.check.js` (15 checks incl. export) passes.

**The fleer "trapped on the bottom edge" was a real engine bug.** `applyWorldWrap` reset a non-rider leaving the top/bottom to `y = ROWS*TILE` or `y = -h`, and the opposite test fired again on the very next frame, so an actor with almost no vertical speed flip-flopped between the two spots, parked outside the board (traced: Wheebuns sat at y 18.00 / -0.69 moving only sideways, invisible). Vertical wrap now carries the overshoot (`y += / -= ROWS*TILE`) for everyone, exactly like the sideways rule from v2.50; the `riding` special case is gone since it is the same rule. The idle trace now shows the fleer visibly roaming and crossing the seam (about 10% of frames partly outside, all seam crossings). The other samples are frame-identical to before (625 frames each), including Crossing Guard; `tests/crossingguard.test.js` still has its two old export-anchor failures.

In Play, `triggerPlayerGravityWellCapture` no longer ends the game: the hero is swallowed (animation as before), then `scheduleLevelRetry()` restarts the room via the normal retry path after `LEVEL_RESET_DELAY_MS`, with lives unchanged. Build is unchanged (sent to start). ## "Event Horizon" (v2.62), Stage 1: Rooms 1 "Orbit" and 2 "Slingshot"

`buildEventHorizonGame()` / `drawEventHorizonIcon`, registered LAST in `SAMPLE_GAMES` (id `event-horizon`; working title; not in the landing lists). Coded sample, **no engine change** beyond the earlier black-hole retry (capture in Play = back to the room's start, no life lost). Existing pieces only: Black Hole, solid ground, doors. Rooms 3-5 (held magnet, two pulls, flipped finale), the exit door/flag and the proof pass are Stages 2-5.

**Look (all sample-scoped, nothing leaks into new projects):** a night-sky Paint kit per room (Orbit: indigo + amber planet; Slingshot: teal + green planet), 3 star variants per band, a 24x24 planet cut into nine Paint tiles, "beacon" lamps, hull-plate platform skins (steel for Orbit, rust for Slingshot), a ceiling trim row. Every cell has a Paint tile or a skin (0 empty, 0 missing defs, checked at 1366x635 and 1920x1080). **Pull rings** are Paint tiles banded by distance from each hole (`EH_BANDS` 7 / 5.5 / 4.5 / 3.3 tiles = pulls at all / stronger than a walk / lifts you against gravity / no way back), progressively violet to red; platforms inside the 7-tile reach wear a violet-tinted skin. The rings are drawn from the same distances the engine uses, so what a kid sees is what pulls.

**Room 1 (Orbit):** one hole at (12,14); a monotone diagonal staircase of 3-wide platforms (rise 2 each, no gap) climbs the left, a 3-wide bridge tops the arch at row 7 (exactly 7 tiles from the hole: out of reach) and the right side steps down to the door at (23,12). The staircase is diagonal on purpose: a platform two steps up that sits over the launch spot caps the jump (the zigzag draft failed every hop for that reason). The floor is open under the hole, so a miss drains into it (free retry). Hop sweep (5 takeoffs x 15 hold times, real engine, hole present): every hop has a window of good hold times 0.25-0.45 s wide (the first hop from the far end of the start ledge needs a run-up, the near 60% of it works).

**Room 2 (Slingshot):** the hero walks off a high ledge (cols 0-6, row 6) and the big hole at (12,9) swings them down onto a wide platform (row 15, cols 9-16, then steps at rows 13 and 11 to the door at (23,10)). Measured in the built room at 30, 60 and 144 fps: every walk-off (any takeoff column, any hold >= 0.8 s) lands on that platform; the only "misses" were holds so short the hero never reached the edge. A second hole at (1,17) is a drain: a hero that misses falls into it and retries free (no stuck state). Slingshot is easy by design (a robust funnel); the harder rooms follow.

**Honest notes:** (1) the "hold" window numbers are over a grid of takeoffs and hold times, not a proof; Stage 4 owns the solver/bot with margins at 30/60/144 fps. (2) The retry on capture does not lose a life (the user's call); everything else that costs a life is unchanged. (3) Room 2's pull and the drain overlap slightly at the platform's left end (distance about 7 from the drain), reading as a faint ring. Verified: older samples frame-identical (`NS=15 gravdir.test.js`, 90/90), landing 39, layout 35, `toyDebug` count 0. Screenshots: `prototypes/eventhorizon-shots/`. Not done: first-frame test, old-autosave check, sample-modal opening via the UI, export play (Stage 5).

The two bonus-lane cheeses at (11,14),(12,14) moved to row 15 to mirror the top pair at row 2. Data-only change (`CHEESE_HEIST_BOARD/SKINS/CHEESE_CELLS`); every open cell is still connected.
