## "Event Horizon" (v2.62), Stages 2-5: Rooms 3-5, doors, proof

Five fixed 24x18 rooms now: Orbit, Slingshot, **Tug**, **Two pulls**, **Flip side** (door pairs 2<->3, 3<->4, 4<->5, arriving one cell inside). Existing pieces only; the one engine change remains the earlier "black-hole capture in Play = free retry". Each room has its own space mood (`tug` magenta, `pull` green, `flip` blue) plus the shared planet/lamps/pull-ring Paint bands.

- **Room 3 "Tug" (the magnet room), a deviation from the brief worth knowing.** A magnet-held crate is NOT a stepping stone: a hero landing on it displaces it out of the 8 px grip (traced). So the magnet is a *catcher feeding a mid-air plate*: a crate-loaded spawner (`spawnerContents` = `PUSHABLE_DEF_ID`; the default content is a hurting hazard ball) fires east, the magnet catches the crate over a red plate, the plate opens the red Gate on the walkway, and the hero never touches the crate. A lava pit and shelf swallow surplus crates so only the held one remains. Gate opens at about 2.5 s and stays open at 30/60/144 fps. A hole at (14,14) pulls under the walkway.
- **Room 4 "Two pulls"**: holes at (6,16) and (17,16), Orbit-style staircase between them, floor drains into the holes (free retry).
- **Room 5 "Flip side"**: Orbit turned 180 degrees with `gravityDir = "up"` (Left/Right keys are mirrored on screen by the existing rule), hole at (11,3), goal flag at (0,5).
- **Jump rules held**: every required step <= 2 rows (64 px of the 122 px worst-case apex), gaps <= 4; takeoff must be before the platform edge (edge takeoffs bonk the platform above); a platform within ~3 tiles above a launch spot caps the jump; landing inside a strong pull band drags the hero toward the hole along the platform.
- **Proof.** `tests/eventhorizon.bot.js` (scripted hops + real doors + wait for the gate) wins with 3 lives at 30/60/144 fps (27/27 variants of release/timing). `tests/eventhorizon.hops.js` sweeps hold times x 5 takeoffs per hop (windows are 0.25-0.45 s wide in Rooms 1/4/5). Every miss ends in a hole or floor drain = retry; no state found where the hero is stuck. `tests/eventhorizon.check.js <old index.html>`: old autosave loads, opens from the Samples gallery (last card), first frame has the space backdrop and animated hole, 0 empty cells/missing defs, board inside window at 1366x635 and 1920x1080, exported game plays (32 checks). `tests/eventhorizon.shots.js` regenerates `prototypes/eventhorizon-shots/`. Regression: gravdir (15 samples, 625 frames, identical) 90/90, landing 39, layout 35, stage1 36.
- **Not measured / caveats**: a human playtest; the hop windows are grids not proofs; the exported game was played for 4 s with a held key only (not a full run); the bot teleports to takeoff spots rather than walking between hops; harness traps: `requestAnimationFrame` is off so screenshots need `renderGame()`, a capture retries to the checkpoint room (re-activate the room per trial), and `lethaltile.test.js` has 2 pre-existing failures.

## Event Horizon: the user's edited layouts, re-dressed; rooms renamed

The five rooms now take their boards, channel colours, crates, spawner and hero from the user's hand-edited project (`my-project_19.json`, embedded as `EH_LAYOUT` inside `buildEventHorizonGame`). Everything cosmetic is regenerated by the same art pass as before, driven by where the holes really are (read from the `gravitywell` tiles): space backdrop, pull-ring bands, tinted hull skin for platforms inside a pull, lamps, planet. So future layout edits only need the board data swapped. Room names: Orbit, **Vortex** (was Slingshot), **Slingshot** (was Tug), **Quad Pulls** (was Two pulls), Flip side. Earlier "Tug"/"Two pulls" notes describe the old layouts; the Stage 2-5 bot/hop scripts target those and were NOT re-run on the new layouts (the new rooms were not re-solved). Fixed on the way: stray `ehflip` skins and bare Paint cells in the edited rooms, Flip side's start position (0,0 -> its door cell). `tests/eventhorizon.check.js` (32) passes with the new names.

## Enemy row: every enemy is its own slot, labelled by name

The Characters tab's Enemy row used to show ONE built-in enemy whose look/behavior followed whichever built-in kind was selected, so changing the first slot's behavior just selected a different built-in and the slot "reverted". Now each enemy is a real def (`enemyType`, in `customActorSlotIds`) with its own behavior (Behavior row), lethal/harmless and look, and the "+" (`addEnemyType`) adds as many more as wanted; changes persist per enemy. An untouched project shows a stand-in first slot (not stored, so a fresh project still counts as empty); clicking it creates the real enemy. Labels come from `enemyLookName(def)`: the name of the look it wears (McDinglechud / Mousecop / Wheebuns / Thelonius) while it still has its behavior's built-in art, else its own name; no "Enemy 2". New enemies copy the wanderer's lethal setting (so the tutorial's harmless wanderer carries over). The tutorial's enemy step now targets the first swatch of `#characterSwatches` and accepts an `enemyType` selection; tests use `.swatch:first-child`. stage1 36, tutorial2 99, tutorialtargets 244, tutoriallook 45 pass.

## Enemy row, round two: behavior never touches the sprite or the name

Each enemy slot is an independent (sprite, behavior, lethal) triple. A Behavior click now only sets `temperament` (the old rule that swapped built-in art on a behavior change is gone). The slot name belongs to the sprite: stored on the def, set from the preset when one is picked from the shelf, and for old/generic names ("wanderer", "Enemy 2", "Actor B") `enemyLookName` resolves it from the art (built-in looks, then presets), defaulting to McDinglechud; a Behavior click freezes the resolved name. The first slot reads "McDinglechud" from the start. "+" still clones the wanderer look, so new slots start with the same name until a sprite is picked. Test: `node tests/enemypanel.test.js` (5 checks). stage1 36 passes; the tutorial suites were not re-run for this change.

## Enemy panel audit: a built-in enemy selection no longer "reverts" on a Behavior click

Audit of the whole enemy path (stand-in slot, `addEnemyType`, Behavior row, lethal row, preset shelf, names, snapshot, tutorial). Findings: (1) the one remaining revert: if a *built-in* enemy kind was selected (old `lastCharacterActorKind`, the tutorial, a restored tab), the Behavior row called `selectActorKind(temp)` and just jumped to another built-in. Now a Behavior click on a built-in selection makes its own enemy slot (same sprite and lethal setting, new behavior) and leaves the built-in def untouched. (2) Everything else held: behavior, lethal and sprite are independent per slot; a preset renames and re-skins only the selected slot; legacy names ("Enemy 2", "wanderer") resolve from the sprite; the triples survive a snapshot round trip. Known, left alone: "+" always clones the wanderer look, so new slots share the name McDinglechud until a sprite is picked; the lethal row on a built-in edits that built-in def directly (old saves keep working). Tests: `tests/enemypanel.test.js` (10 checks), stage1 36, tutorial2 (1366x768) 57, landing 39 pass; not re-run: tutorial3/targets/look, lethalnpc (crashes before this change), export of an enemy slot.

## Enemy panel, round three: Wheebuns default, names everywhere, no Player behavior, voices hidden

The first (stand-in) enemy slot and every "+" slot wear the **Wheebuns** look (fleer art) but start as wanderers (lethal copied from the built-in wanderer); `enemyLookName` falls back to Wheebuns. The remaining "Enemy 2" was the Behavior header (`#customActorEditingName`), which printed the raw stored name; it now uses `enemyLookName`. The Behavior row is exactly Chaser/Wanderer/Fleer/Statue (the "Player" option is gone from the UI; `makeCustomActorThePlayer` stays unused). The player's swatch label is `playerLookName` (the stored default "hero" resolves to the preset it wears, e.g. Gary) and applying a preset to the player renames the def. **Voices are hidden**: the voice block is always `display:none`; `renderVoicePicker`, `def.voice`, `VOICE_PATCHES`, `playActorSound` are untouched. No built-in or embedded sample def has a voice set (grep), so every character currently uses the generic reaction sounds; a voice would only play through `playActorSound` at a chaser catch (gotcha), a harmless-NPC bump (boop/oof/pop), a hazard hit (yelp) and splat deaths. Tests: `tests/enemypanel.test.js` (15 checks), stage1 36, tutorial2 (1366x768) 57.

## Enemy slot looks are a fixed sequence

`ENEMY_SLOT_LOOKS` decides what the nth enemy slot wears when "+" (or the stand-in click) creates it: Wheebuns, Gloop, Mousecop, McDinglechud, crabby, Thelonius, Bonesy (skeleton), crabby; the 9th and later get a random `CHARACTER_PRESETS` entry (so it can be Gary, Princess, Hero... too). The count is the number of existing enemy slots, so deleting is not a concern (there is no delete). Behavior still starts as wanderer, lethal copied from the built-in wanderer. The request listed crabby twice (5th and 8th); built as written. Test: `tests/enemypanel.test.js` (fresh-page order check).

**Update:** the 8th slot is Grumble (the monster preset), not a second crabby. Slot 7 is the skeleton and stays named Bonesy (shown as such in the Enemy row and its tooltip). From the 9th on, the look is a random `CHARACTER_PRESETS` entry, hero-style presets included (accepted).

## Cloud Hopper and Event Horizon touch-ups

Cloud Hopper: the Meadow's Mousecop starts at col 9 (was 7, hero at 2). The Climb's and Sky Island's enemies were fleers (touching one pops it, so they read as collectibles); they are now built-in wanderers, and the wanderer def is non-lethal inside the builder like the chaser and fleer. Event Horizon, room "Slingshot": the three extra crates at (4,12), (5,11), (5,12) are gone (the crate at (7,4) stays), a second spawner faces west at (21,15) (third column and third row from the bottom-right corner), and both spawners now fire the default red hazard ball (no loaded crate). A plate is held by any actor overlapping it, so a hazard ball presses plates like a crate, but only while it overlaps (it bounces, so it flickers a channel instead of holding it) and it also hurts the player. Checked by loading both samples (actor lists, spawner tile, balls appearing after 6 s, no errors); the Slingshot puzzle was not re-solved with balls instead of crates.

## "My sprites": drawn custom characters become looks for the player and enemies

A new block (`#mySpritesBlock`, `renderMySpriteSwatches`, right under "Grab a sprite") lists every custom character made with the custom-character "+" whose picture is not empty. It shows only while the selected character is the player or an enemy slot, never for the custom character itself, and stays hidden when there is nothing to offer. Clicking one copies its picture (and poses) onto the selected character and takes its name, exactly like a built-in look from the shelf; drawing over a sprite still edits only the selected character, and the custom original is never changed. No new behavior or selection mode and no saved-data change (custom characters were already saved and exported). `enemyLookName` now returns a stored "Actor A"-style name when the art matches nothing, instead of falling back to "Wheebuns". Only characters from the "+" count, not sprites drawn on the player or an enemy slot. Test: `node tests/mysprites.test.js` (10 checks); enemypanel and stage1 still pass. There is no rename for custom characters yet, so they read "Actor A", "Actor B"...

## Naming characters and rooms

**Characters.** The "Pixel art (8×8) — editing: ..." heading is now a text box (`#editingNameInput`) whenever the selected character is the player, an enemy slot or a custom "+" character; built-in objects and Paint/Ground tiles keep a plain label. Typing renames live (the swatch labels follow), Enter or clicking away finishes, an empty name keeps the old one, and the behavior words (`wanderer`, `chaser`, ...) are refused because `findDefaultActorDef` matches built-in defs by name. The name travels with a "My sprites" look when it is applied.

**Rooms.** Double-click a room tab to turn it into a text box (`startRoomRename`): Enter or clicking away keeps the name (24 characters max, one undo step, autosaved), Escape cancels, an empty name keeps the old one. The name is plain room data, so saves, exports and samples carry it. Editor only.

Tests: `tests/mysprites.test.js` (names, 16 checks), `tests/roomname.test.js` (9 checks); stage1, enemypanel and the v2.51 layout suite still pass.

The character name box now sits centered directly above the pixel canvas (inside `#pixelEditor`, same width as the canvas) instead of in the heading; while it is shown the heading reads just "Pixel art (8×8)".

**Name box, round two.** Only custom "+" characters can be renamed (the player, enemy slots and every built-in object/tile keep their names; an enemy or player wearing a "My sprites" look takes that sprite's name but isn't editable). The box now updates whenever the selection or look changes: the label/box sync lives in `updateEditingLabel()`, called from `updateModeButtons` and the end of `renderAll` (before, a sprite grab only called `renderAll`, so the box kept the old name). Look: it is styled as a title, not a field (bold centered text, a dashed underline that turns solid accent on focus, a small ✎ at the right, transparent background). `tests/mysprites.test.js` updated.

## Surprise 4661 (Mini Level 1) magnets re-linked; vertical wrap restored for the player

- **Magnets lost their colors in Surprise Me.** Mini Level 1's four magnets are red/orange/green/blue in the author's project (each paired with a same-colored Switch), but the entry predates colored magnets and stored them as plain `magnet`, so they were always on and ignored the Switches. New piece `magnet:<color>` (resolver and serializer both handle it; a plain `magnet` is unchanged); `minilevel1` now uses `N/O/Q/R` = red/orange/green/blue. It was the only authored room with colored magnets (checked every JSON in the repo root). Magnet physics is untouched: `tests/magnet.test.js` passes, every sample is frame-identical (`gravdir.test.js`), `exact` 228/0, `surprise5` passes.
- **Over-Engineered Escape regression found on the way (not magnets).** Since the Garden commit (21f8d58) vertical wrap carried the overshoot for everyone, which broke the Escape's recorded solution (5 checks failed, unnoticed because only the export-anchor failure was being watched). The Escape's route relies on the original placement when the player wraps (exit the top, appear below, hop to the top). `applyWorldWrap` now uses the original reset-to-edge for the player and mover riders and the carry for every other actor (the Garden fleer fix is kept). Escape is back to its previous result (63 checks, only the old `export boot anchor` failure). Side effect: the Garden hero crossing a vertical seam behaves as before 21f8d58 (frame 453 of the idle script differs from HEAD).

## Over-Engineered Escape: the top-left cannon shot is blocked at high refresh rates (embed guard counted frames)

Reported: the uppermost cannon (0,1), which fires the player onto the top conveyor, "no longer works; something blocks the upward push". Traced with a hook harness (`/tmp` scratch, trampoline bounce into the cannon with the green/blue gates open, 30 to 360 fps):
- **Current cause, only at >= ~200 fps:** `keepPlayerOnBoard`'s "embedded in solid" guard counted *frames* (`PLAYER_EMBED_FRAMES` 12). A cannon shot starts inside the (blocking) cannon cell and needs about 50 ms to leave it, which is 12 frames at 240 fps, so the guard teleported the player back to its last clean spot ("Back on the board!") on frame 12 of the flight (traced: x 19.3, y 13 -> x 5, y 66 at 240 fps, repeating every shot). It worked at 30 to 165 fps. Fix: the counter now adds `dt * 60` (capped at 2 per frame), i.e. 12 frames of 60 fps *time*; at 60 fps it is unchanged. Result: the shot lands on the belt at every rate tested (30, 60, 120, 144, 240, 300, 360).
- **Earlier, 144 fps and others:** between the Garden wrap commit (21f8d58) and 8501830 the same shot failed at 144/170/200 fps too (player stuck at x 45); that was the vertical-wrap regression already fixed and documented above, not this guard.
- Frame-count constants elsewhere were not audited; this is the one that surfaced. Tests: `overengineered` (only the old export-anchor failure), `deathdig` 19, `cannonlanding` 8.

## Landing demo plays itself (autopilot)

The landing backdrop (Cloud Hopper) used to run with no input, so the harmless chaser just bumped a motionless hero. `landingAutoplay(dt)` (called from `frame()` only while `landingActive`, before `tick`) now drives the hero through the three rooms to the flag: a per-frame port of `tests/cloudhopper.bot.js` as a queue of small actions (walk to x, hop until x, hold a key, pit bounce, wait for the moving platform). It only sets `keys` like the keyboard would and decides from the hero's position, so a missed jump just retries. After the win (or a lost run) it waits 2 s and calls `restartPlaySessionFromSnapshot()`, so the demo loops (about 18 s per run, measured over 70 s: 3 clean runs, no lives lost, no errors). The win/game-over overlay is not drawn behind the title screen. It runs only when the landing hero is `cloud-hopper`; any other hero stays hands-off. Landing/editor only: `frame` is shared with exports but the call is behind `landingActive`, which is always false there. Reduced-motion users still get the frozen first frame. Tests not re-run beyond a 75 s real-time check at 1366x768; the old bot's frame-rate sweep (30/60/144 fps) was not repeated for this port.

## Player landing thud only after a big fall

`triggerImpact` no longer plays the landing thud for the player on ordinary landings: it needs an impact speed of `PLAYER_THUD_MIN_SPEED` (735, just under terminal fall speed `MAX_FALL` 750, reached after about 6 tiles of fall). Checked on a flat floor: drops of 1, 3 and 5 tiles are silent, 7 and 10 tiles thud once. The squash/wobble animation is unchanged, and objects (crates, bombs...) keep their old thud rule; contact sounds with objects, bounces, hazards etc. are untouched. Export: one `constantsSource` line.

## Anonymous usage counters (v2.63; removable, off unless `?study=1`)

Added for a one-off classroom study (first-graders, no way to ask them anything) to see where children get stuck.
**Counters only**: named events with coarse values, no id of any kind, no cookie, no text, drawing, coordinates, url or error message.
Everything lives in ONE block in `index.html`, `// ===== TELEMETRY (removable) =====` to `// ===== END TELEMETRY =====`
(just before the Boot section). The rest of the game never calls anything but `tele(name, value)`.

- **Switch**: `TELEMETRY_MODE` = `"off"` | `"flag"` (ships as this: active only when the url has `?study=1`) | `"on"`.
  Opt-outs, always honored: `?nostats`, Do Not Track, Global Privacy Control. Mode off or opted out = `tele` is a no-op:
  no listeners, no timers, no requests, no stored flag.
- **Transport**: one plain GET per event to `TELEMETRY_ENDPOINT` (GoatCounter, site `tinygamemaker`), `/count?e=true&p=/e/<event>[/<value>]`,
  `fetch` with `keepalive`, `credentials: "omit"`, `referrerPolicy: "no-referrer"`. Events wait in memory and go out every 8 s and when the
  page is hidden; no retry, nothing persisted. Caps: 25 per event name, 150 per page. The browser unavoidably sends IP and user-agent
  headers to any server; our code puts neither in the address (GoatCounter says it does not store raw IPs: verify its privacy page).
- **Events** (the only ones): `visitor_new`/`visitor_returning` (one stored boolean `toyGameDesigner.seen`, same value for everyone; blocked
  storage sends neither), `landing_choice/<make|samples|tutorial|surprise>`, `sample_opened/<sample id>`, `surprise_me_used`, `first_placement`,
  `first_play`, `play_started`, `win`, `death/<0|1|2-3|4+>` (once, when the page is hidden), `restart`, `tutorial_started`,
  `tutorial_step_reached/<n>`, `tutorial_finished`, `tutorial_quit`, `save_used`, `share_used` (the Share button, formerly "Export game"),
  `session_length/<bucket>` (when hidden), `error_class/<type name>`, `viewport_class`, `browser_family`, `touch_present`.
  Values are checked against fixed lists in `tele`; anything else is dropped.
- **How it observes**: passively. A delegated click listener on button ids, a `pointerdown/up` pair on the canvas, `visibilitychange`, and one
  1-second `setInterval` reading `appMode`, `playSessionEnded`, `lives`, `tutorialActive`, `tutorial.curName`. Nothing per frame. Gameplay
  events are ignored while `landingActive` (the landing demo plays itself) and `first_placement` while a tutorial runs. The only tagged
  call sites outside the block are the two `tele("sample_opened", sample.id) // TELEMETRY` lines (sample gallery card, `landingPickSample`).
- **Never in exports**: nothing is in `runtimeFunctions`/`constantsSource`; verified an exported file contains none of it.
- **Reading the counts**: the GoatCounter dashboard (`tinygamemaker.goatcounter.com`), one row per event path per day.
- **Parent-readable line**: `#teleNote` in the landing footer, shown only while counting is active.
- **Disable**: set `TELEMETRY_MODE = "off"` (or just never use `?study=1`).
- **Remove entirely (about 5 minutes; rehearsed, 169 changed lines, game otherwise unchanged)**: (1) delete the block between the two
  `TELEMETRY` marker comments; (2) in the sample gallery tile click handler replace `{ closeSamplesModal(); tele("sample_opened", sample.id); } // TELEMETRY`
  with `closeSamplesModal();`; (3) delete the `tele("sample_opened", sample.id); // TELEMETRY` line in `landingPickSample`; (4) delete the
  `<div id="teleNote" ...>` line and the two `#teleNote` CSS lines; (5) delete `tests/telemetry.test.js`. Then `grep -n -i "tele(\|telemetry\|teleNote\|goatcounter" index.html` must print nothing.
  The Share rename of the toolbar button is not part of telemetry and stays.
- **Test**: `node tests/telemetry.test.js` (not shipped; network capture of a full session, kill switch, opt-outs, collector down/slow/blocked/500/offline,
  blocked storage, save comparison, tick time). Test traps: the sample cards are `.sampleTile`; after Surprise Me a dialog can block later clicks;
  a live `impactWobbleT` makes two snapshots differ. Failed collector requests print "Failed to load resource" lines in the browser console (Chrome's own
  log, not page errors, not suppressible).

## Surprise Me: "The Great Tailwind Derby" (`windrally`), the first wide Follow-camera entry

A whole-room entry that is 72 columns wide with a Follow camera: four lanes of `gust:e` wind carry the player and three different harmless chasers (slime, mouse, skeleton looks, `sx~windrally~*` defs) to a chequered finish at column 67. Wind cells hold one direction each, so paths cannot truly cross; the crossovers are cannons (`k`/`K`, fixed diagonal, a speed boost and mid-air collisions) that fire racers into a neighbouring lane, plus opened wall rows with cross-wind `s`/`n` cells that dump racers into a lane. Nobody needs input: it plays itself out (traced at 30/60/144 fps; the player wins at about 5.3 s from a 5-tile handicap start, chasers finish 6.8 to 7.6 s; the game ends on the player's win). Row 17 is an open grass strip (Paint only) so the room is not "closed".

Engine support added: `room.camera: "follow"` marks a wide entry (`surpriseIsWide`): it only fits an empty room or goes into a new room tab, gets the full 72-column context, sets `cameraMode = "follow"`, no fillers. `exact.backRows`/`backLegend` paint a Paint-layer picture from rows; `actor|@id` pieces resolve to the entry's own namespaced actors; `exact.wallSkin`. `tests/scenes.test.js` was told to accept `actor|@` and `sx~` ids.

## Surprise rooms open at their starting layout; stacked pictures no longer sink

Reported: whole-room surprises (Mini Level 1 / #9437, Bye Bye Cupcake) opened "already partly run": bombs off their magnets, gates open, and the hero not the cupcake. **Cause of the open gates:** channel state is project-wide by colour (`switchChannels`), so a Button an earlier room's pet or player had flipped stayed on for every later surprise using that colour. Fix: `surpriseApplySpec` deletes `switchChannels[color]` for every coloured tile it places, so a new arrangement starts with its gates shut. **Hero look:** `surpriseApplyExactLook` only replaced the factory/surprise-given look, so after loading a sample or picking any preset the author's hero (the cupcake) was skipped; `surpriseLookIsStock` now also accepts any `CHARACTER_PRESETS` look or any template's `playerLook`, still never a kid's own drawing (tested with a one-pixel edit). **Frozen start:** a press whose template is `category: "room"` or `authored` opens with Build paused (`setGamePaused(true)`, log says "ready, press P to start it"); P or Resume starts it, Play always starts running as before. This is a deliberate exception to "always live" for whole-room events (flag if unwanted); smaller surprises still run at once. `tests/pause.test.js` uses a non-room template for "Surprise Me resumes" and checks the room freeze; new `tests/lockedin.test.js` (5 checks).

**Stacked actors sink (drawing only):** the stand-on lift (`actorLiftShown`) eased at 160 px/s, so a stone landing on a stone, or the hero landing on a stone, was drawn 10 to 30 px inside it for several frames before the picture caught up (cumulative up a stack). Rising is now instant (`target > st.v`), only a drop eases. Boxes and physics are untouched (a 40 s Mini Level 3 run shows zero stone-stone box overlap; the effect was purely the 32 px picture on a 22 px box). The pre-contact picture overlap of a falling stone (up to 10 px at touchdown) is still there.

**The Great Tailwind Derby is ~3.5% of presses**, so 50 presses can miss it (about 17% of the time). `surpriseMe(7)` or `#surprise=7` (also 64, 72) builds it.

## v2.64 Stage 1: "Magnet Mile", a whole-room relay machine starring the magnet (Surprise Me; Stages 2-3 not done)

`magnetmile` (category `room`, `ext: true`, whole 24x18 room) joins `SURPRISE_ROOMS`. Existing pieces only, no engine change. A crate is handed along a floor of
eight magnets (period-4 colours red/orange/yellow/green, plates under each hold cell light the next magnet), is fired by a cannon over a sticky floor, drops a bomb
onto a pile, a brown gate opens, a Large Stone and crates fall into a one-wide shaft and squash the stooge (`expect.chain` declared in the entry).
**Seed split:** `SURPRISE_EXT_SEED_MIN` (10000): templates flagged `ext` are only in the pool for seeds >= 10000 (the dice now draws 5-digit seeds), so every existing seed 1..9999
still builds exactly what it did. Explicit `templateId(s)` ignores the filter. Not asked for by the user; chosen to keep old seeds stable (flag it).
**Design rules found by tracing:** colours are global to the room (every lit colour lights every magnet of that colour), so the cycle must be period 4 (period 3 leaves a backwards-pulling magnet in range);
one relay crate; hold cell = the cell before the magnet; the pen colour must be unique; the stooge shaft must be one tile wide (a two-wide one let the pet walk out from under the falling crate).
**Harness:** `tests/causal.hook.js` gained a `handoff` event (magnet-to-magnet), `tests/machines.activity.js` measures activity (chain events in 10 s, moving things, box %), `node tests/machines.rel.js index magnetmile 20 30,60,144 40`.
Result: chain completes 20/20 at 30, 60 and 144 fps (about 20 s), activity OK (19 chain events and 7-8 movers in 10 s, box 94%). `examples/magnet-mile.json` is the loadable project. Filmstrip: `prototypes/machines-shots/`.

## v2.64 Stage 2: "Everything Goes Down The Drain" (`drain`), a whole-room machine starring the black hole

`drain` (category `room`, `ext: true`, 24x18, gravity down) joins `SURPRISE_ROOMS`. Existing pieces only, no engine change. A crate spawner drops crates onto an east belt; at the belt end the black hole (16,8) pulls the crate
into a swing that drops it on a red plate (chain beltCarry, pull, plateOn); the plate opens the red gate of a pen at the upper right (gateOpen, released), the hole's pull drags the released pets off the pen ledge and eats them
(capture). A pet spawner inside the pen (`spawnerp:s`, drops pets straight down so they don't fly past the hole) keeps feeding the drain: one capture about every 2.5 s, so it runs for as long as the room does.
Declared chain: `beltCarry, pull, plateOn, gateOpen, released, capture`; completes 30/30 at 30, 60 and 144 fps, first capture about 4.6 s, 7 chain events and 11 movers in 10 s, box 100%, hero 14 tiles from the hole in a walled pocket (never pulled).
**Facts found by tracing:** a thing falling or flying past the hole faster than 240 px/s is redirected, not eaten, so wave-2 pets that fell from a high ledge missed it; things that WALK off a ledge at hole height are eaten. A crate resting within about 4.6 tiles of the hole is lifted (pull beats gravity), so the plate sits 6+ tiles below it
and the pile self-limits at 2 high. Sliding crates stop about 2 tiles past where they land, so the plate is 3 wide there. A pet standing on the plate region would trigger the gate before the first crate (cause null), so the stooge walker is inside the pen instead.
The payload swap is the pen's pets (`softchaser` or `walker`); a stone payload was not used (stone is not pulled, so the chain would break). Names: Everything Goes Down The Drain, The Great Plughole, Flush Hour, Swirl Of Events.
`tests/machines.activity.js` now follows the LONGEST cause path to the final event type (a stream of repeated final events used to pick a later, shorter path). `examples/drain.json` is the loadable project, filmstrip in `prototypes/machines-shots/drain-film-*.png`.

## v2.64 Stage 3: "Stone Cold Domino" (`stonedomino`), a whole-room machine starring the Large Stone

`stonedomino` (category `room`, `ext: true`, 24x18, gravity down) joins `SURPRISE_ROOMS`. Existing pieces only, no engine change. A stone spawner (`spawners:s`) drops stones onto a west belt that ends in a black hole (a drain, so the room never clogs);
the first stone rolls over a red Button (stones flip Buttons like any physics object), the red gate under a second stone opens like a trapdoor (the gate is a floor), the stone falls 6 rows onto a bomb actor (a stone falling 2+ tiles sets a bomb off by impact),
the blast clears the support of a third stone, which falls down a one-wide shaft onto the pet standing in it (squash).
Declared chain: `spawn, beltCarry, buttonPress, gateOpen, fall, impact, detonate, blastClear, fall, squash`; completes 30/30 at 30, 60 and 144 fps, squash at about 6.4 s, 11 chain events and 7 movers in 10 s, box 100%, hero in a walled pocket far from the blast and the hole.
**Facts found by tracing / choices:** a stone's horizontal speed is capped near the belt's 160 px/s, so a stone-shoved crate leaves at about 180 (under the 240 needed to detonate a bomb): the bomb is set off by the stone FALLING, not by a shove. Crates preloaded on the belt must be downstream (west) of the Button or they flip it first.
The Button is a toggle, so later stones alternately close and reopen the gate (visible activity, nothing falls after the first run); the room keeps running (a stone every 2.5 s rides the belt and is drained), but the big chain happens once per Play or Reset: that is the "resettable" part, there is no automatic second run.
Payload swap: the belt crate for a pet (`crate`/`pet`). Names: Stone Cold Domino, The Heavyweight Chain, Rock Paper Boom, Somebody Dropped A Rock. `examples/stone-domino.json`, filmstrip `prototypes/machines-shots/stonedomino-film-*.png`.

## v2.64 Stage 4: proof and integration of the three new machines

- **Reliability** (`node tests/machines.rel.js index <id> 100 30,60,144 40`): Magnet Mile, Everything Goes Down The Drain and Stone Cold Domino each complete their declared chain in order 100/100 at 30, 60 and 144 fps (about 20.0 s, 4.6 s and 6.4 s), 0 errors.
- **Variation fuzz** (`TEMPLATE=<id> VARY='{"payload":true,"mirror":true}' node tests/causal.test.js 60 index.html /dev/null 900`, chaos on, forced mirror and payload swap): honest chain 100%, box >= 94%, all three pass. Gravity directions are not applicable (fixed "on", down).
- **Nothing existing changed:** every sample is frame-identical to the pre-v2.64 build (`node tests/gravdir.test.js <pre-v2.64 index.html>`, 91/0); seeds 1..3000 build the identical Surprise spec in the old and new builds (`node tests/seedstable.test.js <pre-v2.64 index.html>`, 3000/3000, because `ext` templates are only in the pool for seeds >= 10000);
  surprise5, surprise2, scenes and exact suites pass; an autosave written by the pre-v2.64 build opens in the new build with the same boards as in the old build, and a machine can be pressed on top of it (`node tests/machines.final.js <pre-v2.64 index.html> <dir>`, which also writes the 1366x635 and 1920x1080 screenshots in `prototypes/machines-shots/final-*.png`; board inside the window, no scroll, no errors).
- **Share of machines:** among dice presses (seeds 10000+, 4000 presses) templates with a declared chain were 6.5% (3 templates) and are now 17.5% (6 templates). The new machines weigh 2 each (3 was tried: 5.5% each, too dominant): Magnet Mile 4.2%, Stone Cold Domino 4.0%, Drain 3.9% of presses. Retune `weight` in the entries.

## Moving Platform: a rider is never carried into a wall; Moving Platform Maze example replaced

`examples/moving-platform-maze.json` is now the user's new mini level (`moving-platform_mini_level.json`, room "Moving Platform Maze"; the old example is in git history).

**Bug reported with it: the player "jumps or warps" when riding a Moving Platform next to a wall.** Reproduced (`node tests/platformrider.test.js`): a platform ignores tiles (its position is a formula), so it slides into a wall carrying its rider; the riding step in `tick()` skipped the wall check for the player (`!rider.isPlayer && ...`), embedded the player in the wall, and next frame `resolveAxis` threw them out the far side: up onto the top of the wall (37px in one frame). Fix: the carry now applies to every rider but never INTO a tile (try the full move, then x only, then y only, else none), so a rider is held at the wall while the platform slides underneath and is carried again when it comes back. Second fix in `shovePushables`' platform branch: a platform sliding sideways into a standing-still actor (v = 0) used to leave the overlap unresolved until gravity snapped them on top in one frame; they are now eased to the nearer side (only when the overlap along gravity is over 6px, so a rider on top is never shoved sideways).

**The "sandwich" snap, found and fixed (second round).** The leftover 28px snap the fuzz kept finding was not a wall sandwich but a *graze*: a platform edge overlapping a player's side by a sliver (1-2px) while the player had a small downward `vy` was read by `shovePushables`' platform branch as "landed from above" and seated on top in one frame (28px); and an actor overlapping the platform's far half and pressing *away* from it was seated on the platform's OTHER side (a 43px sideways teleport), because the branch chose the side from the sign of the velocity alone. Fixes (all in that branch): a landing only counts if the overlap along gravity is about a frame's worth of fall (`|v|*0.05 + 4` px), otherwise the actor is eased out sideways (or left if that spot is a tile); moving away from the platform's centre is no longer treated as moving into it; and a push that a wall refuses this frame (`platSqueezeFrame`, `tickFrameCounter`, new transient global declared in the export template) does not turn into a snap on top, the platform just slides through. `node tests/platformfuzz.js`: 3 snaps before, 0 after (12 runs, ~32,000 frames, 0 embedded). The synthetic wall sandwich in `tests/platformrider.test.js` did not reproduce the old snap (it never lifted in that setup), so the fuzz is the evidence. 15 samples frame-identical (`gravdir.test.js`), Cloud Hopper bot and fall test still pass at 30 and 60 fps.

## Mousetrap example remade: a keep-away machine (examples/mousetrap.json; not in the Surprise pool)

`examples/mousetrap.json` was rebuilt from `tests/mousetrap.gen.js` (writes `tests/mousetrap.entry.json`, a Surprise-style entry; `mkmt`-style export = push the entry into `SURPRISE_TEMPLATES` of a temp copy, `surpriseMe(1, {templateIds, chaos:false, canonical:true, mirror:false})`, Save). One 24x18 room, gravity on, no wrap, no sticky tiles anywhere.
- **Layout.** Mousecop (lethal chaser) and the player both start on the floor of a 3-tall hall (rows 14-16) that spans the whole bottom, so Mousecop roams all of it. The hall roof (row 13) is indestructible. Two red gates (columns 9 and 16) stand open while a crate rests on a red plate at the far right of the machine; together with the walls they are the "mouse trap" (cage = columns 10-15).
- **Machine (about 20.25 s at 30/60/144 fps).** A bait crate drops on a brown plate and opens the brown gate holding the relay crate; the relay crate rides five belt legs (2 rows apart, ends staggered, 160 px/s, 5 tiles/s, no sticky tiles), falls on a bomb, and the blast smashes the crate holding the red plate, so both red gates close.
- **Play.** The player has to keep away from Mousecop the whole time (jump over him; the hall is 3 tall, jump apex ~127 px) and be the lure: run out through the cage a few seconds before the gates close, with Mousecop following, so he is inside and the player outside. An idle player is caught in about 4 s.
- **Proof.** `node tests/mousetrap.bot.js [fps] [secs]` (hook-injected temp copy, manual ticking): the machine alone closes the gates at 20.23-20.31 s; a bot that jumps over Mousecop when he gets within a gap and runs left through the cage `tau` seconds before the close ends with Mousecop caged and the player outside, 0 lives lost: 6 working parameter sets at 30 fps, 10 at 60 fps. At 144 fps the machine is the same but the bot's tau window shifts (Mousecop was outside the gate at the close with the tried values); a human timing window was not measured. The bot's first version recomputed the jump direction every frame and died; the direction must be fixed at jump start.
- **Not done:** not added to `SURPRISE_ROOMS`/the dice pool (say if wanted), no playtest by a person.

## Only the player wins at a goal tile (supersedes the Catapult Factory object-reaches-goal rule)

`checkPhysicsObjectGoalContacts` and its `stepActor` call are removed: a bomb, crate, stone or any other object touching a `goal` tile no longer wins the game (reported: bombs landing on the flag ended a level). Only `checkPlayerTileContacts` triggers the goal. The old Catapult Factory sections that describe a crate winning are history (that sample was replaced). Verified in a hooked copy on the user's `magnet mile - mini level.json` upload: a bomb placed on the flag for 20 ticks leaves `playSessionEnded` null, the player on the flag wins; all samples frame-identical to the previous build (`gravdir.test.js`, 91/0). Note: that upload actually contained the Moving Platform Maze (no magnets, cannon or brown plates), so the "box stopped cycling past the two brown plates" report could not be checked against it.

## "Warpfall" added to Surprise Me; "Everything Goes Down The Drain" rewritten as a full-room whirlpool

**Warpfall** (`warpfall`, whole-room, `ext`, `authored`, weight 2) is the user's `Warpfall - surprise me.json` (kept in `surprise-sources/`; loadable as `examples/warpfall.json`). Gravity on, wrap on both axes, no walls: twelve black holes scattered over the room, a Large Stone spawner top left, a hazard-ball spawner top right and a bomb spawner in each bottom corner. The source had no player, so the entry puts one at (10,6). It uses only stock looks, so there is no `exact` block. It has no `expect.chain` (nothing is a causal chain; `reliability`/`machines.rel.js` crash on a missing chain, which is a harness limit, not a game error). Not played by a person; the Surprise suites are the check.

**Drain** (`drain`, still `ext`, weight 2) is now a whirlpool, not a belt-and-gate machine. Gravity OFF, wrap H and V, **no walls anywhere**. One black hole at (12,9). A disc of open-air wind (`gust`) between 1.5 and 4.9 tiles from it points clockwise (each cell takes the cardinal direction nearest the tangent), so the wind plus the hole's pull make everything swing round and fall in, ever closer. Four spawners sit 6 tiles out on the four sides and throw inward: pets (`spawnerp`, swap `softchaser`), crates, bombs, pets again. Traced at 30/60/144 fps: each spawn orbits clockwise for about 1 s and is eaten (a mover slower than 240 px/s at the hole is captured); 100% of the declared chain `pull, capture` done in about 3.6 s, 140 captures in 90 s with nothing left lying around. The player starts at the top left, free to roam, and is pulled like everything else (a capture in Play restarts the room with no life lost).
**Facts found by tracing:** the wind has to stay inside about 5 tiles. Outside that the hole's pull is weaker than the wind, so wind that points off the disc parks crates in an equilibrium on the rim and they pile up (an early 7-tile disc jammed a crate column). A Large Stone is not pulled by the hole (it is exempt from every force except conveyors), so a stone spawner just fills the room: not used. Spawner launch speed (420) dies to friction in about 1.5 tiles, so the spawners must be within 6-7 tiles of the hole for the throw to reach the pull.

**Drain, round two: five drains.** The black hole's pull reach is fixed (`GRAVITY_WELL_RADIUS_TILES` 7), so one drain cannot fill the room without an engine change; instead the room holds five holes of different sizes (two big with a 4.9-tile wind disc at (6,4) and (18,13), two small with 2.8 at (19,3) and (4,14), one tiny with 2.2 at (12,9)), generated by a script: each cell takes the clockwise tangent wind of the hole it is closest to (as a fraction of that hole's disc), and one spawner per hole (pets, crates, bombs, pets, crates) sits about `disc + 1.2` tiles out facing it. The entry is now `authored` because five 7-tile pull zones cover the whole room, so the "no black hole within 7.5 tiles of the player" floor can never pass; the hero starts at the cell farthest from every hole (7.2 tiles) and a capture just restarts the room free. Traced: 20/20 at 30, 60 and 144 fps for the declared `pull, capture` chain, 175 captures in 90 s, nothing left lying around. Not played by a person.

## Cumulative wind: a stretch of open-air wind eventually beats walking, but only against it

Request: wind should be a touch stronger, so a kid can push through 1 or 2 wind tiles but not 3, and "going with the wind
is back to the old speed, always". `windAccelFor(actor, conveyorOnly, heading)` keeps the old flat push per overlapped wind tile
(1000 each) for everything: tailwind, a standing player, NPCs, objects, belts. Only when the PLAYER holds a direction against a
wind (`heading` = the screen-space walk direction, built in `stepActor` from the held keys and `rightSign()`/`cAx`; free-roam uses
all four keys) does that wind push back with `WIND_FORCE + WIND_RUN_STEP * (run - 1)`, once per direction, where `run` is
`windRunLength` = same-direction `none:wind-*` tiles lined up along the wind's axis (capped at `WIND_RUN_MAX` 6), never less than
the old sum. With `WIND_RUN_STEP` 900: 1 tile 1000, 2 tiles 1900 (walking is 2200, still wins, slower), 3 tiles 2800 (walking loses, and
the player's momentum is gone within the stretch). A first version used step 700 and ignored heading: a 3-tile run only gave net -200
so a running player's momentum carried through it, and tailwind and belts got stronger too (it broke the Over-Engineered Escape).
Belts (wind under a floor you stand on) are unchanged. Exported: `windRunLength`, `WIND_RUN_STEP/MAX`.
**Consequences:** only a player pushing against open-air wind in a stretch of 2+ tiles feels anything new; a vertical run of 2+ upward tiles
pushed against (holding Down) can exceed gravity. **The World's Worst Elevator still differs** when played with input against its wind
(first difference at frame 135 of the scripted pass) and was NOT re-solved; the other 15 samples are frame-identical (`gravdir.test.js`).
Test trace (gravity on or off, walking west into 0..4 east-wind tiles from full speed): 0/1/2 tiles walked through (10.7/11.7/12.7 tiles
travelled), 3 and 4 stop (3.7/3.0); walking east with the wind identical to before (16.9).

### The World's Worst Elevator, room 1 re-tuned for cumulative wind

Traced by best-first search over the physics (hold left/right/jump in 8-frame steps, state dedup, a flood-fill distance to the door as heuristic; scratch scripts, not committed). On the build before the against-wind rule the Lobby's exit was found in 1093 nodes (59 actions); with the rule and the user's layout it took 49,285 nodes (125 actions), because the route fights two west/east wind stretches: the `<<<` pocket at cols 10-12 rows 5-6 (pushing right) and the `>>>>` run at cols 5-8 rows 5-8 (pushing left), each 3-4 tiles. Fix (data only, room 1 board, six cells): the leftmost tile of the first stretch (10,5),(10,6) and the third tile of the second (7,5)..(7,8) were removed, so every stretch the route fights is now at most 2 tiles. Result on the new rule: found in 991 nodes (48 actions), easier than before. Other candidate fixes (removing col 11, or col 6) were slower or failed within 80k nodes. Room 2 was not touched; a scripted sweep (start x 40-240 px on the floor, jump timing 0-0.8 s, Right held for 0-10 s, 9 s at 60 fps) wins 158 combinations in both the old and the new build, identical lists (the best-first search is inconclusive for it: its route is a cannon shot that needs finer jump timing than the 8-frame steps). Not done: a full door-to-goal human-style run through both rooms in one go, and frame rates other than 60 for room 2.

## "The Wind Spiral" added to Surprise Me (`windspiral`)

From the user's `Wind spiral - surprise me.json` (kept in `surprise-sources/`; `examples/wind-spiral.json` is now this version). Whole 24x18 room, gravity off, no wrap, serialized with `surpriseSerialize("all")`; stock pieces only (air wind in four directions, solid walls, two `solid:trap` tiles, a goal flag, the player at the start), so no `exact` block and the walls take a random wall texture like any soft slot. Category `toy` (no cause-and-effect chain), `ext: true` (only for seeds >= 10000, so old seeds are unchanged), weight 2.
**Traced:** with no input the wind carries the player round the spiral and around the whole room again and again (about 15 s per lap at 30, 60 and 144 fps, 90 s without a win, no errors), so it is not `selfStart` in the counter's sense (open-air wind raises no counted event) and `needsInput` is true: to win, steer off the loop at (8,10): hold Left for 0.8 s into the `GG` pocket under the flag, then Left+Up; this wins at 30, 60 and 144 fps. 40 runs of random keys for 2 simulated minutes never won, so the flag takes deliberate steering. Suites: surprise2 900/0, scenes 37/0, surprise5 188/0.

## v2.65 Stage 2: Surprise Me curation, first half of the user's list (63 -> 40 templates)

Removed by id (the user's list, from the review seeds): chaserparade, alltramps, fanclub, pinball, moat, lemmings, sticky, bombdrop, orchestra, roulette, bombrow, dominodig, conveyor, spawnerwar, bombcarousel, upsidedown, conga, roulette2, treadmill, slippery, blackholewall, craterain, stonepets, tornado, fallforever, plus the old `plinko` (replaced). Plain deletion, so **old `#surprise=` seeds now build different things** (the weighted walk shifts); no tombstones were chosen. The second half of the user's list is still to come.

**Added (all `authored`, `ext`, whole-room, weight 2, from the user's projects via `tests/surprise.import.js`, which serializes a room of a saved project into an entry with an `exact` block):**
- `trafficjam` ("The Traffic Jam"; replaces tornado): room 1 of `Traffic_jam.json`, gravity off, wrap both ways, wind lanes with crates and nine characters; the author's own looks and lethal flags kept (Gloop stays lethal).
- `oopstramps` ("Oops All Trampolines"; replaces fallforever): **room 3 of `oops_all_trampolines.json` (the one named "surprise #10248") was assumed to be the intended room**. It is a sealed trampoline box with random gravity buttons, so it is a `toy` and `closed`; the hero was moved from inside the top wall to (1,1) because the validator refuses a player inside a solid.
- `plinko` ("Plinko"; same id): the user's `Plinko.json` (bouncy pegs, two fans, a pet spawner, their ground/Paint art).

**Fixes found on the way:** (1) an `exact.skins`/`backs` entry was namespaced even when the template had no matching `exact.grounds`/`bgs` def, so stonedomino's lava vanished and its rubble loaded as plain solid; the raw built-in id is now kept in that case (`tests/exactdefs.test.js` checks every authored room paints only ids that have defs). (2) The Ground Effect tint over a skinned tile filled the whole cell (plinko pegs sat in brown squares); in Play and exports it is now masked to the skin's own pixels (`drawEffectTint`, Build keeps the whole-cell wash).

**Popcorn Box** is now a bouncy maze (two shelves with offset gaps, posts, three rewards, one wanderer). **Engine change, flagged:** a wanderer/chaser/fleer landing on a plain `bouncy` floor in a gravity room springs back at least `NPC_BOUNCY_LAUNCH_SPEED` (560), like the player's guaranteed launch, and gets only a 100 ms steering lockout on a plain bouncy hit (it kept being locked out in a short gap). Objects and hazard balls are unchanged. All 16 samples stay frame-identical (`gravdir.test.js`, 91/0).

**Crate Carousel**: a bomb spawner above the gate (bombs hit the closed gate from 7 tiles and go off there, so crates survive), a `gust:e` at the belt end that pulls arriving crates fully into the fan column (the belt-end tile overhung the lift and jammed them under it), and a `gust:n` above the fan so a hovering crate rises to the west gust rows. Traced 120 s: no permanent jam.

## v2.65 Stage 2b: three review fixes (Wind Spiral, Crate Opens The Door, Drain)

Seeds 10000+ moved relative to the old review build because the first-half curation deleted templates (plain deletion shifts the weighted walk); the current seed-to-template table for 10000-10051 was handed to the user. Edits below are in place and do not change any pick.
- **The Wind Spiral** (`windspiral`): the trap tiles were `raw|solid:trap` (no skin) while walls got a random skin, so they gave the solution away. New piece `walltrap` (a trap that takes the room's wall skin) replaces them, the entry is `vary:{mirror:false}` (always as designed), and `drawGameplayTile` no longer tints a skinned trap tile once playing (`appMode !== "build"`), so a trap looks exactly like the wall around it in Play and exports; Build keeps the tint as an authoring cue. Only skinned traps were ever tinted, so other content is unchanged.
- **Crate Opens The Door** (`opensdoor`): four cages in waves. Pen 1 (red, as before) plus three closets in a column (blue, green, yellow; each a different enemy: pets, walker, softchaser, real chaser). Plates sit in a column at the end of the cannon's arc (x=15, rows 16 to 13): the crates pile up leaning west, so crate 1 opens pen 1, crate 2 the lowest closet, crates 3-4 the next, about crate 6 the top one. A falling crate pulses the plates it passes for 2-4 frames; that does not release anything (a gate that closes under a pet lifts it back), so no wave comes early. Closets' gate rows have open sides so released enemies walk out. Reliability 20/20 at 30/60/144 fps (first release about 4.1 s). The stack keeps growing while the spawner runs.
- **Drain** (`drain`): the five straight-in spawners are replaced by seven spawners on the wind disc rims throwing along the clockwise flow (pets, crates, bombs), so things orbit before being eaten. 20/20 at 30/60/144 fps, about 77 captures in 30 s.

## Surprise Me opens every room exactly as designed (v2.66)

After the user went through the old seeds 10000-10051, Surprise Me was changed from "a random variation of a template" to "the template, exactly as authored, every time":
- **Exact mode is the default** (`surpriseVaried = false`; only the test harness sets it true, by injecting `surpriseVaried = true;` at its anchor). `generateSurprise` then forces canonical + `chaos:false` + `mirror:false` + `scene:false`: no mirroring, no random wall skin / pet / reward / hero look (`surpriseRandomizePlayerLook` is skipped), no gravity or wrap chaos, no variation operators, no scene fillers, and the first free channel colour / door colours in order (so even the coded templates build identically for any seed). `tests/exactdeterm.test.js` builds every template at four seeds and requires identical boards/actors/defs (30/30).
- **Every press opens frozen** at its starting layout (`freezeIt` is true unless `surpriseVaried`): P or Resume starts it, Play always starts from the saved start. This extends the earlier whole-room exception to "always live" to every surprise; the log says "ready, press P to start it".
- **Colours are per cell**: `exact.colors: [[x,y,color],...]` restores the channel colour of any coloured tile (a fan, a wall...), which the old row/legend pieces dropped (Mini Level 1's blue fans had lost theirs). `template.channels` (`{"red":true,...}`) turns on the Switches the author left on (Mini Level 1: the four magnets start ON and hold their bombs). The importer (`tests/surprise.import.js`) now emits `colors` and takes `--keeppets` (keep the author's own pet defs instead of the generic harmless chaser).
- **`blindTraps`** (Wind Spiral): the trap tiles carry the same stone texture as the walls and, in this room only, are not tinted in Build either (`hiddenTintRooms`, a transient Set of room ids; Play and exports never tint a trap).
- **Fidelity test**: `node tests/exactfidelity.test.js [id...]` builds each authored room through the real path and compares tiles, colours, skins, Paint, actors (cell, kind, look), rules, camera, starting switches and the paused state with the source project (18/19 ids exact; Oops All Trampolines keeps the hero at (1,1) because the author left it inside the top wall; Warpfall has an added space backdrop and a player).
- **Cut by the user** (templates removed): rink, zeropinata, bigrink, slipgauntlet, domino, pinballroom, holesoup, minefield, magnet, allbuttons (earlier: fanclub, treadmill and the first-half list). **Replaced**: `magnetmile` is now the user's own `Magnet Mile` mini level (24 x 18, `magnet_mile_mini_level.json`), `allsprites` is "Oops All Sprites" (every square a harmless wanderer/fleer/chaser from twelve looks, the player in the centre, no gravity or wrap, the flag in the top right corner behind walls on three sides), `windspiral` loads with its original stone skin on every wall and trap, `warpfall` got a space backdrop on the Paint layer (no gameplay change), Mini Level 1 starts with its magnets on.
- **Not in Surprise Me**: the rebuilt Pinball room (`examples/pinball.json`) stays an example only.
- **Engine, perf only**: `checkActorContacts`, `shovePushables`, `bounceOffTrampolineObjects` test the box overlap before looking up defs (a 430-actor room went from 25 ms to 12 ms a tick); behavior is unchanged.
- **Scene fillers are dormant**: the only `filler:true` templates (fanclub, magnet, zeropinata...) are gone, so `tests/scenes.test.js` now has no filler runs (3 of its checks report 0/0). `tests/stage1.test.js`'s 'no key registered on the landing screen' check can fail at some viewports because the landing autopilot holds keys itself (pre-existing, not from this change); `export boot anchor present` failures in pause/overengineered/rockbottom are also old.
- Tests that need the random variation (surprise, surprise2, surprise4, surprise5, scenes, causal, reliability, machines.*, templates.probe, probe_s4, curation.*) inject `surpriseVaried = true;`. surprise2/5 lists were trimmed for the cut templates; the "templates seen in 1000 presses" bar is 20.

## Cannon Range: a prototype shooting challenge (examples/cannon-range.json, not in Surprise Me)

Built by `node tests/cannonrange.gen.js` (loads the factory project in a hooked copy and lays out one 24 x 18 room, gravity down). A row of four cannons (`cannon2-ne`, columns 5/7/9/11) stands on a table along the bottom; a crate spawner and a bomb spawner in the left wall throw their supply east onto a shelf behind a stopper wall. The hero grabs a crate (E), walks/hops along the table and drops it on the cannon of their choice (a crate landing on a cannon loads and fires it at the fixed arc). Across a lava pit, a catwalk (row 14, columns 15-22, a one-tile lip at its left end) carries three harmless wanderers pacing back and forth: they are the moving targets, and a crate coming down on one at 2+ tiles of fall squashes it (it goes home). Measured landing columns (crate, wanderers removed): cannon 5 about 14.9 (short, lava), 7 about 14.3 (hits the lip), 9 about 16.9, 11 about 19.8. Known rough edges, left for the user to edit: only two or three cannons reach the catwalk; the cannon at column 13 was dropped because the catwalk lip blocks its arc; bombs only add blast (a bomb dropped from under 6 tiles does not go off); no score or goal; no sky backdrop; the Moving Platform was tried as the target but a stationary NPC does not ride it (no gravity), so wanderers are the targets.

## Herd the Wheebuns: a prototype where the AI is the mechanic (examples/herd-the-wheebuns.json, not in Surprise Me)

Built by `node tests/herd.gen.js`. One 24 x 18 top-down room (no gravity). Five harmless fleers (the Wheebuns look; touching one pops it, so the hero must not touch them) always run straight away from the hero at 160 px/s against the hero's 220, so the hero herds them by where it stands. A funnel of walls (one row every two columns, from column 6) narrows to a gated mouth at column 17 and a walled pen (columns 18-22, rows 5-11); two fans at the funnel's wide end nudge strays in. The red Gate at the mouth starts open (red is on); the red Button sits back at the hero's start, so running back to it after the herd is in shuts the gate behind them (a trap). A column of yellow plates along the pen's east wall opens a yellow Gate on the goal's little room in the top left whenever a Wheebun is pinned against that wall.
**Facts found while building it**: a fleer pinned against a vertical wall face stays pinned until the hero stands off-axis (its flee vector then has a sliding component along the wall), which is the whole skill; two fans blowing toward each other across a column trap fleers in an oscillation, because strong wind keeps resetting their steering lockout (`displacedCooldown`); a fleer (any steering character) stepping on a Button flips it, so the Button has to be away from their route; fleers fleeing a hero at the west end end up pinned against the east wall of the pen at the hero's row, so a plate column is needed rather than one plate. A scripted bot (stand 2.6 tiles behind the straggler farthest from the mouth, on the side opposite the mouth, rotating the offset until it is not in a wall) herds all five in about 7 s, runs back, closes the gate and wins at about 12 s with no life lost. The trap is not required to win (the herd stays pinned while the hero is west); making it matter (for instance a goal that needs the hero to pass the mouth) is left to the user. The Wheebuns' touch-pop was kept harmless: popped Wheebuns are simply gone.

## Review links for retired seed numbers: `#surprise=SEED:templateid`

The hash form `#surprise=10002:rink` builds that template at that seed (forces the pick, everything else seeded as usual). It exists so seeds from before the curation can be reviewed one by one: `docs/surprise-review-10000-10051.html` lists the 50 old seeds with their old template (fanclub and treadmill, removed on purpose, have no link). The primary room and its name match the old build; scene fillers can differ because the filler pool changed. The pool itself is unchanged, so no current seed moved.

## Repo layout

`index.html` (the app), `CLAUDE.md`, `README.md`, `tests/`, `prototypes/` (sprite editor, experiments, past screenshots),
`examples/` (loadable sample projects, e.g. `wind-spiral.json`), `surprise-sources/` (the author's original `*surprise me*.json` project files, formerly in the repo root; earlier sections that say
"kept in the repo root" mean this folder; `tests/exact.test.js` reads them) and `docs/` (longer design notes, formerly in the root).

## Magnet Mile example: bombs sealed off from the crate path (data only)

`examples/magnet-mile.json`: a brown magnet at (21,10) pulls the crate off the east end of the brown plates, but the four bomb spawners shared the brown Gates' shelf with the crates, so when the Gates opened bombs and crates fell together and the blasts smashed every crate within about 15 s. Fix (no engine change): only the two spawners at (22,0) and (23,0) remain, an indestructible wall runs down column 21 (rows 6-9) so bombs stay in the 22-23 shaft, and only two crates wait above the Gates (columns 19-20) beside the starting crate. Traced 150 s at 60 fps and 100 s at 30 fps: all three crates stay alive and keep circulating, bombs pile up harmlessly in columns 22-23. Not played by a person.

## Trap tiles give way under a hard landing

A crate, Large Stone, bomb or any carried piece landing on a `trap` tile at `CRUSH_MIN_VY` (400, about a 2-tile fall) or more along gravity breaks it (`triggerTrap`) and keeps falling through the hole (`resolveAxis`, just before the across/along-gravity branches). Slower contact still rests on the tile; characters and hazard balls don't trigger it; the player's touch is unchanged. Caption now "Looks solid. Gives way when stepped on or hit hard." No engine/export additions (`triggerTrap`, `CRUSH_MIN_VY` already shipped). Checked: a crate from 1 tile rests, from 4 tiles breaks it and falls through; a stone likewise; a bomb breaks it, falls on and detonates by the 6-tile rule. All 16 samples frame-identical to the previous build (`gravdir.test.js`, 91/0), so no sample relied on the old behavior. Not done: Surprise templates were not separately swept (the Surprise suites were not re-run).

## Trap tiles crumble after a short delay (supersedes the instant player pass-through)

A trap is now solid to the player too. The first touch by the player or by a crate, Large Stone, bomb or other carried piece starts a `TRAP_CRUMBLE_MS` (350 ms, game clock, `trapTimers`) countdown (creak sound, tile shakes 1 px); `tickTraps()` then calls `triggerTrap`. A kid can stand on it and jump away in time; a hard landing (`CRUSH_MIN_VY`, about 2 tiles) still breaks it at once and the object falls through. Characters (wanderer/chaser...) are still held up by it and don't start the timer; a non-blocking `none:trap` still crumbles on plain overlap. `trapTimers` is transient (cleared with the other runtime state, declared in the export template); `tickTraps` is in `runtimeFunctions`. Caption: "Looks solid. Crumbles a moment after you step on it." Checked: a player standing on a trap held 21 frames then fell; 16 samples frame-identical (`gravdir.test.js` 91/0); `surprise5` 183/0. Earlier "player passes straight through" notes are history. Not tried: an exported game with a trap, and Surprise Me templates were not separately swept for traps.

## Magnet Mile example: crates no longer wedge in the cannon cell, bombs now dig out the flag (data only)

Two bugs in `examples/magnet-mile.json` (reported after the bomb-shaft change), both fixed in the data, no engine change:
- **Crates stuck at the bottom.** The red magnet at (20,17) sits diagonally under the bottom cannon's cell, so a crate loaded into the cannon (or just fired) was within the magnet's 8px hold reach and frozen whenever red was on (red is on while any crate rests on a red plate), never leaving. Fix: the magnet at (20,17) is now **pink** and the two plates before the cannon, (17,16) and (18,16), are pink too, so it pulls only while a crate is on those plates and lets go once the crate is in the cannon. Also removed the second crate of the pair that dropped beside the cannon (column 20 dead end): one crate over the brown gate, one on the bottom row. Traced 240 s at 30, 60 and 144 fps: both crates keep cycling (a crate sits in the cannon cell only briefly, 2 to 9 of 48 five-second samples).
- **Bombs didn't open the flag.** The brown magnet at (21,10) touches the bomb shaft (column 22), so bombs stuck to it and never fell far enough to detonate. Fix: the shaft is now column 23 only (one spawner at (23,0), no gate under it, indestructible wall at (22,0..10) between it and the magnet), so each bomb falls 9+ tiles, detonates and clears the block under it and its left neighbour (22,y). The flag's encasing tiles are gone by about 15 to 20 s. Player access to the flag is through that shaft; not played by a person.

## "Stone Cold Domino" replaced by the user's remade level (Surprise Me, `stonedomino`)

The `stonedomino` whole-room entry (still `ext`, category `room`, weight 2, same four names) is now the user's remade level (`Stone_Cold_Domino_-_mini_level.json`, also `examples/stone-domino.json`), serialized with `surpriseSerialize("all")` and marked `authored` with an `exact` block (their lava and rubble skins, their Princess hero look; walls stay plain). New compared with the old coded version: a second stone spawner and a bomb spawner (`spawnerb:e`) feeding the shaft, a lava floor, a trap-tile staircase (`raw|solid:trap` + rubble), two gravity Buttons (`gbutton:left`, `gbutton:down`), a goal flag top left and the player in a pocket bottom right. **There is no pet/stooge any more**, so the old "squash" payoff is gone. `expect.chain` is now `spawn, beltCarry, buttonPress, gateOpen, fall, impact, detonate, blastClear` and `expect.check` is `impactBomb`; discover is the goal (`F`). Payload swap is still the belt crate (`crate`/`pet`).
Checked: chain completes 100/100 at 30, 60 and 144 fps (about 5.3 / 4.0 / 3.9 s); activity OK; forced payload+mirror causal run 40/40 honest, 0 errors; `surprise5` 183/0. Stone spawners keep producing, so stones pile up in the shaft and the lava row over time (no cap besides `SPAWNER_MAX_LIVE`). The player route to the flag (gravity-left button, trap staircase) was NOT traced or played. Not re-run: surprise2, scenes, exact, seedstable suites.

## Mousetrap example replaced by the user's magnet-and-button version; cannons re-placed so the crate reaches the bomb (data only)

`examples/mousetrap.json` is now the user's `Mousetrap - surprise me.json` (the coded `tests/mousetrap.gen.js` version and `tests/mousetrap.bot.js` describe the old layout and are history). Four colored magnets (cyan, yellow, pink, purple) hold the relay crate at the end of each belt leg while their channel is on; the file's saved `switchChannels` has all four ON on purpose. The four colored Switches on row 12 release them: **the player presses them by jumping from the hall floor** (verified: a floor jump reaches row 12 at columns 2, 7, 13 and 18), so the crate waits at each magnet until the player lets it go, while Mousecop chases. Do not "fix" the stall at the first magnet by clearing `switchChannels`.

**Cannons.** The crate falls off the end of the purple belt onto the first cannon and has to hop cannon to cannon to the bomb at (21,12). A shot is a fixed arc but the belt row 8 is a ceiling over columns 4-21, so a shot from rows 10-12 is bonked down after about 1-3 tiles of rise and each hop is only about 4 tiles. The old cannons at (12,11) and (17,11) were half a tile out of reach (the crate fell past into the cage). Now: (3,10) and (7,11) unchanged, **(12,11) -> (10,10)** and **(17,11) -> (15,12)**. Traced in the real engine: hop 1 lands on (7,11); hop 2 lands on (10,10); hop 3 lands on (15,12); hop 4 hits the bomb, which clears the crate on the red plate, so the red Gates close. Timeline with the player releasing each magnet as soon as the crate is held: presses at about 4.5 / 9.0 / 13.5 / 18.0 s, bomb at about 22.9 s (60 fps; 22.7 s at 144 fps).

**Facts found (reusable):** a crate landing on a cannon top while its own cooldown (800 ms after firing) is running keeps sliding (it keeps its sideways speed after a ballistic flight) and only loads once the cooldown ends, so it must come to rest still touching that cannon: about 0.7 tile of slide at 60 fps and about 1.1 at 30 fps. That makes hop landings fragile. A search of every 3 and 4-cannon layout in rows 9-12 (about 2000 simulated runs, with the drop point varied) found none that works at 30, 60 and 144 fps together.

**Known limit: works at 60 and 144 fps, NOT at 30 fps.** At 30 fps the crate slides off (10,10) (and the last shot also lands past the bomb), so the bomb never goes off. A sticky ground effect on the cannons fixes the (10,10) slide but not the last shot. If it matters, the next lever is a wider landing pad (an extra cannon or a stopper wall) or accepting the limit as the Over-Engineered Escape's booth does.

Test harness (scratchpad scripts, not committed): remove the chaser in place AFTER `enterPlayMode()` (editing `room.actors` first is undone by `activateRoom`, and a live chaser kills a parked player at about 4 s and the level restart deletes any test crate); release a magnet by setting `switchChannels[color] = false` once the relay crate is held. Not played by a person.

## v2.65 Stage 1: Surprise Me curation review (report only; nothing deleted)

Evidence scripts, not shipped: `tests/curation.evidence.js` (per template and fps 30/60/144: box share, movers, hero alive, first interaction, honest/strong/freed chain, 10 s no input) and `tests/curation.poke.js` (remove one piece, does the outcome change). Telemetry has no per-template counts (only the generic `surprise_me_used`, and only with `?study=1`). Harness caveat: the `MECH` map in the evidence/reliability scripts lacks beltCarry/pull/handoff, so drain and magnetmile show honest 0 although `machines.rel.js` proves their declared chains 100/100. The proposed deletion list awaits the user's approval; `index.html` is unchanged. Plain deletion changes ~86% of old `#surprise=` seeds (the weighted walk shifts), a tombstone (retired ids stay in the walk and resolve to a fallback) would change only the retired templates' ~23% share.

## Chasers, fleers and wanderers: spread out, bend, escape corners

`steeringAccelFor` gained three fixed rules (no new piece, no scripting). (1) **Separation**: `steerSeparation` adds a soft push (`STEER_SEPARATION_PX` 30, `_ACCEL` 12000) away from any close non-player character, for wanderers, chasers and fleers, so a herd no longer stacks. (2) **Wobble**: chasers and fleers bend their heading by up to `STEER_WOBBLE_RAD` (0.5 rad) with two slow sines seeded from the actor id and the game clock (deterministic, pause-safe, no `Math.random`). (3) **Corner escape (fleers only)**: when the step ahead is blocked and the hero is within 9 tiles, scan 8 directions (not toward the hero), score open distance (up to 5 tiles) plus 0.6 x "away", and commit to the best for 800 ms (then 300 ms cooldown); state in `steerState`. Export: constants line, `steerSeparation` in `runtimeFunctions`, `steerState` declared in the template. Measured with `tests/fleerspread.test.js` on Herd the Wheebuns (hero chasing the nearest fleer for 25 s): frames with two fleers overlapping 1430 -> 196 of 1500. Chasers behind walls are still not path-finding. Samples with chasers/fleers now play slightly differently (not re-solved); Cloud Hopper bot and fall test, stage1, enemypanel, landing, exact determinism still pass.

## Examples: Rampart (tower defense) and Cannon Range Space (prototypes, not in Surprise Me)

`examples/rampart.json` (`node tests/rampart.gen.js`; `rampart.measure.js` prints each cannon's landing, `rampart.sim.js` fires volleys in Play) is the cannon range rebuilt as a Rampart-style fortress: a descending firing stair (cannons at rows 5/8/11), crate and bomb spawners the hero can reach, and a one-tile-thick fortress (curtain wall, yard, keep, back tower, banners/torches/slits/flags) over a lava moat. **Enemies only die in lava in Play** (Build sends them home). Measured: 14 enemies -> 7 after 10 volleys; the rest (tower, deep chambers) need hand-thrown bombs. Cannons must be 2+ cells apart and have no solid on their NE side or the shot is clipped. Bombs from low cannons land with <6 tiles of fall and do not detonate; use crates there.
`examples/cannon-range-space.json` (`tests/cannonrange-space.gen.js`) is the no-gravity version: free-roam hero, spawners in reach, a 2-wide lava river, wanderers on the far side. **Rough**: with gravity off a cannon shot only travels ~1.4 tiles (friction ends it at once) and a thrown object about as far, so only targets near the bank can be hit; bombs only explode by fuse. Not played by a person; tune the gap and targets.

## Bomb fuse: reset on pickup and cannon, lit on landing; "stays gone" for characters

**Fuse.** Picking a bomb up resets its fuse to a full `BOMB_FUSE_MS` (it was paused with whatever was left). A bomb loaded into a cannon that was already lit (fuse ticking or waiting) gets a full fuse that does NOT tick during load and flight (`fuseWaitLand`, `fuseWaitSince`); a thrown bomb waits the same way (it used to start ticking at release). The fuse is lit when the bomb has landed (`stepActor`: not carried, not loaded, not `ballistic`, >150 ms since the wait began, grounded in a gravity room or slower than 40 px/s in free-roam). Dropping at the feet still lights at once; a player shove still lights an unlit bomb; the >6-tile fall rule still detonates on landing. A never-lit bomb loaded into a cannon stays unlit (spawner/belt bombs, the Over-Engineered Escape: that sample stays frame-identical). Reason: lit by the drop, a cannon bomb used to explode mid-flight (300 ms load + 1.3-1.5 s flight against a 1200 ms fuse). Test: `node tests/fusegone.test.js`.

**Stays gone.** `def.staysGone` (Characters tab, "If it dies": comes back / stays gone, shown with the lethal row for every NPC character). No field = comes back, exactly as before (every older save, sample and Surprise room unchanged; all 16 samples frame-identical). With it set, lava/other lethal ground, a black hole and a crate/stone squash remove the character for good in Build and Play instead of sending it home (`npcStaysGone`, `logBuildKill`). In Build, "Reset to start" brings a stayed-gone character back (it uses the Build blast log). The Rampart and Cannon Range Space examples set it on their wanderer def (`tests/rampart.gen.js`, `tests/cannonrange-space.gen.js`); Rampart sim now 14 -> 6 enemies after 10 volleys. The earlier "enemies respawn" notes for those two examples are superseded. Export: `npcStaysGone`, `logBuildKill` in `runtimeFunctions`.

## Bomb blasts kill characters; "If it dies" shares the lethal/harmless row

`detonateBombAt` now treats a character (wanderer/chaser/fleer/statue, never the player) inside the blast like lethal ground: `triggerNpcLethalReset(actor, true)` (sent home in Build; in Play removed until the level restarts; always removed if "stays gone"). It used to only knock them back. Objects are unchanged (crates still smashed, bombs chain). Audit of what this changes: no built-in sample has a bomb and a character in the same room (Ice Cave's bombs and yeti are in different rooms), so samples are unaffected; Surprise Me rooms that now see it: Monster Escape, Chaos One, Stay Alive, Mini Level 1, Magnet Mile (bomb spawners), Carousel; examples: Mousetrap (Mousecop next to the bomb), Rampart, Cannon Range (+Space), Magnet Mile. In the Characters tab the "If it dies" swatches (comes back / stays gone) now sit in the same row as lethal / harmless. (`tests/gravdir.test.js` currently fails ~10 samples at frame 0/75 even HEAD-vs-HEAD, so it was not usable as the guard this time.)

Update: a blast now sends a "comes back" character home (it respawns) in Play as well as Build, and only "stays gone" characters are removed (it used to remove them in Play until the level restart). So Chaos One's endless chaos keeps its enemies. Mini Level 1 (Surprise Me `minilevel1`: magnets releasing bombs, the skeleton wanderers behind the wall over lava, the brown gates, the flag) now has `staysGone: true` on its skeleton def, so killing them is permanent and opening the way to the flag is the goal.

## Pinball gets random balls and joins Surprise Me; Moving Platform Maze joins too (`pinball`, `platmaze`)

**Why the balls all took the same path:** the spawner launches identically and a cannon shot is a fixed arc, so every ball was the same trajectory (traced: 59 of 60 balls loaded cannon (17,1) then (18,10), never the left two). **Fix, opt-in per def so nothing else changes:** an actor def with `launchJitter: true` (the example's own "pinball" ball, `pbBall`, a hazard-ball temperament with silver art) gets a random spawn (`SPAWN_JITTER_*`: +-30% speed, a sideways kick up to 60%, +-5 px across the cell, in `spawnActorAt`) and a random cannon shot (`CANNON_JITTER_*`: +-12% speed, +-0.08 rad, in `fireLoadedCannon`). Default hazard balls, crates, pets etc. are untouched (`gravdir.test.js` vs the previous build: 91/0, every sample frame-identical). Measured, 300 balls: share of balls that reach each cannon (17,1) 60%, (18,10) 60%, (4,10) 32%, (6,2) 25%; first cannon split 60/40 (was 100/0 and the left cannons 0%). Exported games ship the constants. Harness: scratchpad `pb.js` (not committed).
**Surprise Me:** `pinball` (toy, `ext`, `authored`, weight 2, `exact` block, new piece `spawnerj:<dir>` = a spawner loaded with the entry's own `pbBall`) and `platmaze` (toy, `closed`, `ext`, `authored`, weight 2; red channel starts on; new piece `key:<color>` = a Key of that color, so the yellow key opens the yellow Locked Door; vertical platform via `platformy`). Sources saved in `surprise-sources/Pinball.json`, `Moving_Platform_Maze.json`. `exactfidelity` differs for platmaze only by its bomb (an actor in the source, a tile in the entry; becomes an actor on the first tick). Neither was played by a person; the maze was not re-solved. Inventory: 39 designs (toy 19).
